# Source survey, 2026-11-02/03

Twenty-eight candidate inspection sources probed live from a cloud container (not from a GitHub runner) by parallel agents, one per source, before the November 2026 expansion. Each entry records what the probe actually observed; anything it could not confirm is marked unverified in the text. Raw captures stayed outside the repository. Feasibility is the probe's own call: high = a fetcher is a straightforward build, medium = buildable with a notable cost or policy decision, low = thin or fragile, blocked = no machine-readable path.

## Georgia DPH statewide food-service inspections — ga.healthin

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: Georgia statewide minus Gwinnett, Newton and Rockdale (GNR Health, already covered via MHD 'gwinnett'): 156 of 159 counties, ≈9.8 M people (Georgia ≈11.0 M minus ~1.0 M Gwinnett, ~0.12 M Newton, ~0.1 M Rockdale; 2024 estimates, approximate/unverified). Includes Atlanta proper and its core counties Fulton (~1.07 M), DeKalb (~0.76 M), Cobb (~0.78 M), plus Savannah/Chatham, Augusta/Richmond, Columbus/Muscogee, Macon/Bibb, Athens/Clarke, Alpharetta/Johns Creek, Valdosta, Warner Robins (all seen in t ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. The statewide search returns 0 rows for county=GWINNETT, NEWTON and ROCKDALE (verified), which are exactly the counties DineScores already fetches through MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS['gwinnett'] (GNR Health). No other Georgia coverage exists (the Tennessee buckets hold TN cities only). Minor note: 'BUFORD' appears in both sources — Hall County's side of Buford is on the state portal, Gwinnett's side on MHD — different facilities, no id collision expected.
- **Base URL**: https://ga.healthinspections.us/stateofgeorgia/API/index.cfm/  (portal page https://ga.healthinspections.us/stateofgeorgia/; lower-case 'api' also works; /publicportals/api/... -> 404; https://ga.healthinspections.us/ -> 302 tylertech.com; /georgia/ -> 302 dph.georgia.gov/environmental-health-inspections)
- **Auth**: None. No login, no token, no cookie/CFID session needed (every call made cookieless), recaptchaActive "no" in clientData. Plain GET with a Chrome UA + Accept: application/json; served by Taffy (ColdFusion REST, header 'allow: GET'). All ~720 API requests from this container answered HTTP 200.
- **Date filter**: Server-side, VERIFIED: search json keys "from"/"to" (filter id 5, 'Last Inspection Date: ', type date) filter on the FACILITY'S LAST inspection date, inclusive on both ends. Formats 'YYYY-MM-DD' and 'MM/DD/YYYY' both accepted (Cobb 10-30: 11 rows either way; Fulton page 0 identical). from=to=2026-10-30 Fulton -> 37 rows all dated 10-30-2026; Cobb 2026-10-27..2026-10-28 -> 15 = 11 (27th) + 4 (28th); Cobb 2026-10-27..2026-10-30 -> 41; 'from' with no 'to' is open-ended to today (Cobb from=2026-10-27 -> 41, same as ..10-30). Values are base64-encoded in the JSON like every other filter value. Caveat: it is a facility-level filter — a facility inspected twice in the window appears once (under its newest date); its earlier inspection comes from inspectionsData. 'to' without 'from': untested. sco ...
- **Pagination**: search/{json}/{page} and facilities/{page}: {page} starts at 0, FIXED 5 facilities per page (the SPA's infinite scroll), an empty JSON list [] marks the end (verified: Fulton 2026-10-30 -> 8 pages/37 rows then []; statewide 7-day -> 242 pages/1,208 rows then []). Order is last-inspection-date DESC, then facility id ASC (checked on 1,208 statewide rows and 37 Fulton rows); distinct ids == rows on every paged run (stable). inspectionsData/{id} is unpaged (all of a facility's inspections, newest first, 1-8 seen).
- **score**: OFFICIAL 0-100, higher is better (score_scale '100'): inspectionsData columns['2'] 'Score: NN' numeric on 203/203 inspections (75 were 100, min seen 88 in samples); facility columns['4'] 'Last Inspection Score: NN'. Deductions per violation in 'Points: 9|4|3|2|1'
- **grade**: Not in the JSON; the printable prints CURRENT GRADE with A=90-100, B=80-89, C=70-79, U<=69 — identical cutoffs to DineScores' A/B/C/F, so derive from the score
- **violations**: inspectionsData 'violations' dict keyed '0'..'n' (empty {} when clean), each a 6-string list: [0] '6-1B - proper hot holding temperatures' (form item + title), [1] rule citation '511-6-1.04(6)(f) - time/temperature control for safety; hot holding (p)', [2] 'Points: 9', [3] 'Corrected during inspection?: Yes|No', [4] 'Repeat: Yes|No', [5] 'Inspector Notes: <observation text, same as the printable>'
- **severity**: citation suffix in list[1]: (p) priority, (pf) priority foundation, (c) core, sometimes combined '(p, c)', '(pf, c)', '(p, pf, c)' (take the highest); over 340 lines: single c 157, pf 71, p 37, combined ~53, null citation 11, one '©' typo — fall back to list[2] Points (9 -> priority, 4 -> priority_foundation, 3/2/1 -> core; items 1-9 are FDA risk factors/interventions, 10-18 good retail practices)
- **coordinates**: NONE (showMapbox false; the SPA would geocode mapAddress via Mapbox). Must geocode: geocode_missing_coords (Census batch + Nominatim fallback cap 150) in dinescores_pipeline.py
- **address**: 'mapAddress' == columns['0'] = 'STREET \r\nCITY, GA ZIP[-4]' (e.g. '2651 COBB PKWY SE  \r\nATLANTA, GA 30339-3111'); regex \n\s*(.+?),\s*GA\s*(\d{5}) parsed 689/689 statewide rows; city/zip from the second line; county only known from the search filter (none in the record); phone in columns['1']; county EH phone in columns['6']
- **inspection_date**: inspectionsData columns['0'] 'Date: MM-DD-YYYY'; facility list columns['5'] 'Last Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY' (matched the newest inspectionsData record on 47/47 facilities)
- **facility_id**: search record 'id' = base64 of a decimal facility id (OTQ4OTgwNw== -> 9489807); stable across search and inspectionsData (facilityId). Permit number also in columns['3'] ('Permit Number: FSP-060-007884'; formats vary by county: 'FSP-###-####' 559/689, '#-#####', 'FS###-######', ... — do not derive county from it)
- **inspection_id**: inspectionsData 'inspectionId' (int, e.g. 57560333; == fsimID of the printable); 203/203 distinct across 47 facilities
- **inspection_type**: inspectionsData columns['1'] 'Inspection Purpose: Routine|Followup|Initial|Temporary|Issued Provisional Permit' (seen: Routine 175, Initial 19, Followup 9 of 203; Temporary on festival stands); program = permitType 'Food Service' (search key permitType; row columns['2'] 'Permit Type: Food Service'); other permitType values 'Tourist Accommodation', 'Swimming Pool'
- **Weekly volume**: ~1,200 facility-inspections/week statewide (Food Service only, GNR excluded): the live 7-day window 2026-10-26..2026-11-02 returned 1,208 facilities (Mon 245, Tue 256, Wed 267, Thu 260, Fri 166, Sat 14, Sun/today 0); single days 10-27 -> 256, 10-28 -> 267, 10-30 -> 166. Atlanta core: Fulton 37 on Fri 10-30 (incl. ~14 festival stands Sat 10-31), Cobb 41 for 10-27..10-30, DeKalb 11 on 10-30. True inspection count is slightly higher (a facility inspected twice in a week is listed once).
- **Weekly requests**: ~1,450 GETs: ~250 search pages (5 rows/page; per-day windows add one empty terminator page per day) + 1 inspectionsData per listed facility (~1,200). Measured pace ~0.45 s/request + chosen delay; the statewide 7-day paging took 2m48s for 243 requests single-threaded, so a full weekly ≈ 15-20 min at 1 worker (~8-10 min with 2 inspectionsData workers). Plus Census batch geocoding for facilities not yet in D1 (up to ~1,200 addresses the first weeks, far fewer later).
- **90-day backfill**: ~18,500 GETs: ~13 weeks x ~1,200 ≈ 15,000-16,000 facilities with a last inspection in the window -> ~3,100-3,200 search pages (per-day or one open-ended from=today-90 window) + ~15,000 inspectionsData calls (~2.5-3 h at 1 worker, ~1.5 h at 2). Each inspectionsData returns the facility's whole ~3-year history (earliest seen 2023-11-06), so the 90-day backfill also yields the prior 2-3 years for every facility it touches; ~15,000 addresses to geocode (Census batch). Whole-portal backfill (from=2023-11-01, every facility ever listed): size unverified, likely 40-50k facilities.
- **History**: Earliest inspection seen 2023-11-06 across 47 facilities / 203 inspections (1-8 per facility; e.g. Cobb McDonald's 2024-07-31, 2025-11-06, 2026-10-30) — looks like a rolling ~3-year window; exact retention unverified. Every inspection carries score, purpose, inspector, violations and a printable link.
- **Recommended strategy**: New fetcher fetch_georgia (API/CSV-class group, 400-day lookback cap) using requests + the existing _tyler_request backoff (429/5xx) and TYLER_DETAIL_WORKERS/TYLER_DETAIL_DELAY pacing: (1) window start = _lookback_since('Georgia DPH', ...), i.e. the day before the last SHIPPED inspection; iterate SINGLE-DAY windows oldest-first: GET search/{"permitType":b64("Food Service"),"from":b64(day),"to":b64(day),"keyword":""}/{page} (statewide, no county filter) for page 0,1,2,... until the server returns [] — the empty page is the completeness signal; every page must be 200 after retries or the day is NOT shipped and is held with _hold_window_start('Georgia DPH', day, counted=False). Also assert the invariant (date DESC, facility id ASC) across a day's pages and that distinct ids == rows; a violation means the list shifted under the run — re-read that day. Running on the Sunday cron, the Mon-Sat day lists are frozen, so shifts are unlikely. (2) For every facility id listed (union over the days): GET inspectionsData/{id}; ship the inspections dated >= window start (the first time a facility is seen, optionally its whole history — it comes free); a failed inspectionsData drops that facility's in-window inspections and calls _hold_freshness('Georgia DPH', date, key=facility_id) so the give-up count applies; a deadline cut reports _set_group_status('Georgia DPH', 'skipped'/'blocked') and holds the unread days. Cross-check: the facility's 'Last Inspection Score/Date' must equal the newest inspectionsData record (47/47 in the probe) — otherwise drop and hold that facility. (3) Map: name (title-case where all-caps), street = mapAddress line 1, city/zip from line 2, state 'GA', original_score = risk_score = Score (official 0-100, A/B/C/U cutoffs equal DineScores'), inspection_type = Purpose, violations = [classify_violation(item + notes), severity from the citation's (p)/(pf)/(c) (highest of a combo; null -> Points 9 priority, 4 priority_foundation, else core), text = item title + Inspector Notes], source_id = f'georgia_{decimal_facility_id}_{inspectionId}', source_url = the printablePath or the portal page. (4) Geocode via geocode_missing_coords (Census batch; Nominatim cap 150) and rely on the COALESCE merges to keep coordinates. (5) _record_group('Georgia DPH', records); _note_source_lag if the newest date is >21 days old. Weekly cost ≈ 250 search pages + ~1,200 inspectionsData; never send the decimal facility id (500) and never rely on the score filter.
- **Risks**:
  - Date filter is on the facility's LAST inspection date, not per inspection: the listing never enumerates inspections, so every listed facility needs one inspectionsData call (~1,200/week), and a facility inspected twice in the window shows once (its earlier visit comes from inspectionsData)
  - Page size is fixed at 5 and the list is live-sorted date DESC: an inspection entered mid-run shifts page boundaries (duplicate or skipped row). Mitigate with single-day windows, the Sunday cron, dedupe by id and the ordering invariant check; re-read a day that paged out of order
  - No coordinates at all — geocoding dependency (Census batch first, Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run); ~1,200 new addresses the first week and ~15k for a 90-day backfill; mapAddress parsed 689/689 but festival/temporary stands share one address (e.g. 6905 VIRLYN B. SMITH, Fairburn) and will collapse into one restaurant id
  - Tyler ColdFusion portal: the pipeline's other Tyler portals intermittently 503 and rate-limit detail pages; none seen here in ~720 requests at ~1.4 req/s, but keep _tyler_request backoff and gentle pacing; reachability from GitHub-hosted runners is unverified (only this container was tested; Houston/San Antonio/DC Tyler portals are fetched from runners in production)
  - 11 of 340 violation citations were null and one ended in '©' instead of '(c)': severity must fall back to the Points value; combined designations '(p, c)', '(pf, c)', '(p, pf, c)' need a highest-wins rule
  - History appears to be a rolling ~3-year window and retention is unverified; score filter (scoreFrom/scoreTo) did not apply in the probe and 'to' without 'from' was not tested
  - No terms of use or robots.txt found (robots.txt -> 404); DPH data-use policy unverified
  - Facility names are all-caps in some counties (Cobb, DeKalb) and mixed case in others (Fulton): normalise before make_restaurant_id so re-inspections keep their ids; permit-number formats vary by county so county cannot be derived from them (county is known only when searching per county)
  - Temporary/Initial/Followup purposes are scored like Routine; 'Issued Provisional Permit' inspections exist on the form (not seen in samples) — decide whether to ship non-Routine purposes
  - Statewide volume (~250 facilities/weekday) is well under any per-request cap, but a whole-portal backfill (from 2023-11) is tens of thousands of inspectionsData calls — stage it like the MHD backfills
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://ga.healthinspections.us/stateofgeorgia/` (none) Landing page of the Tyler publicportals Backbone SPA (require.js data-main ../publicportals/main); confirms host and app layout; JS modules saved under js/, templates under templates/ [200 (2,744 B); /stateofgeorgia/search.cfm and index.cfm -> 404 (not the older Tyler layout)]
  - `GET https://ga.healthinspections.us/stateofgeorgia/API/index.cfm/clientData` (none) Portal config: name 'We Protect Lives' / 'Environmental Health Inspection Search Portal', recaptchaActive 'no', showMapbox false (no coordinates anywhere), advSearchToggle true [200 (621 B)]
  - `GET https://ga.healthinspections.us/stateofgeorgia/API/index.cfm/filters` (none) Filter definitions: city dropdown (566 values), county dropdown (159 values incl. GWINNETT/NEWTON/ROCKDALE which return 0 rows), score range (scoreFrom/scoreTo), permitType dropdown ['Food Service','Tourist Accommodation','Swimming Pool'], permitnum text, 'Last Inspection Date' from/to [200 (8,586 B)]
  - `GET https://ga.healthinspections.us/stateofgeorgia/API/index.cfm/search/{json}/{page}` ({json} = {"county":"<b64>","permitType":"<b64>","from":"<b64>","to":"<b64>","keyword":"<b64 or empty>"} — optional keys city, scoreFrom, scoreTo, permitnum; EVERY value base64-encoded, then '/' in the ...) Paged facility list filtered by county/permit type/last-inspection-date window; record = {id (base64 of decimal facility id), name, mapAddress 'STREET \r\nCITY, GA ZIP', columns {0: address, 1: 'Phone Number: ..', 2: 'Permit Type: Food Service', 3: 'Permit Number: FSP-060-007884', 4: 'Last Inspectio ... [200 on all ~560 search pages fetched (statewide 7-day: 243 requests, 2m48s, no non-200)]

[200 on 70+ calls; 500 for the non-base64 id]

## Louisiana statewide — LDH Retail Food inspections, Tyler Tec

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True; blocked: Not blocked. ~270 requests from this container over ~25 minutes, every well-formed request answered 200 (a 30-request burst with no delay: 30x200, avg 0.80 s, max 5.58 s). The only non-200s were my own malformed calls (raw numeric facility id -> 500; filters without the keyword key -> 500; unencoded braces -> 400). No captcha, no login, no robots.txt (404), no terms-of-use text on the portal. Reachability from GitHub-hosted runners is unverified from here (precedent: the Houston/San Antonio/DC healthinspections.us portals are fetched directly from runners); the site relay's ALLOWED_HOSTS (func ...
- **Population**: Statewide Louisiana, all 64 parishes (parish filter lists all 64): ~4.6 million residents (citypopulation.de 2025 estimate 4,618,189; 2020 Census 4,657,757). Includes New Orleans (Orleans Parish ~362k residents, ~3,057 listed facilities; Jefferson Parish ~2,760 facilities), Baton Rouge (East Baton Rouge ~456k residents, ~2,640 facilities), Shreveport (Caddo ~224k, ~1,438 facilities), Lafayette (~254k, ~1,676 facilities), plus St. Tammany ~1,442, Calcasieu/Lake Charles ~1,390, Ouachita/Monroe ~98 ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. CLAUDE.md lists no Louisiana metro or state, and the pipeline has no Louisiana fetcher (grep for louisiana/new orleans/baton rouge hits only CLAUDE.md's candidate list). The only existing touchpoint is the same vendor platform: houston-tx, sanantonio-tx and dc.healthinspections.us are already scraped with _tyler_request (pipeline line 1645) — a different (HTML search.cfm) product, but the same host family and backoff behaviour.
- **Base URL**: https://la.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/  (ColdFusion JSON API behind the Backbone app; printables at https://la.healthinspections.us/paper/_paper_inspection_form.cfm). The /stateoflouisiana/ path that router.js and the GA snippet expect now 404s; the app and API live at the host root.
- **Auth**: None. No API key, cookie, session, CSRF or Referer/UA requirement: 200 with no User-Agent, with 'python-requests/2.32.3', with curl's default UA, and with a Chrome UA. (POST /API/index.cfm/security and /user exist only for the optional bookmark accounts; GET on them is 404.)
- **Date filter**: Server-side, VERIFIED, on the facility's LAST inspection date (not on individual inspections): `GET /API/index.cfm/search/{urlencoded JSON}/{page}` with JSON `{"keyword":"","from":"<base64 date>","to":"<base64 date>"}`. Keys are the filter input class names (`from`/`to`, views.js lines 392-395), values base64-encoded, the JSON itself percent-encoded in the path (the portal also replaces '/' with %2F, views.js line 96). Both `YYYY-MM-DD` and `MM/DD/YYYY` are accepted and return identical rows. BOTH BOUNDS ARE STRICTLY EXCLUSIVE: (2026-10-18, 2026-10-20) -> only 10-19 rows; (10-19, 10-20) -> []; (10-18, 10-19) -> []; (10-22, 10-24) -> only 10-23; (10-23, 10-24) -> []. So an inclusive window [A,B] is from=A-1day, to=B+1day. `from` alone works. The `keyword` key MUST be present (even empty) or ...
- **Pagination**: Path page index, 0-based, FIXED 5 rows per page on both `facilities/{page}` and `search/{json}/{page}` (helpers.js getJsonData line 31-70; views.js navigateFwd lines 239-259 increments pageVal by 1 per scroll). Past the end the server returns `[]` with HTTP 200 (`facilities/999999` -> `[]`); the last page may hold 1-4 rows (page 5717 had 2). Lists are sorted by the facility's LAST inspection date DESC, then numeric facility id ASC (verified pages 0,1,5,20,50,100,300,1000,2000-2029,3375,4562,5156,5453,5601,5675,5712,5716,5717). No total-count field; a binary search for the last non-empty page c ...
- **score**: NONE. LDH publishes no numeric score (`original_score` must be None); risk_score has to be computed from Critical / Non-Critical violation counts (Omaha-style policy decision; nothing in the source states a tier or points).
- **grade**: NONE. No letter grade or pass/fail anywhere (the printable is a 'Notice of Violations'). Grade must come from calculate_vetted_grade on the computed risk_score.
- **violations**: inspectionsData `violations` = object keyed '0'..'n', each value a 3-element list [severity label, '<item#> - <item title>' (e.g. '75 - non-food contact surfaces clean'), tag]; a CLEAN inspection is exactly {'0': ['None']} (observed inspection 151551920) — explicit, so a failed fetch is never mistaken for clean. Item titles only; the inspector's narrative ('Fryer has an accumulation of grease; microwave') plus LAC Title 51 code reference (e.g. '75 - 2501.2 - ...') is only in the printable HTML ' ...
- **severity**: Element 0 of each violation list: 'Critical Items' or 'Non-Critical Items' (LDH's two statutory categories; the printable groups them under 'CRITICAL ITEMS: These items MUST BE CORRECTED IMMEDIATELY' and 'NON-CRITICAL ITEMS'). Element 2 is the status tag: ' - Repeat', ' - Corrected' or null (printable: '[Repeat]', '[Corrected]'). Sampled mix: 6 critical / 24 non-critical entries in the two saved files.
- **coordinates**: NONE. No lat/lng in any response; the portal geocodes `mapAddress` client-side through Mapbox at render time (helpers.js lines 127-200, its own token). The fetcher must geocode: addresses are clean and structured ('4215 GOVERNMENT ST \r\nBATON ROUGE, LA 70806 '), well suited to geocode_census_batch (pipeline line 7661) with the Nominatim fallback (geocode_missing_coords, line 7723). ~28.6k facilities to geocode once; coalesce lat/lng on merge.
- **address**: facilities/search `mapAddress` (= `columns['0']`): 'STREET \r\nCITY, LA ZIP ' — split on the CRLF; city also in `columns['3']` ('City: BATON ROUGE'), parish in `columns['2']` ('Parish: East Baton Rouge'); state is always LA; ZIP is the trailing 5 digits. Name = `name` (e.g. "JAY'S BAR B QUE"); the inspection's 'Permit Name' repeats it with the permit-type suffix.
- **inspection_date**: inspectionsData `columns['0']` = 'MM-DD-YYYY' (e.g. '10-26-2026'); facility-level `columns['1']` = 'Last Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY'; the printable prints 'Date 10/26/2026' and 'Time 02:50 PM'.
- **facility_id**: facilities/search: `id` = base64 of a numeric facility id (e.g. 'NTk3MQ==' -> 5971; ids seen 2500..11512431); inspectionsData echoes it as `facilityId`. Stable (same id across 7 inspections 2024-2026). The LDH Permit Number (e.g. '17-0001894-1') appears ONLY in the printable HTML. Use the decoded numeric id as the facility key; make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) as usual.
- **inspection_id**: inspectionsData `inspectionId` (integer, e.g. 151712897; monotonic with time: 147599301 in Nov 2023, 151712897 on 2026-10-26); also the `inspectionID=` query value in `printablePath`. Proposed source_id 'la_{inspectionId}'.
- **inspection_type**: inspectionsData `columns['2']` = 'Inspection Purpose: <X>'; values seen over 281 inspections: Routine/Renewal 212, Follow-up 46, Complaint 9, Other 6, 2nd Re-inspection 4, Opening 2, Preliminary 1, Post Fire 1. Permit TYPE is the suffix of `columns['1']` 'Permit Name: <name> <type>' (e.g. 'Restaurant', 'School Cafeteria'; full list unverified) — the only handle for excluding schools/day cares/hospitals/groceries.
- **Weekly volume**: About 1,300 facilities receive a new inspection per week (counted from the date-sorted list on 2026-11-02: ISO weeks 09-28..10-04 ≈ 1,145, 10-05..10-11 ≈ 1,335, 10-12..10-18 ≈ 1,285, 10-19..10-25 ≈ 1,395 facilities; cross-checked by the exclusive date search: (10-18,10-27) = 1,697 facilities for 10-19..10-26), i.e. roughly 1,400-1,600 inspections/week (follow-ups are ~16% of inspections and a facility re-inspected within the week is listed once) ≈ 70k inspections/year across 28,587 listed facilities (LDH says ~32,000 permitted operations). Posting lag: on 2026-11-02 the newest inspection date  ...
- **Weekly requests**: ~2,050 requests for a 9-day self-healing lookback without narratives: ~340 search/facilities pages (1,700 facilities / 5 per page) + ~1,700 inspectionsData calls (one per facility; each returns full history). Add ~1,800 printable fetches (one per NEW inspection) if the narrative text/permit number is wanted → ~3,900. Observed 0.5-2.1 s per call (avg 0.80 s in a 30-request burst), so ~30 min sequential or ~15 min with 2 workers; no rate limiting seen at ~250 calls in 20 minutes.
- **90-day backfill**: ~17,000 requests without narratives: ~13,000-15,000 facilities had an inspection in the last 90 days (5,460 in the 5 weeks to 10-26, extrapolated) → ~2,800 list pages + ~14,000 inspectionsData calls, and because inspectionsData returns the facility's whole history, this also yields those facilities' complete 2023-11-07..present history. With one printable per inspection in the window (~19,000 inspections) ≈ 36,000. A full all-facility history load is 5,718 list pages + 28,587 inspectionsData ≈ 34,300 requests (printables for 3 years of ~200k inspections are impractical; take narratives only go ...
- **History**: About 3 years: the oldest inspection in 22 sampled facility histories is 2023-11-07 (three facilities start exactly there; the two facilities on the final list page have a single inspection dated 11-07-2023), and the facility list's oldest 'Last Inspection Date' is 11-07-2023. inspectionsData returns every inspection per facility (sampled 4-39 per facility, mean 12.8 among recently inspected ones). Earlier years (the Eat Safe era, 2011-2023) are not in this portal; the Baton Rouge Socrata dataset has EBR Parish 2020-01-02..2025-09-30 only.
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly incremental, bounded and self-verifying: (1) Window start = _lookback_since (day before the recorded latest shipped inspection), end = today. Page `GET /API/index.cfm/search/{"keyword":"","from":b64(start-1 day),"to":b64(end+1 day)}/{page}` (bounds are strictly exclusive; dates as YYYY-MM-DD, JSON percent-encoded, '/' -> %2F) from page 0 until the server returns `[]`; a page with fewer than 5 rows is the last. Record expected = pages*5 + last-page rows. Take the newest 'Last Inspection Date' on `facilities/0` as the source max for _note_source_lag (LDH runs ~7 days behind). (2) If any list page is non-200 or non-JSON before `[]`, stop: _set_group_status(group, 'error' or 'skipped' on deadline), _hold_window_start(source, start) so next week re-reads the whole window, ship nothing from the list — never a partial window. (3) For each listed facility call `inspectionsData/{id}` (base64 id verbatim); retry once with Tyler-style backoff on 5xx/timeout, then DROP the facility and _hold_freshness(source, its last-inspection date, key=decoded id) (counted=False for deadline/breaker drops). Keep inspections dated >= start (on the first run keep everything: 3 years of history for free). `{"0": ["None"]}` is the explicit clean marker; a missing or failed inspectionsData is never recorded as clean. Completeness check before shipping: facilities read == facilities expected, else report partial via _set_group_status. (4) Optionally GET each new inspection's `printablePath` (HTML, ~5 KB, 0.2 s) for the narrative text and Permit Number; on failure ship the JSON item titles with their Critical/Non-Critical severity rather than holding — the JSON already carries the complete violation list. (5) Scoring policy: original_score None; risk_score computed from critical/non-critical counts (e.g. the Omaha pattern 100 - a*critical - b*noncritical, weights to be chosen by the owner), grade via calculate_vetted_grade, same-day duplicates via _same_day_rank; source_id 'la_{inspectionId}'; filter permit types by the 'Permit Name' suffix after sampling the suffix vocabulary. (6) Coordinates: geocode_census_batch on the clean 'STREET, CITY, LA ZIP' addresses with the Nominatim fallback, one-time for ~28.6k facilities (do it as a seed, not inside the weekly deadline), weekly only for new facility ids; coalesce lat/lng on every merge. (7) Pace: 2 workers, 0.3-0.5 s delay, ~2,000-4,000 requests/week (15-30 min); register the group with _record_group; no relay needed unless runners are refused (unverified).
- **Risks**:
  - No coordinates in the source: ~28.6k addresses must be geocoded once (Census batch + capped Nominatim fallback) and new facilities weekly; unmatched addresses would be unmappable, and any merge path must coalesce lat/lng.
  - No official score or grade: risk_score and grade would be entirely DineScores-computed from Critical/Non-Critical counts (LDH's two categories only), so the weighting is a policy decision and the page must not imply an LDH score.
  - The JSON violation entries are item TITLES only; the inspector narrative (what Jev reads) requires one printable HTML fetch per inspection (~1,400-1,600/week, ~19k for a 90-day backfill); the printable layout is unversioned HTML and a layout change would silently drop narratives (fall back to titles, never to 'clean').
  - 5 rows per page makes list traversal request-heavy (~340 pages/week, ~2,800 for 90 days, 5,718 for the full list); a 90-day backfill is ~17k requests without narratives.
  - Search filters silently require the `keyword` key (500 without it) and treat both `from` and `to` as strictly exclusive; a fetcher that forgets either loses whole days without any error. Dates are filterable only at facility level (last inspection), so a facility inspected twice in a window must be found through its inspectionsData.
  - Reachability from GitHub-hosted runners is unverified (only this container was tested); the site relay's ALLOWED_HOSTS does not include la.healthinspections.us, so a runner block would need a relay allowlist change (same Tyler platform as the already-scraped Houston/San Antonio/DC portals, which runners reach).
  - The portal 500s on malformed ids; a well-formed request that 500s must be treated as transient (retry, then drop + hold) — Tyler portals in the pipeline are known to 503 under modest load, although none was seen here at ~250 calls / 20 min.
  - All permit types are listed together (schools, day cares, hospitals, groceries, bars, hotels, 'Serving Kitchen'); the permit-type suffix in 'Permit Name' is the only type signal and its vocabulary is unverified, so restaurant filtering needs sampling first.
  - LDH posts inspections up to ~7 days late (newest date 10-26 on 11-02) and the parish searches showed no facilities with a last inspection older than ~2 years while the full list reaches 2023-11-07 (unexplained; unverified cap) — freshness reporting should use _note_source_lag and the unfiltered list's page 0.
  - Unofficial API: no documentation, no versioning, no terms of use (robots.txt 404); the Backbone client could be redeployed with different paths (the GA/router code already references a /stateoflouisiana/ path that no longer exists). The legacy eatsafe.la.gov host now serves an expired certificate for an unrelated domain and redirects to an LDH microsite.
  - The Baton Rouge Socrata dataset (ux2t-b9wr) is a tempting coordinate-bearing source for EBR Parish but has been stale since 2025-10-17 (max inspection 2025-09-30); do not rely on it.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/facilities/{page}` ({page}: 0-based page index; 5 facilities per page; sorted last-inspection-date DESC, facility id ASC; [] past the end) Facility list: id (base64 of numeric id), name, mapAddress 'STREET \r\nCITY, LA ZIP', columns {0: address, 1: 'Last Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY', 2: 'Parish: X', 3: 'City: X'}. Scanning from page 0 until a row is older than the window start is a cheap 'new since X' pass. [200 on pages 0,1,5,20,50,100,300,1000,2000-2029 (30 rapid, no delay, all 200, avg 0.80s), 3375-5717; 200 '[]' on 5718+ and 999999]
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/inspectionsData/{facilityId}` ({facilityId}: the base64 id exactly as the list returns it (e.g. NTk3MQ== = '5971'); the raw numeric id -> HTTP 500) Every inspection of one facility, newest first: inspectionId (int), facilityId, printablePath, columns {0: 'MM-DD-YYYY', 1: 'Permit Name: <name> <permit type>', 2: 'Inspection Purpose: Routine/Renewal|Follow-up|2nd Re-inspection|Complaint|Opening|Preliminary|Post Fire|Other'}, violations {i: ['Criti ... [200 for NTk3MQ== (7 inspections, 3,971 B) and MjUwMA== (6 inspections) and 20 more sampled facilities; 500 for the raw '5971']
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/search/{urlencoded JSON}/{page}` (JSON object, percent-encoded in the path, '/' replaced by %2F first; keys: keyword (REQUIRED, may be ''), from, to (dates, strictly exclusive, 'YYYY-MM-DD' or 'MM/DD/YYYY'), parish, city — every value ...) Filtered facility list (same row shape as facilities). The date window gives a bounded 'facilities with a new inspection in [A,B]' set that ends with an empty page. [200 with keyword; 200 with keyword+from/to, keyword+parish, keyword+city; 500 when the keyword key is absent; 400 (IIS) when the JSON braces are sent unencoded]
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/paper/_paper_inspection_form.cfm?inspectionID={inspectionId}&parentTableName=tblInspection&dsn=DHD_626&domainid=626&wguid=1367&wgunm=sysuser&wgdmn=626&logintime=%2200:00:00%20AM%22&public=1` (exactly the host-relative `printablePath` string from inspectionsData (inspectionID is the only variable part)) Printable 'Retail Food Notice of Violations' as HTML (not PDF, ~5 KB, 0.22 s): Permit Number (e.g. 17-0001894-1), Permit Name, Name of Establishment, Address, Date, Time, purpose, and per violation the Category, Code Reference and the FULL narrative with [Repeat]/[Corrected] tags under CRITICAL ITEM ... [200 for inspectionID 151712897 and 151712675]
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/filters` (none) Filter definitions: date filter (id 0, 'Last Inspection Date'), parish dropdown (64 parishes, className 'parish'), city dropdown (508 cities, className 'city') — the authoritative value lists for parish/city searches [200 (6,788 B)]
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/clientData` (none) Portal config (name 'State of Louisiana', map center 31.1801,-91.8749); useful only as a liveness check [200 (192 B)]
  - `GET https://la.healthinspections.us/ (and /publicportals/main.js, /publicportals/app.js, /publicportals/backbone-app/{router,models,collections,views}.js, /publicportals/libs/helpers.js, /publicportals/templates/**.hbs)` (none) Portal HTML and the Backbone/RequireJS sources the API paths were read from (helpers.js lines 31-70 build 'API/index.cfm/<resource>/...'; views.js lines 77-96 and 392-395 build the search JSON and the from/to date inputs) [200 for all; /stateoflouisiana/ -> 404; /robots.txt -> 404]
  - `GET https://data.brla.gov/resource/ux2t-b9wr.json (Socrata, City of Baton Rouge: 'Retail Food Inspections', East Baton Rouge Parish only)` (SODA ($limit, $order, $select, $where on inspectiondate, permitid, permitname, address_full_core, inspectionpurpose, iscritical, shortdesc, violation_comments, violationtype, geocoded_column)) Secondary/alternative source for EBR Parish only, WITH coordinates and narrative — but stale: rowsUpdatedAt 2025-10-17, max inspectiondate 2025-09-30, 103,121 violation rows / 6,052 permits since 2020-01-02. Not usable as a live source. [200 (metadata, $limit=3 sample, count query); 400 for a wrong column name in my first query]

## Columbia / Boone County, Missouri — Columbia/Boone County De

- **Feasibility**: medium; reachable: True
- **Population**: Boone County, MO: 192,154 (Census Bureau 2024 estimate); Columbia city ~130,900. The city-county department covers the whole county: of the newest 300 facilities 280 are Columbia, others Centralia, Hallsville, Sturgeon, Ashland, plus a few out-of-county addresses (Fulton, Mexico, New Franklin) that look like mobile vendors' home bases. ~900 Retail Food permits, ~1,406 permits of all types.
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores has no Missouri source (CLAUDE.md lists Kansas City and St. Louis under 'no machine-readable source at all'); Boone County is 120 miles from both.
- **Base URL**: https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/
- **Auth**: None. No login, cookies, token or captcha (clientData recaptchaActive "no"); plain GETs with a Chrome UA + Accept: application/json + X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest answered 200. The printable PDF also answered 200 without Referer or cookies. Not behind the MHD-style block: reachable directly from this container (~185 requests, all well-formed ones 200), so no relay needed (GitHub-runner reachab ...
- **Date filter**: Server-side, VERIFIED. Search JSON keys "from" and "to" (every value base64-encoded, '/'→'%2F', JSON percent-encoded in the path; see scripts/cmo_probe.py search_url). Filters the FACILITY's "Last Inspection Date" (the list column), not individual inspections; inclusive both ends. Formats YYYY-MM-DD and MM/DD/YYYY both accepted. Counts: Retail Food from=to=2026-10-30 → 4; 2026-10-27..28 → 14 (=5+9); 2026-10-26..11-02 → 28; from=2026-10-27 with no to → 24 (open-ended to today); to-only also works. Without permitType the same day returned 9 (4 Retail Food + 5 Pool/Spa), so always send permitType=Retail Food. CAVEAT (observed 1 of 28): IHOP (id MTU0) lists 10-27-2026 while its inspectionsData has a complete 10-30-2026 Reinspection (id 105565, the highest id seen), so the list date lags newly  ...
- **Pagination**: search/{json}/{page} and facilities/{page}: {page} starts at 0, 5 records per page, an empty list [] marks the end (verified: Retail Food 7-day window = 6 pages + 1 empty = 28 records). Ordering is facility last-inspection date DESC then facility id ASC (verified on 300 rows), and paging is stable (distinct ids == rows on every paged run). ~1,406 facilities of all permit types (last non-empty facilities page 281), ~900 Retail Food (last non-empty search page 179).
- **score**: None. No official numeric score in the JSON (437 inspections: only Inspection Date/Purpose/Inspector/Comments labels) or on the PDF. risk_score must be computed from violation counts by severity (the PDF's critical / non-critical split), i.e. a SCORE_FROM_TIERS-style source.
- **grade**: None. No letter grade on the JSON or the PDF form (PUBLIC HEALTH PRIORITY Low/Medium/High is the facility's risk category, not a result).
- **violations**: inspectionsData `violations` = dict '0'..'n' of 5-string lists: [0] 'NN - item title' (24 distinct codes, items 1-19 + legacy titles), [1] 'S-SSS.SSS - Food Code title. full paragraph text' (null never seen in 667 lines), [2] 'Corrected during inspection?: Yes|No' (229 Yes / 438 No), [3] 'Repeat: Yes|No' (68 Yes), [4] 'Comments: <observation>' (30-1,045 chars, median 173). 667 lines over 437 inspections, 0-9 per inspection in the sample week (mean 1.9, 13/33 clean).
- **severity**: NOT in the JSON (element [1] is the Food Code citation + paragraph text with no (P)/(Pf)/(C) marker; no Points element — Georgia's 6th element is absent). Only the printable PDF gives it: 'CRITICAL ITEMS' vs 'NON-CRITICAL ITEMS' sections plus a totals line. Verified on 6 PDFs that critical+non-critical totals == JSON violation count (3=1+2, 9=5+4, 0=0+0 ×4) and JSON 'Comments' text appears verbatim in the PDF (12/12), so rows join by citation + comment prefix. The same citation (4-601.11) is cri ...
- **coordinates**: None anywhere (clientData showMapbox false; no lat/lng in any endpoint) — geocode name+address+city, same as Georgia.
- **address**: search/facilities record mapAddress = "STREET \r\nCity, MO ZIP" (also columns['0']); parse `\n\s*(.+?),\s*MO\s*(\d{5})?`. Zip missing on 21/300 recent facilities; city case varies (Columbia/COLUMBIA); one bad house number seen ('915 A 0' = Shortwave Coffee). PDF header repeats ESTABLISHMENT NAME / ADDRESS / CITY/ZIP / PHONE.
- **inspection_date**: inspectionsData columns['1'] = 'Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY' (e.g. '10-30-2026'); list columns['3'] = 'Last Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY'; PDF 'DATE OF INSPECTION' MM/DD/YYYY. Verified identical across JSON and PDF on 6 inspections.
- **facility_id**: search/facilities `id` = base64 of a small decimal facility id (NjQ5 → 649, MTU0 → 154); the same base64 string is inspectionsData's `facilityId`. Stable key for the portal; DineScores id still make_restaurant_id(name, address, city).
- **inspection_id**: inspectionsData `inspectionId` (integer, monotonically assigned: 1328 in 2021 … 105565 on 2026-10-30; 437/437 distinct), also the inspectionID query parameter of printablePath. 0 marks the 'No Inspections To Display' placeholder.
- **inspection_type**: columns['2'] = 'Inspection Purpose: X' with X ∈ Routine (325/437), Reinspection (91), Reinspection with Fee (8), Emergency Response (7), attempt/Attempt (4 — closed on arrival, skip), Verification (1), Pre-Opening (1).
- **Weekly volume**: ~33 inspections at ~28 Retail Food facilities per week (window 2026-10-26..11-02: 23 Routine, 8 Reinspection, 2 'attempt' no-shows; 4-9 facilities per weekday, none Sat/Sun; 5 facilities visited twice). Including Mobile Food/Temp/Farmers Market permits adds a few; Pool/Spa (116 of the newest 300 facilities) should be excluded via permitType. ~900 Retail Food facilities in all.
- **Weekly requests**: ~70-90 requests: 6-8 search pages (28 facilities ÷ 5 + the empty terminator; ~10 with the pipeline's 9-day overlapping window), one inspectionsData per returned facility (~28-35), one printable PDF per new inspection (~31-35, ~500 KB each ≈ 16-18 MB). Measured pace with a 0.3 s delay: 28 inspectionsData in 36 s; PDFs 1-3 s each. No throttling seen at 60 undelayed requests.
- **90-day backfill**: ~850 requests: ~65-75 search pages (≈300-350 Retail Food facilities whose last inspection falls in the last 90 days, extrapolating 28/week and the newest-300 sample spanning 09-24..10-30), ~330 inspectionsData, and ~430 PDFs (13 weeks × ~33 inspections; ~215 MB). A full-history backfill (portal starts mid-2020) is ~180 search pages + ~900 inspectionsData but ~14,000 PDFs (~7 GB) — the PDFs are the whole cost, so limit PDF-backed severity to a bounded history (e.g. 2 years) and ship older inspections without a computed score or not at all. No bulk/CSV export exists.
- **History**: From mid-2020: earliest inspection seen 2020-07-22 over 437 inspections at 28 facilities (1-18 per facility; facility id 1 starts 2020-12-01, id 100 on 2020-10-20). inspectionsData returns the full history in one call (no paging). Nothing before the Tyler portal went live (~2020) is online.
- **Recommended strategy**: Shared Tyler-publicportals fetcher (Georgia/Louisiana/Columbia) parameterised per portal: base URL, permitType value ('Retail Food'), list column positions, inspection column labels, violation element layout (GA 6 incl. 'Points: N' and (p)/(pf)/(c) in the citation; LA 3 [section 'Critical Items'/'Non-Critical Items', item, status]; Columbia 5 with no severity → PDF), and severity source. Weekly: (1) since = _lookback_since('Columbia MO', ...) with the API/CSV 400-day cap; search permitType=Retail Food, from=since, to=today, page 0.. until [] — any non-200, non-JSON or exception while paging aborts the group (status error, ship nothing) rather than shipping a truncated facility list. (2) For each returned facility GET inspectionsData/{id}; skip inspectionId 0 placeholders and 'attempt' purposes; a failed fetch → _hold_freshness('Columbia MO', <facility's list date>, key=facilityId) so the window re-reads it. (3) Ship every inspection with date >= since that is not already known (compare source_id 'columbia_mo_{inspectionId}' / restaurant+date against data.js and D1 — ship unknown older ones too, since a late-entered inspection surfaces only when its facility is next returned). (4) For each shipped inspection GET the printable PDF, parse the critical/non-critical rows by following cm/Do into the Form XObjects, and REQUIRE (a) the totals line to parse and (b) critical+non-critical == len(JSON violations) and (c) each JSON violation matched to a PDF row by citation + comment prefix; otherwise drop that inspection and _hold_freshness(source, its date, key=inspectionId) (counted=True on a parse failure, counted=False on timeout/5xx/deadline); never record a blocked PDF as clean. Map critical → 'priority' (5 pts), non-critical → 'core' (1 pt) in SCORE_FROM_TIERS_SOURCES (policy to confirm); original_score None; inspection_type from Purpose; 'Repeat: Yes' and 'Corrected: Yes' as chips/text only. (5) _same_day_rank handles Emergency Response + Reinspection on one day. (6) Completeness check for the run: the search list's 'Last Inspection Date' for each facility must appear among its inspectionsData dates, and the number of distinct facilities paged must equal the sum of page sizes; log both. (7) Because the list date lags newly entered inspections (IHOP case), rely on the overlapping lookback plus a cheap monthly sweep: page the dateless permitType=Retail Food search (~180 requests) and fetch inspectionsData for any facility whose list date is newer than its stored latest OR whose stored inspection count differs — ~5 minutes at 0.3 s. Register the group with _record_group('Columbia MO', ...) and _set_group_status when PDFs were skipped for the deadline. Geocode name+address+city (no coordinates); normalise 'COLUMBIA' → 'Columbia', fill missing zips from the geocoder.
- **Risks**:
  - No official score or grade: DineScores would publish a computed risk score only (critical/non-critical counts), a policy decision for a source whose county never grades.
  - Severity lives only in a ~500 KB PDF per inspection whose values are flattened form-field XObjects; the pipeline's stdlib PDF parser (_oc_pdf_text_rows) reads only the template, so new cm/Do-following parsing code is required and a form-layout change silently breaks severity (mitigate with the totals==JSON-count checksum and the Orange-County-style mass-failure guard).
  - The facility list's 'Last Inspection Date' lags newly entered inspections (IHOP: list 10-27, newest inspection 10-30 with a finished report), so a date-window search can miss a facility until the field refreshes; lag length unverified.
  - Small source: ~33 inspections/week, ~900 Retail Food facilities, Boone County ~192k people — low value per line of fetcher code unless the fetcher is shared with Georgia/Louisiana.
  - No coordinates; some bad addresses ('915 A 0'), 21/300 without zip, so geocoding must be robust and 'Never lose coordinates' applies.
  - Full-history backfill with severity costs ~14,000 PDFs (~7 GB); bound it.
  - Same citation can be critical or non-critical depending on the observation, so no shortcut table; every inspection needs its PDF.
  - 'attempt' (closed on arrival) and 'No Inspections To Display' placeholder records (inspectionId 0) must be skipped or they ship as clean inspections.
  - No published terms of use or robots.txt found (robots.txt 404); data policy unverified. GitHub-runner reachability unverified (this container reached it directly, no block seen at 60 undelayed requests).
  - Permit-type filter must always be sent: without it the same window returns Pool/Spa, Tattoo and Tobacco permits (116 of the newest 300 facilities are pools).
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/clientData` (none) Portal config: advSearchToggle true, recaptchaActive "no", showMapbox false (no coordinates), map centre 38.9517/-92.3341 [200 (480 B)]
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/filters` (none) Advanced-search filter definitions: city dropdown (179 dirty values), permitType dropdown (Retail Food, Mobile Food, Push Cart, Temp Food, Farmers Market Retail/Sampling, Pool/Spa, Tattoo/Body Art, Tobacco, Junkyard), 'Last Inspection Date' range (keys from/to) [200 (2,542 B)]
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/facilities/{page}` ({page} from 0; 5 per page) Unfiltered facility list, same record shape/order as search; used only to count facilities (~1,406) and sample ordering [200 x 79 (incl. 60-request undelayed burst, median 0.38 s); page past the end → 200 with []]
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/search/{json}/{page}` ({json} = {"permitType":b64,"from":b64,"to":b64,"keyword":b64-or-empty} with '/'→'%2F' then percent-encoded; e.g. .../search/%7B%22permitType%22:%22UmV0YWlsIEZvb2Q=%22,%22from%22:%22MjAyNi0xMC0yNg==%22 ...) Paged facility list filtered by permit type and facility last-inspection date; record = {id (base64 of decimal facility id), name, mapAddress "STREET \r\nCity, MO ZIP", columns {0 address, 1 'Phone Number: NA', 2 'Permit Type: Retail Food', 3 'Last Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY'}}. No score column, no ... [200 on every call (~45 calls); [] at end]
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/inspectionsData/{facilityId}` ({facilityId} MUST be the base64 form from the list (NjQ5 = 649); the decimal id → HTTP 500 HTML) All inspections of one facility newest first: {inspectionId int, facilityId, printablePath, columns {1 'Inspection Date: MM-DD-YYYY', 2 'Inspection Purpose: Routine|Reinspection|Reinspection with Fee|Emergency Response|Verification|Pre-Opening|attempt', 3 'Inspector: ..', 4 'Inspection Comments: ..' ... [200 x 36; inspectionsData/649 (decimal) → 500]
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/_templates/638/1/inspections/_report_full.cfm?inspectionID={inspectionId}&parentTableName=tblInspection&dsn=dhd_638_1&domainid=638` (inspectionID = inspectionId (printablePath in the JSON, relative to API/index.cfm/)) Printable Food Establishment Inspection Report as PDF (~500 KB, 1 page; 2 pages/900 KB when a section overflows). The ONLY severity source: sections 'CRITICAL ITEMS' and 'NON-CRITICAL ITEMS' (rows = CODE REFERENCE + description + CORRECT BY) plus a totals line 'CRITICAL ITEMS n NON-CRITICAL ITEMS m' ... [200 x 7 (with and without Referer), application/pdf;charset=UTF-8]
  - `GET https://columbia-mo.healthinspections.us/API/index.cfm/security` (none) Present on Louisiana's portal; absent here [404]

## Virginia statewide (VDH) on MyHealthDepartment — Richmond, H

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: Virginia minus Fairfax County (which runs its own site and is already a DineScores source): approximately 7.6 million people — Virginia ~8.8M (2024 Census estimate) minus Fairfax County ~1.15M plus Fairfax City/Falls Church ~40k served by Fairfax County HD. Population figures are from memory, unverified. The 33 district slugs include Richmond city + Henrico + Chesterfield + Chickahominy (Richmond metro), Virginia Beach, Norfolk, Chesapeake, Portsmouth, Hampton, Peninsula (Newport News/Williamsbu ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. Fairfax County is NOT on the portal at all: its 'View Inspections' link on /virginia/districts goes to https://www.fairfaxcounty.gov/health/food/inspection-reports, and searchStr probes for Fairfax/Herndon/Reston/McLean/Vienna/Annandale/Lorton/Burke/Great Falls/Centreville over 2026-10-01..10-26 returned zero Fairfax County rows (hits were street names elsewhere). The 18 'Chantilly' rows are all zip 20152 (Loudoun County's South Riding, served by va-loudoun); the single 'Falls Church' row  ...
- **Base URL**: https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ (search API: POST to the root with task 'searchInspections'; pages: /virginia, /virginia/districts, /{district-slug}, /{district-slug}/inspection/?inspectionID=, /{district-slug}/permit/?permitID=)
- **Auth**: None. Same browser-like headers the pipeline's _mhd_session() sends (CHROME_UA, Content-Type application/json, Origin/Referer the portal). No cookies or tokens needed; all 172 logged POST/GET requests plus 8 curl GETs from this container answered HTTP 200 (zero 403/429). Production must still ride functions/api/relay.js like every MHD jurisdiction (the portal 403s GitHub runners; the relay's host  ...
- **Date filter**: filters.date = "YYYY-MM-DD to YYYY-MM-DD" (same string the pipeline builds), verified server-side: single-day windows return only that day's rows (inspectionDate values all equal the requested day on every probe), multi-day windows span exactly the range, and the 33 districts' single-day rows sum to the statewide total (218 = 218 on 2026-10-20). Rows carry inspectionDate as MM/DD/YYYY ("10/15/2026"), which _parse_mhd_record already handles (lines 6504-6509, comment literally says 'MM/DD/YYYY (Virginia)').
- **Pagination**: start offsets 0,25,...,200 with count 25 (count ignored, as on other MHD slugs); the portal rejects start >= 225 ({"err": true, "msg": "bad request"}), so a query is capped at 225 rows. Statewide 'virginia' paging is UNSTABLE: 2026-10-20 returned 218 rows over 9 pages but only 137 unique inspectionIDs (rows re-served), and Thursday 2026-10-22 hit the 225 cap — never fetch from the statewide slug. Per district the whole day fit in one page (max 19 rows, Chesterfield); a 7-day district window needs 1-5 pages (Arlington week = 48 rows = 2 pages). Dedupe by inspectionID regardless (_mhd_search_all ...
- **score**: NONE. score = 0 and scoreDisplay = "" on every one of the ~550 rows seen across 33 districts; the detail page prints 'Score & Grade: 0  Grade:' and the FAQ describes P/Pf/Core classification only. score_scale must be 'none' (as for Clark County WA / Yolo / Orange County): risk_score comes from the scraped violations, never from search rows.
- **grade**: None published (scoreDisplay always empty; detail page 'Grade:' blank).
- **violations**: Detail page HTML only (no JSON): <h3 class="observations-header">Observations &amp; Corrective Actions</h3><p class="observations-text">...</p>, items separated by <br /><br />, each 'NN: 12VAC5-421-XXXX Observed <text><br />Corrective Actions: <text>' where NN is the FDA inspection-form item number and 12VAC5-421-XXXX the Virginia Food Regulations section. Parsed unchanged by _mhd_parse_detail_html (verified on 2 pages with 6 and 2 violations). Clean inspections have an EMPTY block (2 of 4 page ...
- **severity**: NOT printed: no Priority / Pf / Core tag anywhere on the detail page (0 matches), despite the FAQ describing those classes. Severity must be derived: the pipeline's classify_violation text path (then Jev) as for every other MHD jurisdiction; optionally a deterministic hint from the item number (FDA form: items 1-29 = foodborne-illness risk factors and interventions, 30-56 = good retail practices) or from a 12VAC5-421 section -> P/Pf/C table (would need building; unverified).
- **coordinates**: NONE anywhere — search rows have no lat/lng keys, the detail page and the permit page have no map or coordinates (0 matches for latitude/longitude/maps). Geocode from addressLine1 + addressLine2 + city + state + zip; main() already runs geocode_missing_coords(mhd_data) on MHD groups (line ~9055).
- **address**: addressLine1 (e.g. '365 Merchant Walk Square'), addressLine2 (usually ''), city ('Charlottesville'), state ('VA'), zip ('22902'); detail/permit pages print the same. Mobile units carry the operator's base address (one Arlington-permitted cart lists a Falls Church 22044 address).
- **inspection_date**: inspectionDate, string MM/DD/YYYY (e.g. "10/15/2026"); also printed on the detail page as 'October 15, 2026'. Verified. timein is a time-of-day stored as 1899-12-30T10:37:00.000Z — ignore.
- **facility_id**: permitID (GUID, e.g. DA8019B5-19BA-442E-A83C-0DB5CF6B870C) on every search row; the permit page lives at /{district}/permit/?permitID=. DineScores id stays make_restaurant_id(name, address, city). Verified.
- **inspection_id**: inspectionID (GUID, e.g. 39BFD954-A02F-4F22-AA73-6E5028FD5070) on every search row; detail page at /{district}/inspection/?inspectionID=. Verified; _parse_mhd_record already uses it as source_id.
- **inspection_type**: purpose: observed values Routine (~70%), Risk Factor (~15%), Follow-Up (~7%), Pre-Opening (~5%), Other (~2%); the district page's dropdown also offers Courtesy, Critical Procedures, Local, Shellfish Certification, Training. inspectionType == permitType = facility class: Full Service Restaurant, Fast Food, Educational Facility Food Service, Long Term Care and Other Custodial Living Centers, Child Care Food Service, Mobile Food Unit, Convenience Store Food Service, Carry Out, Hospital Food Service ...

Measured statewide daily row totals: Mon 10/19 182, Tue 10/20 218, Wed 10/21 187, Thu 10/22 >=225 (capped; true count unknown), Fri 10/23 188, Sat 10/17 15, Sun 10/18 and 10/25 2 each, Mon 10/26 175 (week of 10/19 >= 1,017). Roughly two thirds are restaurant/retail types (Full Service Restaurant + Fast Food + Carry Out + Mobile + Convenience + Caterer ~ 143/218 on 10/20) and one third institutional (schools 43, long-term care 15, child care 9, hospitals 3, correctional 2, camps/commissary). Detail pages are the scarce resource: at the portal's measured cadence on other jurisdictions (a few hundred per ~15-minute ban window, MHD_BAN_COOLDOWN 900 s) th ...

At a few hundred detail pages per 15-minute ban window that is ~55 windows, 14+ hours — must be staged over several runs with --until-date and --skip-known-inspections, oldest-first, exactly like th ...
- **History**: About 2 years: September 2024 returns 0 rows statewide and from va-richmond; '2024-10-01 to 2024-10-04' already returns a full page (10/04/2024 rows), and every later month has rows. Earliest exact day not pinned (between 2024-09-29 and 2024-10-04, unverified). Permit pages list each permit's inspections back to October 2024 (e.g. Don Tito: 10/2024, 02/2025, 06/2025, 10/2025, 02/2026, 06/2026, 10/2026). The portal appears to have launched around then (VDH's navigation guide PDF is dated 2026-07-16).
- **Recommended strategy**: Add the 33 district slugs as ordinary MHD jurisdictions (NOT bucket mode, NOT the statewide slug), reusing _fetch_mhd_jurisdiction_api / fetch_dfw via MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS entries built by a small helper like _mhd_tn_bucket, e.g. {'display_name': 'VDH Richmond City', 'default_city': 'Richmond', 'state': 'VA', 'score_scale': 'none', 'metro': 'Richmond' | 'Hampton Roads' | 'Northern Virginia' | '', 'programs': ['Food Establishment'], 'lag_days': 14}; MHD_METRO_SLUG_GROUPS entries 'richmond' (va-richmond, va-henrico, va-chesterfield, va-chickahominy), 'hamptonroads' (va-virginia-beach, va-norfolk, va-chesapeake, va-portsmouth, va-hampton, va-peninsula, va-western-tidewater), 'nova' (va-arlington, va-alexandria, va-loudoun, va-prince-william) and 'virginia' (all 33), so coverage can start with the metros. Weekly incremental: per district, plain date search (searchStr '' and purpose '') over the self-healing window (_lookback_since from that district's recorded latest SHIPPED inspection minus FRESHNESS_OVERLAP_DAYS, lag_days 14 because the portal was 7 days behind on 2026-11-02), default 7-day windows with the existing cap bisection (_mhd_fetch_window), dedupe by inspectionID, then fetch EVERY inspection's detail page under its own district slug (_mhd_path keeps this right since each district is its own jurisdiction), trust_official_score False / score_scale 'none' so risk is computed from the scraped observations (classify_violation + Jev, Jev deciding F/summary as for other no-score sources). Completeness guarantees (never ship a partial window silently): (1) a search page sequence that reaches MHD_QUERY_CAP on a single day is already logged and should mark the group via _set_group_status (no district came within 10x of it); (2) any detail page that fails (403/ban/timeout/deadline) drops the inspection AND _hold_freshness(source, date) pins the district's latest before it, so the window is re-read next run — the existing MHD machinery; (3) add a Virginia layout guard: a detail page whose observations block is empty must count as clean ONLY if its checklist is present and shows zero 'OUT' rows (and the 'Score & Grade' marker is present); an empty block with OUT rows, or a page without the checklist, is 'unread' -> drop + hold, so a template change cannot read as zero violations; (4) re-scrape /virginia/districts each run and fail the group (or at least warn in _GROUP_NOTES) if the slug set differs from the config, so a new or renamed district cannot silently vanish; (5) optional cheap cross-check: for each uncapped day, the statewide 'virginia' row total (sum of page sizes, 9 POSTs) must equal the sum of district rows — a mismatch means a slug is missing. Because detail pages dominate (~1,000+/week, 4-5 ban windows), consider running Virginia as its own fetch group/job (own deadline) or excluding institutional inspectionTypes (schools, long-term care, child care, hospitals, correctional) via a permitType denylist to cut ~1/3; this is an owner poli ...
- **Risks**:
  - robots.txt ends with 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /' and explicitly blocks anthropic-ai and ClaudeBot (plus GPTBot, CCBot, ...); the portal shows no terms-of-use page (unverified beyond the landing/detail/permit pages). This is the same portal DineScores already scrapes for DFW/Portland/CO/UT/GA/TN, so it is a policy call for the owner, not a technical blocker.
  - No score of any kind (score 0, scoreDisplay '' on every row; FAQ and detail page confirm): every shipped record depends on a successful detail-page read; a search-only backfill or any undetailed record would ship the neutral risk-70 placeholder as a false C.
  - Detail-page volume: ~1,000-1,100 pages/week (13,500-14,000 for 90 days) on a portal that bans detail pages after a few hundred per ~15-minute window (measured on other jurisdictions; Virginia's own cadence unverified) — roughly doubles the weekly MHD detail load inside the shared 240-minute fetch deadline; a 90-day backfill is 14+ hours and must be staged.
  - Detail pages are served only under the inspection's own district slug (the statewide path returns the welcome page; another district's path returns an empty shell) — a statewide design or a wrong path would read every inspection as unparseable or, worse, clean. Keep one jurisdiction per district.
  - Severity is not printed (no P/Pf/Core tags on the page); severity and risk rest entirely on text classification/Jev, with only an optional item-number hint (items 1-29 vs 30-56) — grades will be text-derived, like Clark County WA/Yolo/Orange County.
  - No coordinates anywhere (search rows, detail page, permit page): ~1,000 inspections/week need geocoding for new permits (re-inspected restaurants keep coordinates through the COALESCE merge); geocoder capacity/cost unverified.
  - Publishing lag: on 2026-11-02 the newest published day was 2026-10-26 (7 days) and the last 7 days were empty — one observation; whether it is a weekly batch is unverified. Needs lag_days >= 14 so the self-healing lookback re-reads the late days.
  - The statewide 'virginia' slug is unusable for fetching: paging re-serves rows (218 rows, 137 unique on 10/20) and Thursday 10/22 hit the 225 cap. Bucket mode (searchStr) is also NOT viable: searchStr switches to one row per permit (confirmed: a permit with inspections 10/20 and 09/28 returned only 10/20) AND silently drops ~40% of permits (7 of 11 Arlington permits on 10/20, 25 of 48 in the week, even when split by purpose), for reasons unverified.
  - District list may change: VDH has 35 districts (Roanoke City and Alleghany already appear merged as va-alleghany-roanoke, Thomas Jefferson is now va-blue-ridge); a new or renamed slug would silently drop a district unless /virginia/districts is re-scraped and compared each run.
  - About one third of rows are institutional food service (schools, long-term care, child care, hospitals, correctional) that _mhd_is_food_record keeps; decide whether to map them or exclude by permitType — exclusion also cuts the detail-page load by a third.
  - Mobile Food Unit rows carry the operator's base address (an Arlington-permitted cart lists a Falls Church 22044 address), so they geocode to the owner's location, not where food is sold.
  - Transient infrastructure: one request from this container failed with the agent proxy dropping the connection (ProxyError RemoteDisconnected), not a portal error; the pipeline's retry ladder covers this in production.
  - History is only ~2 years deep (starts ~2024-10-01), so long histories are not available; detail pages for 2024-2026 are reachable but would cost one request each.

## Tucson / Pima County, Arizona — Pima County Health Departmen

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: All of Pima County, Arizona (Tucson, Oro Valley, Marana, Sahuarita, Green Valley, Vail, Catalina, Ajo, Arivaca, Amado, Mt Lemmon; the Tucson MSA is coterminous with the county): ~1.04 million residents (2020 census 1,043,433 — from memory, not live-verified). Food program: 5,177 permitted facilities (4,940 ever inspected), of which 630 are mobile units/push carts/vehicles whose siteAddress is a commissary or base — decide whether to ship them. Arizona's other large market (Maricopa/Phoenix) is a ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores has no Arizona coverage (CLAUDE.md's metro list has no AZ entry); Tucson/Pima appears only in the 'Candidate sources surveyed but not added' list as 'Lubbock TX and Tucson/Pima (Hedgerow portals)'.
- **Base URL**: https://healthinspect.pima.gov/portal/api/v0/portal/
- **Auth**: None for the public "disclosure" endpoints: no API key, no login, no CSRF token; plain GET/POST with a Chrome-like User-Agent and Accept/Content-Type: application/json worked from this container (~130 requests, all 200). The site is fronted by Cloudflare (Server: cloudflare, sets a __cf_bm cookie) but served no challenge to curl/python-requests; the cookie is not required. Admin endpoints answer 4 ...
- **Date filter**: No server-side date filter exists on any endpoint observed (verified: the SPA searches/filters entirely client-side — `filterService.filter(...)` in main.3d46a4d1b8b9a5e2.js — and the only request body is {sort, disclosureProgramId, fields}). Incremental selection must be done client-side from the facility list's `lastInspectionDate` (ISO local timestamp with -07:00 offset, e.g. "2026-10-17T11:40:05.6099184-07:00"; 4,940 of 5,177 facilities have one, null = never inspected), then from each inspection's `inspectionDate` (same format) in the facility's inspection list. Use the first 10 characters as the date; converting to UTC moves late-evening inspections to the next day (observed "2026-10-16T23:45:39.7994619-07:00").
- **Pagination**: None usable. POST disclosure/program/facilities returns the WHOLE program's facility list in one response (Food: 5,177 records, 5,042,894 bytes, 5.3 s). The response carries pageNumber/pageSize/totalNumberOfRecords but they are always 0 and request-body pageNumber/pageSize are ignored (verified: same 500/200 behaviour with and without them), so completeness cannot be checked against a server count. Per-facility inspection lists (inspectionDetails/) and per-inspection checklists (inspectionResults/{id}) are unpaged arrays. The `fields` body parameter does not restrict returned columns (all fiel ...
- **score**: NONE. hazardScore is null on all 5,177 facilities and on every inspection seen (Food, Pools, Lodging, Campgrounds). original_score must be None; risk_score has to be computed from citations (cannedComments[].severity → Priority/Priority Foundation/Core, e.g. 100 − 5P − 2PF − C as fetch_omaha does from TOTAL counts) or mapped from the rating. inspection/{id}/details/ gives criticalInfractionCount/nonCriticalInfractionCount (citation counts; e.g. Probation inspection 8/9, Fail 1/1, Excellent 0/0-1 ...
- **grade**: hazardRating (categorical, no letter): current scheme Excellent (2,806 facilities) > Good (1,381) > Satisfactory (210) > Needs Improvement (23) > Probation (1) > Fail (4); plus Educational (273; a new facility's first, ungraded visit), Pass (226; an Enforcement re-inspection that passed), Complaint Open/Closed, Observations/Site Visit, Emergency Response, IHH (imminent health hazard), No rating, null (237 never inspected). Each rating has a stable hazardRatingModelElementId GUID (Excellent 03a86 ...
- **violations**: inspectionResults/{inspectionId} → array of checklist items {description, result, isCritical, category.description, cannedComments[]}. A violation = an item with result 'Out of Compliance' or 'Corrected During Inspection'; each such item carries one or more cannedComments citations {description = Food Code section text ('4-601.11 (B) The FOOD-CONTACT SURFACES of cooking EQUIPMENT and pans shall be kept free of encrusted grease…'), severity, observationComment = inspector's observation ('Cookline ...
- **severity**: cannedComments[].severity ∈ {'Priority', 'Priority Foundation', 'Core'} (12 citations seen: 1/2/9; other values unverified). Item-level isCritical is a boolean on the checklist item and does not always match the citation severity (a Priority Foundation citation sat on a non-critical 'Proper date marking' item). Prefer citation severity for scoring; counts in inspection/{id}/details/ (criticalInfractionCount = Priority+PF citations, nonCriticalInfractionCount = Core; consistent in 5 of 5 cross-ch ...
- **coordinates**: facilities POST → latitude/longitude (WGS84 decimals, e.g. 32.4076245, -110.7152009): 5,173 of 5,177 non-null, 5,166 inside the Pima County bbox (31.3<lat<32.6, -113.4<lng<-110.4). No geocoding needed; coalesce the 4 nulls.
- **address**: facilities POST → siteAddress, one string 'STREET[, Suite], City AZ 85xxx' ('4210 N 1st Ave Ste 100, Tucson AZ 85705'; whitespace sometimes doubled: '12900    ORGANIZATION RIDGE RD, MT LEMMON                 AZ 85619'). Split at the last comma; city/zip by regex ',\s*(.+?)\s+AZ\s+(\d{5})$' — matches 5,163; 14 deviants: 'Tucson Arizona 85705' (11), 'Tucsonaz 85713', one empty, one missing the zip. Cities: Tucson 4,579, Oro Valley 148, Marana 143, Green Valley 122, Sahuarita 52, Vail 44, Ajo 26, C ...
- **inspection_date**: inspectionDetails/ → [].inspectionDate, ISO local time with -07:00 offset ('2026-10-17T11:40:05.6099184-07:00'); facility list → lastInspectionDate same format (newest inspection of the facility; equals the newest list entry in every case checked). Use [:10]; never convert to UTC.
- **facility_id**: facilities POST → result[].id. Two formats, both stable primary keys of the portal: synthetic legacy ids 'f1130177-ffff-ffff-ffff-fffff3130223' (2,709 of 5,177) and real GUIDs 'a4f7493b-7f65-487d-bd81-e5cd0c30e882' (2,468). facilityNumber/siteNumber/parcelNumber are always null. 5,177 distinct ids; 7 name+address duplicate groups (e.g. VILLA HERMOSA x3) collapse onto one DineScores id via make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) — _same_day_rank handles them.
- **inspection_id**: inspectionDetails/ → [].id (GUID, e.g. 462a1932-e706-489e-9ca6-952a02b6db11) used by inspectionResults/{id} and inspection/{id}/details/; human number in inspectionNumber ('Food Safety -26-397161', 'Campground Health & Safety-22-232648'). Suggested source_id 'pima_<guid>'.
- **inspection_type**: inspectionType: 'Standard Frequency Inspection ' (trailing space, 174 of 206 sampled) and 'Enforcement Inspection' (re-inspection after Needs Improvement/Probation; rated Pass or Fail). inspectionReason/actionsTaken were null on all 206 sampled.
- **Weekly volume**: About 110-140 Food inspections per week. Facilities whose lastInspectionDate falls in each ISO week, Aug-Oct 2026: 109, 113, 115, 90, 69, 113, 134, 104, 141, 128 (this undercounts same-week re-inspections of one facility slightly). A 15-facility random sample shows ~1.25 inspections/facility/yr (19 in 2024, 18 in 2025) → ~6,500/yr for 5,177 facilities ≈ 125/week. 1,175 facilities had their last inspection within 90 days of the snapshot; 271 within 30 days.
- **Weekly requests**: ~270-300 requests: 1 facility-list POST (5.0 MB, ~5 s, sort by id) + ~130 inspectionDetails/ GETs (facilities whose lastInspectionDate is on/after the lookback start) + ~140 inspectionResults/{id} GETs (one per new inspection). Add ~140 more if inspection/{id}/details/ is also fetched for the official critical/non-critical counts (~420 total). Measured ~0.2-1.2 s per GET with no throttling, so a weekly run is a few minutes.
- **90-day backfill**: ~2,700 requests (1 list POST + 1,175 facility inspection lists + ~1,500 inspection checklists), ~4,200 if inspection/{id}/details/ is fetched too. A full-history load of all 5,177 facilities (~28,500 inspections at the sampled 5.5 per facility) would be ~34,000 requests (~60k with details); at ~0.5 s each that is ~5 hours over several runs.
- **History**: Inspection lists go back to the portal's Oct 2019 launch: earliest inspection seen 2019-12-05 (15-facility random sample; Domino's #7626 list starts 2020-10-01, BAG O CRAB 2025-09-18), 5.5 inspections per facility on average (0-26). Facility lastInspectionDate ranges 2022-01-31..2026-10-17. Pre-2019 records from the retired system are not in this API (unverified). Newest data as served on 2026-11-02 was 2026-10-17 (whole portal, all programs): a ~16-day lag of unknown cause (fixed publication delay vs. stalled sync — unverified).
- **Recommended strategy**: Model it on fetch_omaha (dinescores_pipeline.py:5048) — list rows then per-record detail — but with JSON instead of PDFs. (1) POST disclosure/program/facilities with body {"sort":[{"field":"id","order":"asc"}],"disclosureProgramId":"c1df4447-c948-4d39-a649-b8855a33b857","fields":["facilityName"]} (sort MUST be id; facilityName times out). Treat the list as atomic: require HTTP 200, valid JSON, len(result) >= max(4000, 0.9 x the count recorded in the previous run's freshness detail); otherwise _record_group(..., status='error') and ship NOTHING (no partial window). Record max(lastInspectionDate) and pass it to _note_source_lag so the portal's own ~16-day lag reads as source lag, not a broken fetch. (2) Window start = _lookback_since(group, default, FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_API_DAYS cap) (self-healing from the recorded latest SHIPPED inspection). Select facilities with lastInspectionDate[:10] >= window start (≈130/week; the window start minus overlap also catches an older in-window inspection of the same facility because step 3 reads the facility's whole list). (3) For each selected facility, oldest lastInspectionDate first (a deadline cut drops the newest tail, like MHD/Omaha): GET disclosure/program/{P}/facility/{id}/inspectionDetails/; keep inspections with inspectionDate[:10] >= window start. On any non-200/parse failure: drop the facility and _hold_freshness(source, lastInspectionDate[:10], key=facility id) (counted=False when the drop is the deadline or a breaker). (4) For each kept inspection GET disclosure/program/inspectionResults/{inspectionId}; violations = items with result in ('Out of Compliance','Corrected During Inspection'), one violation per cannedComments citation [category via classify_violation(description), severity from cannedComments[].severity (Priority→critical, Priority Foundation→major-ish, Core→minor), text = observationComment + ' — ' + description]; optionally GET .../inspection/{id}/details/ for criticalInfractionCount/nonCriticalInfractionCount. A failed checklist read drops THAT inspection and holds freshness at its date (never ship 'no violations' for an unread checklist — the hard invariant). (5) Record: original_score None; risk_score computed from citation severities (e.g. 100 − 5·Priority − 2·PF − 1·Core, matching fetch_omaha) and/or the rating: Fail/Probation/IHH should force an F (policy decision for the owner, like FAIRFAX_RESULT_RISK's result mapping); keep hazardRating in `results` ('Rating Needs Improvement'); inspection_type from inspectionType.strip(); skip ratings 'Educational' (ungraded first visit), 'Complaint Open/Closed', 'Observations/Site Visit', 'Emergency Response' or ship them unscored — decide explicitly; name/address/city/zip from facilityName + siteAddress split at the last comma; lat/lng from the list (coalesce on merge); source_id 'pima_<inspection GUID>'; source_url the SPA hash route. Same-day pairs exist (GETSU-KO SUSHI: Standard 'Needs Improvement' + Enforcement 'Pass' on 2026-10-15) — _sam ...
- **Risks**:
  - The Food facility list only works when sorted by `id`: the SPA's own `facilityName` sort returned 500 after ~31 s on 6/6 attempts (server-side query timeout on the 5,177-row program; smaller programs are fine). The id sort took 5.3 s and could also start timing out as the table grows — alternative sort fields were not tested.
  - No numeric score anywhere (hazardScore null on all 5,177 facilities and every inspection); grades are categorical (Excellent/Good/Satisfactory/Needs Improvement/Probation/Fail). risk_score must be computed from citation severities or mapped from the rating — a policy decision, and the rating→score mapping would need calibration (CLAUDE.md: interim mappings for CO/Utah ran 9-27 points harsh).
  - The whole portal lagged ~16 days at probe time (newest inspection 2026-10-17 on 2026-11-02, across Food, Pools, Lodging). Whether that is a fixed publication delay or a stalled sync is unverified; the freshness report will flag STALE at >21 days, so use _note_source_lag and expect catch-up runs.
  - No server-side date filter and no server-side count: completeness rests on the single 5 MB list response plus per-facility reads. A back-dated inspection for a facility whose lastInspectionDate did not change is invisible to the incremental path (needs periodic full re-reads).
  - Access from GitHub-hosted runners is unverified: the site is behind Cloudflare (Server: cloudflare, __cf_bm cookie). From this container all ~130 requests returned 200 without any challenge, but MHD's experience shows runner IPs can be treated differently; the relay allowlist (functions/api/relay.js) would need healthinspect.pima.gov added if so.
  - Terms of use / disclaimer text could not be read: the SPA's FAQ, Glossary and Privacy Policy text areas come from `siteinfotext*` endpoints that answer 401 anonymously (the SPA must obtain them another way). robots.txt redirects to /portal/robots.txt (not followed). Treat usage terms as unverified.
  - Undocumented internal API (Hedgerow Hedgehog portal v5.14.0.5969): field names and the sort-by-id workaround can change with a vendor upgrade; two facility-id formats coexist (synthetic 'f…-ffff-ffff-ffff-fffff…' and GUIDs), presumed stable but not verified across time.
  - 630 of 5,177 Food facilities are mobile units/push carts/vehicles whose siteAddress is a commissary or base; 237 facilities were never inspected (null lastInspectionDate); 14 addresses deviate from the 'City AZ zip' format; 4 lack coordinates. Ratings 'Educational' (273 facilities' newest), 'Pass' (226, enforcement re-inspections), 'Complaint Open/Closed', 'Observations/Site Visit', 'Emergency Response', 'IHH' need explicit handling or skipping.
  - Checklist item isCritical does not always match the citation's severity (a Priority Foundation citation on a non-critical item); observationComment once read literally 'No violation observed.' on an Out of Compliance item — use citation severity and tolerate noisy text.
  - Volume is modest (~125 inspections/week) but a full-history load is ~34k requests; the portal returned 200 to 20 back-to-back requests, yet its rate-limit policy is unknown — keep a delay and a breaker.
