## Lubbock, Texas — City of Lubbock Environmental Health Depart

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: City of Lubbock, TX (Census Bureau July 2024 estimate ~272,000; Lubbock County ~327,000). The portal is the City's own Environmental Health Department (2,288 of 2,323 facility addresses are in Lubbock); whether the department also inspects county towns (Wolfforth, Slaton, Shallowater, Abernathy appear only in a handful of mobile/temporary vendor addresses) is unverified. 2,323 food facilities: Food Risk 1-4 1,448, Mobile Food Unit 146, TF15 Temporary Food 729.
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. CLAUDE.md's Texas coverage is Austin, Houston, DFW and San Antonio (Amarillo only surveyed); Lubbock is ~300 miles from DFW and not in any existing fetch group. Same vendor product as the surveyed-but-unadded Tucson/Pima portal (healthinspect.pima.gov/Portal/, app 5.14.0.5969) — the two Hedgehog bundles expose identical data-service paths, so one `_hedgehog_*` helper would serve both (Pima's API itself was not exercised live: unverified).
- **Base URL**: https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/  (SPA at https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/#/..., apiUrlPrefix "/api/v0/portal" in main.c71df496e5ae69c8.js, app version 5.7.7.5014; the front end proxies the API to https://clhd.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/, which 404 bodies echo). Food disclosure program id: d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b; portal site id: 3fa21146-98d6-4 ...
- **Auth**: None for the public "disclosure" endpoints: no login, cookie, CSRF token or API key; plain GET/POST with a Chrome User-Agent and Accept: application/json answered 200 from this container. Admin/user endpoints (disclosure/program list, disclosure/*Settings, disclosure/fieldSettings/{id}, siteinfo/all, siteinfotext) answer 401 "Authorization has been denied for this request." and are not needed. No  ...
- **Date filter**: No server-side date filter. The filters array only applies to string columns (facility field settings mark nothing filterable; a {\"field\":\"lastInspectionDate\",\"matchMode\":\"gte\",\"values\":[\"2026-10-20\"]} filter was silently ignored — totalNumberOfRecords stayed 2323; verified). The cheap equivalent is server-side sort by lastInspectionDate desc and paging until a row's lastInspectionDate (ISO-8601 with -05:00/-06:00 Central offset, e.g. 2026-11-02T12:47:03.4620881-06:00; take the local date part) falls before the window start — verified sorted. inspectionDetails rows carry inspectionDate in the same format for the per-inspection cut.
- **Pagination**: POST disclosure/program/facilities: pageNumber (0-based) + pageSize in the body; response echoes pageNumber/pageSize and gives totalNumberOfRecords plus result[]. Multi-column server-side sort via sort:[{field,order:'asc'|'desc'}] (verified for lastInspectionDate desc and facilityName asc). pageSize 3000 was honoured (all 2,323 rows, 2.0 MB, 3.3 s) — no page-size cap observed up to 3000. The per-facility inspectionDetails and per-inspection inspectionResults endpoints return whole arrays, no paging.
- **score**: hazardScore — the official 0-100 score (post-April-2022 system), present on every inspection (45/45 in the week sample, 73-100) and on the facility row for its latest inspection; 65-100 observed overall. Use as original_score and risk_score (official-score source, like Wake).
- **grade**: facilities[].hazardRating A/B/C/F = official letter for the LATEST inspection; observed bounds A 90-100 (1,764), B 80-89 (228), C 70-79 (50), F 65-69 (2) — identical to DineScores' calculate_vetted_grade cutoffs. Per-inspection hazardRating is the literal 'INFO_REDACTED' (the SPA nulls it), so derive grades from hazardScore. hazardRatingColor e.g. 'FF008000'.
- **violations**: inspectionResults[] per inspection: description (item title, e.g. 'Proper Hot Holding Temperature (>135°F).'), result ('Corrected During Inspection' | 'Out of Compliance'), category.description (e.g. 'Time and Temperature for Food Safety'), cannedComments[].description (TFER §228.xx citation + rule text), cannedComments[].observationComment (free-text observation, never empty in 99 sampled lines), cannedComments[].locationComment. Suggested line text: item description + ' — ' + observationCommen ...
- **severity**: cannedComments[].severity: 'Priority' (37 of 99 sampled lines) -> priority, 'Priority Foundation' (19) -> priority_foundation, 'Core' (43) -> core. isCritical on the item is always false (unused).
- **coordinates**: facilities[].latitude / longitude — present on all 2,323 rows (0 nulls), WGS84 decimal (33.59, -101.88). No geocoding needed.
- **address**: facilities[].siteAddress — a single string 'STREET, CITY ST ZIP' (e.g. '2902 4th St, Lubbock TX 79415'); split on the last comma, then 'City ST 79xxx' into city/state/zip. 5 of 2,323 rows have an empty siteAddress (drop or geocode-free since coords exist). mailingAddress null. 2,288 rows are Lubbock; a handful list Wolfforth/Slaton/Abernathy/Shallowater/Amarillo business addresses (mobile/temporary vendors).
- **inspection_date**: inspectionDetails[].inspectionDate (ISO-8601 datetime with Central offset); facilities[].lastInspectionDate is the facility's newest. Verified: each of the 45 facilities with lastInspectionDate >= 2026-10-26 had exactly one inspection in that window.
- **facility_id**: facilities[].id — GUID, stable (0 duplicates among 2,323; the SPA routes on it). facilityNumber is always null. make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) stays the DineScores join key; 13 name+address duplicate pairs exist (shared id -> the one-inspection-per-day rule applies).
- **inspection_id**: inspectionDetails[].id — GUID, plus human inspectionNumber 'INSP-0NNNNN' (sequential, e.g. INSP-019817, INSP-021220). Use e.g. source_id 'lubbock_<inspectionNumber>' or the GUID.
- **inspection_type**: inspectionDetails[].inspectionType ('Routine Inspection' on all 56 facility histories sampled) and inspectionReason (' Compliance', note the leading space). Other types unverified (none seen).
- **Weekly volume**: Exactly 45 inspections dated 2026-10-26..2026-11-02 (7 days), all Routine/Compliance, one per facility (counted via inspectionDetails of every facility whose lastInspectionDate fell in the window). 174 facilities had their latest inspection in the last 30 days and 633 in the last 90 days, so expect ~45-60 inspections/week (~200-250/month), spiking during temporary-event seasons (729 of 2,323 facilities are 'TF15 Temporary Food' booths with a single inspection each). 2,044 facilities carry at least one published inspection.
- **Weekly requests**: ~100-150 requests: 1 POST facilities (pageSize 500, sorted lastInspectionDate desc; a second page only if >500 facilities fall in the window) + one GET inspectionDetails per facility with lastInspectionDate >= window start (~45-60 with the 1-day overlap; a lost week doubles it) + one GET inspectionResults per inspection in the window (~45-60). At 0.3-0.5 s spacing that is 1-2 minutes of fetch time. Measured latencies: list 1.5 s (50 rows) / 3.3 s (all rows); inspectionDetails 0.4-1.0 s; inspectionResults 0.7-2.6 s.
- **90-day backfill**: ~1,300-1,400 requests: 1 list POST + 633 inspectionDetails (facilities with lastInspectionDate >= 2026-08-04) + roughly 650-750 inspectionResults (one per inspection in the 90 days; each facility averages just over one). A full-history backfill (everything since Nov 2023) is ~1 + 2,044 inspectionDetails + ~5,000-7,500 inspectionResults (mean 3.6 inspections per sampled facility, biased toward permanent restaurants; temp booths have 1) — roughly 7,000-9,500 requests, ~1-1.5 hours at 0.5 s spacing.
- **History**: Published inspections start in November 2023: oldest inspectionDate seen is 2023-11-11 across 56 facility histories (the three facilities with the oldest lastInspectionDate are 2023-11 temporary booths; permanent Risk 3-4 restaurants show 3-8 routine inspections back to 2023-11/2024). Facilities by latest-inspection year: 2023: 10, 2024: 126, 2025: 372, 2026: 1,536. The disclaimer mentions pre-April-2022 demerit-system inspections, but none that old are served (likely the Hedgehog go-live cut-off; unverified). Inspection report PDFs are not exposed (document endpoints return null/[]).
- **Recommended strategy**: fetch_lubbock(since_date) as a new fetch group registered with _record_group (source 'Lubbock Environmental Health', API-class lookback cap FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_API_DAYS via _lookback_since). Step 1: POST /api/v0/portal/disclosure/program/facilities with {pageNumber:n, pageSize:500, criteria:'', sort:[{field:'lastInspectionDate',order:'desc'}], disclosureProgramId:'d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b', fields:[], filters:[]}; page while the last row's lastInspectionDate (local date part) >= since; stop at the first row older than since or at totalNumberOfRecords. Keep every row with lastInspectionDate >= since (skip null lastInspectionDate; optionally skip facilityType 'TF15 Temporary Food'). This yields the exact set of facilities that can have new inspections — the completeness check: if the list POST fails, raise/skip the whole group (ship nothing, status 'error'); if paging stops early (deadline), _set_group_status('skipped') and _hold_window_start. Step 2: for each kept facility GET .../disclosure/program/{P}/facility/{id}/inspectionDetails/ (0.3-0.5 s apart, 1-2 workers); keep inspections with inspectionDate >= since; each has hazardScore, inspectionNumber, inspectionType/Reason. On failure after one retry: drop and _hold_freshness(source, facility.lastInspectionDate, key=facility id). Step 3: for each kept inspection GET .../disclosure/program/inspectionResults/{inspectionId}; build violations [[category via classify_violation, severity map Priority->'priority', Priority Foundation->'priority_foundation', Core->'core', text = item description + ' - ' + observationComment (+ locationComment)]]; result 'Out of Compliance' vs 'Corrected During Inspection' can be appended. On failure after one retry: DROP the inspection (never ship as clean) and _hold_freshness(source, inspectionDate, key=inspectionNumber). Step 4: record = {name: facilityName, address/city/state/zip split from siteAddress, latitude/longitude from the row, inspection_date, original_score = risk_score = hazardScore (official 0-100; grade via calculate_vetted_grade, whose cutoffs match the county's A/B/C/F exactly), inspection_type, results: result, source_id 'lubbock_<inspectionNumber>', source_url the portal facility page}. Only shipped records advance freshness; deadline/breaker drops pass counted=False. Because the list tells the fetcher exactly which facilities and (via inspectionDetails) exactly which inspections fall in the window, a partial window is always known and reported, never silent. Weekly cost ~100-150 requests; a full first load ~7-9.5k requests (run once with --since-date 2023-11-01 and ship as a seed). The same helper parameterised by host and program id should cover Tucson/Pima (healthinspect.pima.gov/Portal/api/v0/portal/, unverified live).
- **Risks**:
  - Vendor-hosted portal (Hedgerow on Azure; hostname clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com, index last modified 2024-08-06). An app upgrade (Pima already runs 5.14 vs Lubbock's 5.7.7) or a hostname change could alter payloads; the API is reverse-engineered from the SPA bundle, not documented.
  - No server-side date filter: the incremental relies on sort by lastInspectionDate desc. A back-dated inspection entered for a facility that already has a NEWER inspection would not surface (its lastInspectionDate does not move); mitigated by re-reading facilities in the lookback window and by occasional full re-reads.
  - Temporary-event booths dominate the facility list (729 'TF15 Temporary Food' rows, one inspection each, often at event venues) and would flood the map; filter facilityType to Food Risk 1-4 and Mobile Food Unit (or keep TF15 but expect churn). 'Mobile Food Unit' names carry sales-tax suffixes like 'CE Tacos Aguilar  - TX 968-552K'.
  - criticalInfractionCount is the TOTAL item count (county labels it 'Total Violations'), and isCritical is always false; severity must come from cannedComments[].severity ('Priority'/'Priority Foundation'/'Core'). Per-inspection hazardRating is 'INFO_REDACTED' — derive the grade from hazardScore.
  - A failed inspectionResults call must drop the inspection (never ship a scored visit with zero violations) and _hold_freshness(key=inspectionNumber); the list endpoint gives the exact set expected, so every miss is detectable.
  - siteAddress is one unstructured string (5 rows empty); 13 name+address duplicate facility pairs share a DineScores id (same-day worst-of rule applies). Coordinates are complete, so geocoding is not needed.
  - Rate limiting not observed (≈140 requests incl. a 30-request/4-worker burst, all 200, no Retry-After/429), but no published policy or robots.txt exists; keep 0.3-0.5 s spacing and treat any 401/403/429/5xx as a transient drop with holds, not as clean.
  - Only 'Routine Inspection' / ' Compliance' seen in 56 facility histories; follow-ups or complaint inspections may exist with other labels (unverified) — do not filter on inspectionType.
  - History only from Nov 2023; pre-April-2022 demerit scores are not served, so no scale mixing was observed, but hazardScore semantics for any older rows that might appear later would differ (score 10 demerits ≈ 90).
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/startup?locale=en` (locale (optional)) SPA bootstrap: configuration (map center 33.572963,-101.859658), siteInfo (menu items -> program ids), disclosureConfiguration (programSettings, facilitySettings/inspectionSettings with the column field settings). Confirms the Food program and that per-inspection hazardRating is not published. [200 (54,957 B)]
  - `GET https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/siteInfo?locale=en` (locale) Menu items; the Food Inspection Results item (menuType 5) id d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b is the disclosure program id (Body Art cb488e24-..., Recreational Water 82d244ac-... are the other programs). [200 (10,996 B)]
  - `POST https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/disclosure/program/facilities` (JSON body {"pageNumber":0,"pageSize":N,"criteria":"<name search or empty>","sort":[{"field":"lastInspectionDate","order":"desc"}],"disclosureProgramId":"d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b","fields": ...) THE facility list: id (GUID), facilityName, siteAddress (one string: '2902 4th St, Lubbock TX 79415'), facilityType, lastInspectionDate (ISO, Central offset), hazardScore (latest official 0-100 score), hazardRating (A/B/C/F), latitude, longitude, closure. Server-side sort by lastInspectionDate desc  ... [200 on all 6 variants]
  - `GET https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/disclosure/program/d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b/facility/{facilityId}/inspectionDetails/` (path: program id, facility GUID (trailing slash as the SPA sends it)) All published inspections of one facility: id (GUID), inspectionNumber ('INSP-019817'), inspectionDate, inspectionType ('Routine Inspection'), inspectionReason (' Compliance'), hazardScore (0-100), criticalInfractionCount (= number of violation items; county labels it 'Total Violations'), nonCritica ... [200 x 87 calls (incl. 45 sequential at 0.3 s and a 30-request/4-worker burst in 4.2 s), 0.38-1.04 s each]
  - `GET https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/disclosure/program/inspectionResults/{inspectionId}` (path: inspection GUID (no program id)) Violations of one inspection: list of items with description (checklist item title), result ('Corrected During Inspection' | 'Out of Compliance'), isCritical (always false — unused), category.description, cannedComments[] each with description (TFER §228 citation + rule text), severity ('Priority' | ... [200 x 6 (0.7-2.6 s)]
  - `GET https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/disclosure/program/d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b/inspection/{inspectionId}/details/` (path: program id, inspection GUID) Single inspection row (same fields as inspectionDetails) plus actionsTakenList, showInspectionDisclaimer/inspectionDisclaimer HTML (pre-April-2022 demerit note), documentId. Not needed for the fetcher — the per-facility list already carries score/date/type. [200 (Cache-Control: no-cache; no rate-limit headers)]
  - `POST https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/disclosure/facilityDetails/d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b` (JSON body: array of facility GUIDs, e.g. ["24fdfeb2-ceaf-4604-ba3a-a4f6201728ff"]) Facility detail rows (same shape as the list plus riskRating 'Risk Category 1..4'). Optional. [200]
  - `GET https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/disclosure/facilityType/list/d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b  and  .../disclosure/program/detail/d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b  and  .../disclosure/hazardRating/list` (path: program id) Vocabulary checks: facility types (Food Risk 1-4, Mobile Food Unit, TF15 Temporary Food, School, Processing Risk 1-4); program detail {label 'Food Inspection Results', showMap true}; hazard ratings [A,B,C,F,Fail,High,Low,Moderate,N/A,Not rated,Pass]. No terms-of-use clause found. [200]

## Oklahoma City-County Health Department (OCCHD) — Inspect2Go 

- **Feasibility**: medium; reachable: True
- **Population**: Oklahoma County, OK: ~816k (Census QuickFacts July 1, 2024 estimate 816,490 per search snippet; 2020 census 796,563; not fetched directly). OCCHD states its jurisdiction as 'Oklahoma County/Oklahoma City' (occhd.org/food FAQ); PDFs seen carry cities Oklahoma City/OKC, Edmond, Midwest City, Choctaw. Parts of Oklahoma City outside Oklahoma County (Canadian, Cleveland, Pottawatomie counties) are inspected by the state (OSDH), not this portal.
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. CLAUDE.md's coverage list has no Oklahoma metro; grep of dinescores_pipeline.py for oklahoma/okc/occhd/inspect2go finds no fetcher (only street names inside public/data.js). Adjacent, unprobed: OSDH's statewide portal (https://www.phin.state.ok.us/inspections) for the rest of Oklahoma, and Tulsa Health Department for Tulsa.
- **Base URL**: https://occhd.inspect2go33.com
- **Auth**: None. Every JSON and PDF endpoint answered 200 with no cookie, token, API key or special header (tested with and without the antiforgery cookie; results identical). Only the HTML form POST /Public/Index carries an ASP.NET Core antiforgery token, and it is not needed for data access. Server: Microsoft-IIS/10.0 + ASP.NET Core on Plesk (x-powered-by-plesk: PleskWin); no WAF/CDN headers seen. robots.t ...
- **Date filter**: None. No endpoint takes a date (confirmed: CLAUDE.md's 'no date filter' note holds). Substitute verified live: food inspection ids are a dense, monotonically assigned sequence in upload order that tracks inspection date within about a day (id 113256 = 10/26/2026, 113300 = 10/27, 113400 = 10/28, 113500 = 10/29, 113600 = 10/30, max 113699 = 10/29 11:00 am; 31/31 ids in 113500..113530 exist; 14/15 random ids in 100007..113599 exist), so 'new since X' becomes 'ids above cursor X'. Caveat verified: the block 109974..110011 (>=38 consecutive ids) is missing, so a scan must probe ahead past a gap rather than stop at the first run of misses. Date format in JSON 'MM/DD/YYYY h:mm AM' (U+202F in newer rows); in the PDF header '10/26/2026' and '2:39PM'.
- **Pagination**: None anywhere. AutoComplete returns every match in one response (2,306 items for prefix '1'); GetAll returns an establishment's whole history in one response; the DataTables paging is client-side; guessed list-everything endpoints (/Public/GetAllInspections, GetRecentInspections, Search, Inspections, Export, /api/..., /swagger) all 404. There is no global inspection list: inspections are reachable only per establishment (GetAll/{estId}) or per sequential inspection id (the PDF endpoint).
- **score**: None. No numeric score anywhere (JSON or PDF). The header's 'Type: 45' (same on every PDF), 'Class' (seen F, C, R, B, S, E3) and 'Priority' (seen H-2, M-2, L-1, H-3, S-2) are establishment risk/frequency codes, meanings unverified. risk_score must be computed from violations (Omaha-style 100 - 5P - 2PF - C).
- **grade**: None. No letter grade; the portal image 'OKC_Risk_Level.jpg' is just the department logo. original_score would be None.
- **violations**: PDF page 1: 58 numbered items; cited ones carry a mark in the IN/OT/NA/No columns plus optional CDI (corrected during inspection) and R (repeat), e.g. '44 OT', '45 OT ... R', '16 IN'. PDF page 2 'Observations And Corrective Action': item number + free-text observation per OT item (e.g. '47  Rodent droppings on the shelf next to the dough maker...'; '53  Hole in the back door...'), then 'PPM Value', 'General Comments'. An inspection with no violations prints the page-2 table empty (Walmart 108324 ...
- **severity**: Not labelled per item. Derivable from the form's structure: items 1-35 are 'Priority & Priority Foundation Violations' (a '•' after the number marks the CDC risk-factor/intervention items: 2,4,5,6,8,9,10,11,17,18,19,20,21,23,24 — inferred from the column header '● CDC Risk Factors & Food Code Interventions', unverified against OSDH docs), items 36-58 are 'Core Violations'. Item titles are printed in full (e.g. '16 Insects, rodents, & other pests controlled'), so classify_violation can also run o ...
- **coordinates**: None in any endpoint or in the PDF. Must geocode (pipeline has geocode_census_batch at dinescores_pipeline.py:7661, geocode_address at :883, geocode_missing_coords at :7723); suffix-less street forms are a geocoding risk (unverified hit rate).
- **address**: PDF: 'Physical Address: 3618 N PENN   City: Oklahoma City   Zip code: 73112' (city spelling varies: 'Oklahoma City', 'OKLAHOMA CITY', 'OKC', 'Edmond', 'Midwest City', 'CHOCTAW'; streets are suffix-less, e.g. '3701 E 2', '2818 SW 29'). JSON: establishmentaddress / label 'NAME_ADDRESS' without city or zip. State is always OK (County# 55 = Oklahoma County).
- **inspection_date**: GetAll 'inspectionDate' ('10/26/2026 2:39 PM'); PDF header line under 'Inspection Date: Inspection Time:' ('10/26/2026  2:39PM'). Both verified equal for ids 113255, 110363, 108324.
- **facility_id**: PDF header 'Establishment#' == JSON estLic (license number, e.g. 59440; stable across years: est 1's 2018 and 2026 PDFs both print 97897). The portal's numeric establishment id (AutoComplete val / GetEstaBlishment id / GetAll estid, e.g. 447) is the lookup key. make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) inputs all come from the PDF (name 'PIZZA ZONE', 'Physical Address: 3618 N PENN', 'City: Oklahoma City').
- **inspection_id**: GetAll 'id' == the ?id= of GenerateFoodInspectionpdf (e.g. 113255); sequential integer, separate id space per form (child care ids 49..12403). Suggested source_id 'okc_<id>'.
- **inspection_type**: GetAll 'purpose' (Routine, Compliance, Complaint, Issue License Application; the form lists 8: 1-Routine 2-Compliance 3-Issue License Application 4-Complaint 5-Other 6-Out Of Business 7-Follow Up Activity 8-Reserved). In the PDF the selected purpose is NOT text: it is a drawn rectangle (~53x11 pt, pdfminer LTRect) around the chosen option (x=150 for '2-Compliance', x=114 for '1-Routine', x=288 for '4-Complaint'), so a text-only parser needs GetAll for it or must read the 're' operators. PDF also ...
- **Weekly volume**: ~300-360 food-form inspections per week (all purposes and classes). Basis: id 108324 = 07/16/2026, id 113699 = 10/29/2026 -> 5,375 ids in 105 days = 51/day = 358/week, minus an unknown share of missing ids (one >=38-id gap found; 1 of 15 random ids missing). That includes schools, convenience stores, cannabis retailers and hotels on the food form, not only restaurants (class filter TBD).
- **Weekly requests**: ~450-800 requests, ~240 MB: ~360 PDF GETs at 615-715 KB and 3.5-5 s each (4 workers ~1 PDF/s -> ~6-8 min), ~40-100 cheap miss probes (500, 7 KB, 0.3 s) incl. ~8 probe-ahead requests past the end, plus 0-360 GetAll/{estId}?formId=1 calls (1-3 KB) if purpose is taken from JSON instead of parsing the purpose rectangle. No catalog pass needed weekly (the PDF carries name, address, city, zip, license number).
- **90-day backfill**: ~4,600 ids (51/day x 90) -> ~4,300 PDF GETs (~2.8 GB) + ~300 misses + ~1,500 GetAll calls for purpose if wanted. ~75-90 min at 4 parallel workers (8-way parallel measured 1.1 PDF/s but per-request latency rose to 7 s; keep concurrency <= 4), ~5 h sequential. Full 8-year history (~113,700 PDFs, ~74 GB) is reachable but impractical; a 1-year backfill is ~18,700 PDFs / ~12 GB.
- **History**: Since mid-2018: food inspection ids start near 1 (id 1698 = 2018-08-22, PDF still renders), child-care id 49 = 2018-07-18; GetAll lists every inspection per establishment since then (est 447: 32 inspections 2018-10-16..2026-10-26). Newest inspection on 2026-11-02 (Monday) was dated 2026-10-30 (Friday), so publication lag is about one business day. Catalog: 6,658 food-form establishments via AutoComplete digit prefixes (ids 1..15,994 across all forms).
- **Recommended strategy**: Cursor-by-id scan of the PDF endpoint, never by date. (1) Persist 'okc_last_id' = highest food inspection id SHIPPED (in the group's entry of data/source_freshness.json, next to the existing 'latest'); first run seeds it by binary search between a known-good id and a 500 (as done here: 113699), minus ~90 days (~4,600 ids) for the backfill. (2) Each weekly run scans id = cursor+1 upward with <=4 workers: GET /Public/GenerateFoodInspectionpdf?id=N. 200 + '%PDF' -> parse with a new stdlib reader built on _oc_pdf_text_rows (rebuild word spacing from x gaps; read OT/CDI/R marks by item number on page 1 and the 'Item Number' + observation lines on page 2; require the header fields 'Inspection Date', 'Establishment#', 'Establishment:', 'Physical Address' AND the page-2 title 'Observations And Corrective Action' to be present, else the PDF is unread -> held, never shipped as zero violations). HTTP 500 whose body starts with the 'System.InvalidOperationException ... LINQ query parameter expression' + 'System.NullReferenceException' signature -> 'no such id' (a hole, like OMAHA_PERMANENT_500S matching on the body); any other status, body, timeout, HTML or non-PDF 200 -> transient: retry once, then _hold_freshness(source, date-if-known, key=N) and do NOT advance the cursor past N. (3) End of list: after 40 consecutive signature-500 misses, probe ahead at last_hit+64, +128, ..., +8192 (one request each, 7 KB); any 200 -> resume the linear scan from the first miss and record the skipped ids as holes with a per-id run counter (retry up to FRESHNESS_HOLD_GIVE_UP_RUNS=3 runs, then forget); none -> done, cursor = last 200 id. The verified >=38-id gap at 109974..110011 is why a plain 'N misses = end' rule must not be used alone. (4) Purpose: call GetAll/{estid}?formId=1 once per distinct establishment touched (estid is not in the PDF, but Establishment# == estLic; keep a small estLic->estid map refreshed from AutoComplete digit prefixes 0-9, 10 requests, when a license is unknown) or parse the purpose rectangle from the content stream; GetAll's id list is also a free completeness check (every id <= cursor for that establishment must be shipped, a known hole, or held). (5) Scoring: original_score None; risk_score = 100 - 5P - 2PF - C with P = OT items 1-35 marked '•' (CDC risk factors), PF = other OT items 1-35, C = OT items 36-58; violations [category, severity, 'item title: observation'] so Jev reads the observation text (named vermin -> F); R flag for repeats. (6) Completeness guarantee: because ids are dense and assigned in upload order, every id in (old cursor, new cursor] ends the run as shipped, a signature-500 hole, or held; a hold or a deadline/breaker cut (3 consecutive non-signature failures -> stop, like SNHD_BREAKER_TRIP) leaves the cursor at the last contiguous shipped id and reports _set_group_status(group, 'skipped'/'blocked', detail) so the run goes red; late uploads get higher ids so they are never skipped; _record_group registers the group and _n ...
- **Risks**:
  - Heavy detail fetch: every inspection is a server-rendered 615-715 KB PDF taking 3.5-5 s (7 s under 8-way parallel); ~240 MB/week and ~2.8 GB for a 90-day backfill; a single Plesk/IIS host with no CDN, so politeness matters and rate limiting over thousands of requests is unverified (none seen in ~250 requests over 20 minutes).
  - Id gaps: a verified block of >=38 consecutive missing ids (109974..110011) answers the same 500 as 'past the end'; a scanner that stops at the first run of misses would freeze the cursor or skip real inspections. Needs the probe-ahead and per-hole retry described in the strategy.
  - Misses are HTTP 500, not 404: 'no such id' must be recognized by the NullReference exception body signature; a transient 500 with a different body must be held, not treated as a hole.
  - No score or grade published: risk_score is wholly computed from OT marks; P vs PF is inferred from the '•' CDC risk-factor marker (unverified against OSDH documentation), so calibration against DineScores' A/B/C/F bands is untested.
  - Purpose/inspection type is not text in the PDF (a drawn rectangle around one of 8 printed options); needs GetAll per establishment or rectangle parsing; GetAll's inspectionDate uses U+202F before AM/PM in newer rows.
  - New stdlib PDF parser required: text is CID/ToUnicode glyph runs with no space glyphs (word spacing must be rebuilt from x positions); _oma_pdf_text_rows does not decode it; a wkhtmltopdf template change would silently shift positions — the header/title presence checks must gate shipping.
  - No coordinates; addresses are suffix-less ('3701 E 2', '2818 SW 29') and city spelling varies (OKC/OKLAHOMA CITY/Oklahoma City), so geocoding hit rate and id stability (make_restaurant_id uses city) need care; the stable facility key is Establishment# (estLic).
  - Food form mixes restaurants with schools (S/E3), convenience stores (C), cannabis retailers (R), hotels and grocery (Class codes seen but meanings unverified); a class filter or display rule is needed.
  - Catalog listing is only via AutoComplete substring prefixes (uncapped, 10 digit prefixes cover 6,658 establishments) and GetAll per establishment; there is no global inspection list and no date filter, so freshness tracking must be id-based, a new mechanism beside _lookback_since.
  - Behaviour from a GitHub-hosted runner IP is unverified (probe ran from the cloud container only); no robots.txt or terms of use found on the portal, but the vendor's terms were not checked.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET (and POST form-urlencoded Form=1&TempData[Form]=&EstName=&__RequestVerificationToken=) https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/Index` (Form: 1 Food Establishment, 2 Unattended Food Establishment, 3 Child Care, 4 Hotel) HTML search panel; the POST only sets hdndrpEmpList and injects the jQuery-UI autocomplete that calls /Public/AutoComplete; EstName is ignored server-side [GET 200 (8,353 B); POST 200 (10,085 B)]
  - `POST form-urlencoded https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/AutoComplete` (prefix=<substring of 'NAME_ADDRESS'>, Form=<1-4, ignored: Form=1 and Form=2 return byte-identical lists>) Establishment lookup: {"data":[{"label":"PIZZA ZONE_3618 N PENN","val":447}]} sorted by val; substring match (prefix 3701 matched addresses); NOT capped (prefix '1' -> 2,306 items, 136,676 B); union of prefixes 0-9 = 6,658 distinct establishments, ids 1..15,994; form-3 ids absent, so it appears to l ... [200 on all 26 calls (0.25-0.6 s)]
  - `GET https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/GetEstaBlishment` (Estid=<numeric establishment id> (a name string returns empty), formid=<ignored; the row carries its own formID>) One establishment row: {"data":[{"id":1,"establishmentname":"DAYLIGHT DONUTS","estLic":"97897","establishmentaddress":"3701 E 2","formID":1}]}; no city/zip/coords. Ids are sparse: random sample of 60 ids in 1..16000 gave 28 formID 1, 3 formID 3, 3 formID 4, 26 empty [200 on ~100 calls incl. 40 back-to-back in 15.7 s; empty {"data":[]} for unused ids]
  - `GET https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/GetAll/{estId}` (path {estId}; query formId=<must match the establishment's form: 1 for food; missing -> [] , wrong -> {"data":[]}>) Full inspection history of one establishment, oldest first: {"data":[{"estid":447,"id":113255,"inspectionDate":"10/26/2026 2:39 PM","purpose":"Compliance"}]}; purpose values seen: Routine, Compliance, Complaint, Issue License Application. Dates 'MM/DD/YYYY h:mm AM' (older rows 'hh:mm AM', newer rows ... [200 (1.3-2.8 KB, ~0.45 s)]
  - `GET https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/GenerateFoodInspectionpdf` (id=<food inspection id; sequential, 1..113699 as of 2026-11-02 20:13 UTC>) The inspection report itself: 4-page A4 PDF (pages 3-4 blank) rendered on demand by wkhtmltopdf 0.12.5 (Qt 4.8.7), 615-715 KB, 3.5-5 s each; the ONLY place with city, zip, class, OT/CDI/R item marks and violation text. Missing id -> HTTP 500 text/plain 7.1-7.2 KB whose body starts 'System.InvalidOpe ... [200 application/pdf for 60+ ids (1698 from 2018, 100007..113699); 500 for 113700+ and for the block 109974..110011 (>=38 consecutive ids) on all four generators; HEAD -> 405; 8 parallel downloads 7.3 s wall, no 429/403]
  - `GET https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/GetAllInspection` (Estid=<numeric id>, formid=<ignored>) Legacy DataTables feed: returns the establishment row repeated once per inspection (16 identical rows for est 1) with no inspection fields; only useful as an inspection count [200 (1,578 B)]
  - `GET https://occhd.inspect2go33.com/Public/GetData` (id=<establishment id>, formId=<1-4>) HTML 'Inspection Details' page with hidden ID/formId/EstName/EstAddress; its table is filled client-side by /Public/GetAll via /js/GetInspection.js [200 (6,712 B)]

## Snohomish County, WA (Snohomish County Health Department: Ev

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: Snohomish County, WA: 864,113 (Census QuickFacts July 1 2024 estimate; 2025 vintage 870,656), the state's third-largest county, directly north of King County. 3,090 permitted food facilities enumerated (restaurants, groceries/mini-marts, schools, mobile units, vending, catering endorsements): Everett 802, Lynnwood 497, Marysville 263, Snohomish 208, Edmonds 208, Bothell 202, Arlington 160, Monroe 152, Lake Stevens 123, Stanwood 96, Mukilteo 92, Mountlake Terrace 79, Mill Creek 54, Sultan 38, Gra ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None by design: Snohomish County is not in CLAUDE.md's covered list; King County (Seattle, via Socrata r878-4sxa) borders it to the south and Clark County WA is the only other WA coverage. Edge: 18 records carry other-county city names (Seattle 7, Kirkland 2, Issaquah 2, Tukwila 2, Kent 2, Puyallup 2, Bellevue 1, Renton 1, Poulsbo 1, Mount Vernon 1), and Bothell (202) / Woodinville (18) straddle the King County line (these are Snohomish-permitted units, e.g. the Bothell 98012/98021 side). Whethe ...
- **Base URL**: https://snohomishonline.envisionconnect.com (Accela/DECADE EnvisionConnect 'PressAgent' public portal; API prefix /api/pressAgentClient/, PressAgentOid=0c75a5fa-3183-4b7c-b28e-a72200ed49a9 taken from the ui-router state table inline in the root HTML; linked from https://www.snohd.org/229/Food-Safety-Complaints-Inspections-Closu as 'Restaurant Inspections' -> /#/pa1/search)
- **Auth**: None. No login, cookie, token or captcha on any API call; requests without a User-Agent and with 'Python-urllib/3.12' also answered 200. (The page loads Google's recaptcha_ajax.js for its payment/complaint forms; it is not enforced on these endpoints.)
- **Date filter**: None server-side (verified: StartDate/since/FromDate/ToDate/Top on inspections are ignored; the search DTO has only FacilityName/StreetName/City/Zip). Dates come only from activity_date ('YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00') in the inspections response, so 'new since X' must be filtered client-side after a per-program read. Cheap change signal (verified on a 123-facility stratified sample, 274 GETs): every inspection Oid and attachmentId is a SQL COMB GUID whose last 12 hex digits encode a datetime (4 hex = days since 1900-01-01, 8 hex = 1/300 s ticks; d382d258-...-b42500d2545f -> 2026-04-07 12:45:47 = the inspection's activity_date). The facility search row's attachmentId therefore dates the facility's newest report: equal to the newest activity_date in 103/123, 1-5 days after it in 20/123, never before ...
- **Pagination**: None. searchFacilities returns at most 50 rows with no offset/page parameter (any extra body key -> 500), sorted by street name, so a query at 50 rows is truncated and must be split. Verified adaptive starts-with splitting on FacilityName (split a prefix whenever it returns 50) over a 53-char alphabet: 2,226 POSTs, all 200, 348 s with 3 workers, 3,090 distinct facilities, 41 prefixes split (deepest 'CANTEEN @ '). A zip-first split sized on the enumerated data needs ~835 searches: 57 zips, 21 with >50 facilities, and no (zip, first letter) cell over 50. programs/facilityDetail/inspections retur ...
- **score**: inspections[].score = Washington violation points (float, 0 = clean, higher = worse; observed 0..95, never null; 1 violation -> 2-5 pts, 6 violations -> 47-95). Same scale as King County's inspection_score, so map exactly like fetch_seattle (dinescores_pipeline.py lines 1525-1650): original_score = points, risk_score = max(0, min(100, 100 - points)). Search/programs LastScore mirrors the newest inspection's score (122/123).
- **grade**: None published (the county states it leaves ratings to the reader); grade comes from calculate_vetted_grade on risk_score.
- **violations**: inspections[].violations[]: violation_code ('01'..'50', zero-padded string, all numeric), violation_description ('21 - Proper cold holding temperatures (5 points if 42F to 45F)'; strip the leading 'NN - ' as fetch_seattle does), violation_text (short form title), v_memo (inspector narrative + WAC citation + correction; median 660 chars, p90 1,355, max 3,124, never empty - this is the text Jev should read, cut to 500 like other fetchers). 38 distinct descriptions in 356 sampled violations; most c ...
- **severity**: By violation_code, as the portal's own detail template does: code < 28 -> red/critical ('likely to cause foodborne illnesses') = 'priority'; > 27 -> blue/non-critical = 'core'. Observed codes 01-26 and 29-50. violation_degree is null (289/356) or '1' (61) / '2' (6), only ever on red items; meaning unverified (possibly repeat count) - do not rely on it.
- **coordinates**: None anywhere in the API. Address + CityStateZip only -> geocode_missing_coords (Census batch, then Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run), so the initial 3,090-facility load will leave stragglers for later runs; the coalesce rules keep them once found.
- **address**: Address ('711 112TH ST B1 STE ', upper case, trailing space, suite tokens appended as 'B1 STE' / '105 STE' / '#A12'), CityStateZip ('EVERETT WA 98208-5283 ' - parse with ^(.*?)\s+([A-Z]{2})\s+(\d{5})(?:-\d{4})?\s*$; 3,088/3,090 parse, all WA, 33 city spellings incl. one 'LYNWOOD' typo and a few King/Pierce/Skagit cities).
- **inspection_date**: inspections[].activity_date, ISO 'YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00' (local date, no time). Newest first.
- **facility_id**: FacilityId ('FA0002518', stable, range FA0000062..FA0024911) plus ProgramId ('PR0002465') per permit; facility Oid GUID in facilityDetail/FacilityOid. make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) will merge a facility's programs (grocery deli/bakery counters) exactly as it does for Disneyland/groceries today; inspections are distinct per program (0 shared Oids across QFC #879's 6, Sprouts #456's 7 and Patty's Eggnest's 2 programs), so same-day program inspections fall under the existing _same_day_ran ...
- **inspection_id**: inspections[].Oid (GUID, unique; violations carry it as DailyOid). Suggested source_id 'snohomish_<Oid>'.
- **inspection_type**: inspections[].service: 'ROUTINE INSPECTION' (181/225 sampled), 'REINSPECTION-FOOD  (FEE CHARGED)' (20), 'REINSPECTION (NO FEE)' (17), 'ROUTINE INSPECTION - CHANGE OF OWNERSHIP' (7). PE_DESCRIPTION on the program gives the risk class (SEATING 0-50 - HIGH RISK, SEATING 0-151+ LOW RISK, MOBILE FOOD VEHICLE, SCHOOL KITCHEN, VENDING MACHINE, CATERING ENDORSEMENT...).
- **Weekly volume**: ~70-95 inspections/week. 3,090 facilities x 1.54 inspections per facility in the last 365 days (123-facility sample) = ~4,760/yr = ~92/week including ~20% reinspections and change-of-ownership visits; facilities whose newest report landed in each of the last 17 ISO weeks: 38-100 (mean ~69). Routine cadence is ~1-2 per facility per year (two per year for higher-risk classes; the 2017 launch article's 'twice a year' claim is only partly borne out).
- **Weekly requests**: Full-read weekly (recommended): ~835 (zip-first) to ~1,850 (name-prefix, 44-char alphabet) POST searches to enumerate facilities + ~3,800 GET inspections (one per program; 3,090 facilities x 1.23) + programs GETs only for new or changed facilities (~10-100) = ~4,700-5,700 requests, ~0.3 s each -> ~25-30 min single-threaded, ~9-10 min with 3 workers (the probe ran 8,700 requests at 3 concurrent with every one HTTP 200). Shortcut variant once the attachment-date signal is trusted: enumeration + programs/inspections only for facilities whose attachmentId date >= window start - 7 days, plus the 17 ...
- **90-day backfill**: Same as a first full read, because there is no date filter and the inspections endpoint returns each program's entire available history (~3 years, back to 2023-11): ~835-1,850 searches + ~3,090 programs GETs + ~3,800 inspections GETs = ~7,700-8,700 requests (~15 min at 3 workers at the observed 0.3 s latency). A 90-day backfill cannot be made cheaper than the full history; the whole history is the natural first load (use --since-date 2023-11-01 for the seed).
- **History**: ~3 years. Oldest activity_date observed 2023-11-03 across 123 sampled facilities (several at 2023-11-03/07/08); oldest attachment date among all 3,090 facilities 2023-12-21; 4.5 inspections per facility (median 4, max 21) in that span. The portal launched in Nov 2017 with records 'back to June 2016' (Food Safety News), so it evidently trims to a rolling ~3-year window - mechanism unverified.
- **Recommended strategy**: Model it on fetch_seattle (same WA point scale) with a search-then-poll design, registered as its own fetch group ('Snohomish County', source 'Snohomish County Health Department', state 'WA') through _record_group (dinescores_pipeline.py:8506) and the weekly api_since()/_lookback_since window (8392; the 400-day FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_API_DAYS cap is fine because reads are cheap and complete). Step 0: GET the root HTML and regex the live PressAgentOid from the state table ("pressAgentOid":"<guid>"), fallback to the constant. Step 1, enumerate facilities every run: POST searchFacilities per ZIP from a stored list of the 57 known ZIPs; any ZIP that returns 50 is split by first letter (A-Z, 0-9, and the productive punctuation ' & - . + @), and any (zip, prefix) that still returns 50 is split one character deeper (~835 searches; or run the pure name-prefix enumeration over A-Z/0-9 roots, ~1,850 searches, which needs no ZIP list - do this one at least monthly or whenever the ZIP enumeration's count drops >5% from the last recorded count, since a new ZIP is invisible to the ZIP list). Treat 'a leaf query still at 50 after the deepest split' or any search that is not 200 after 3 retries as an incomplete enumeration -> _set_group_status(group, 'error', detail) so the run goes red. Step 2: keep a committed FacilityId -> [ProgramId] cache (like data/king_county_address_map.json); call programs only for FacilityIds not in the cache or whose attachmentId changed (new permits), so a steady week costs ~10-100 programs GETs. Step 3: GET inspections for every known program (~3,800 GETs, 3 workers, 0.1-0.2 s pause, timeout 60 s, retry 5xx/timeouts 3x with backoff); ship every inspection with activity_date >= window start; map score -> original_score and risk_score = max(0, 100 - score), violation_code < 28 -> 'priority' else 'core', description = violation_description minus 'NN - ' + v_memo[:500], inspection_type = service, source_id = 'snohomish_<Oid>', address/city/zip parsed from Address/CityStateZip, then geocode_missing_coords. Completeness guarantee: the run has read everything iff every enumeration leaf returned <50 and every inspections GET returned 200; for each program whose GET failed after retries call _hold_window_start(source, window_start, key=ProgramId) so next week re-reads the same window (counted=False when the deadline or a breaker caused it), and if more than ~2% of programs failed or the deadline cut the loop, _set_group_status(group, 'skipped'/'blocked', detail) so freshness never advances past unread data. Record the enumerated facility count in the group detail so the report shows a shrinking enumeration. First load: --since-date 2023-11-01 reads the full ~3-year history (~8,000 requests) and seeds D1; the attachmentId COMB date can later prioritise stalest-first or skip facilities whose newest report predates window_start - 7 days (always polling the 176 null-attachment facilities), but only as an optimisation on top of a monthly full read, n ...
- **Risks**:
  - Search cap of 50 rows with no paging and no sort control: completeness depends on adaptive prefix splitting. A facility whose FacilityName exactly equals a prefix shared by >50 other names cannot be split out and could be missed (none observed; the independent StreetName enumeration found 2,570 facilities, all 2,570 present in the name enumeration).
  - No server-side date filter on any endpoint: every weekly run is a full read of every program (~3,800 GETs) plus a re-enumeration; cost grows linearly with the permit count and the run must be marked skipped/partial if the fetch deadline cuts it.
  - No coordinates in the source: all 3,090 facilities need geocoding (Census batch + Nominatim capped at 150/run), and the suite tokens in Address ('711 112TH ST B1 STE', '617 128TH ST 105 STE', '520 128TH ST #A12') need stripping or Census will miss them.
  - Undocumented, unofficial API inside an Accela EnvisionConnect portal: an Accela upgrade can rename /api/pressAgentClient/* or rotate the PressAgentOid (embedded in the root HTML's state table) - discover it at runtime and fail the group loudly on 404/500.
  - Rate limiting from GitHub-hosted runner IPs is unverified: this probe (8,700 requests, 3 concurrent, 0.1-0.15 s pauses, ~25 min) was answered 200 every time from the container's proxy egress, with no rate-limit headers, no WAF/Cloudflare signals and no robots.txt (302 to Error404.html).
  - Score semantics: WA violation points, not a 0-100 score; a 95-point inspection floors risk_score at 0, identical to King County's handling (risk = 100 - points) but the grade is a DineScores construct the county does not publish.
  - violation_degree (null / '1' / '2', red items only) is unexplained - must not drive severity; use violation_code < 28 as the portal's own template does.
  - The attachmentId COMB-date shortcut lags the inspection by 1-5 days in 16% of cases and is absent for 5.7% of facilities (176) that still get inspected, so it can only skip work with a margin and a periodic full read, never decide completeness.
  - The portal appears to keep only ~3 years of history (oldest 2023-11-03 seen despite a 2016 launch), so a later gap cannot be re-read from the source beyond that window.
  - Non-restaurant programs are mixed in (schools, vending machines, mobile units, catering endorsements; PE_DESCRIPTION tells them apart) and a few records carry King/Pierce/Skagit city names (Seattle 7, Bothell/Woodinville straddle the line) - possible duplicates against the King County feed, unverified.

## Anchorage, Alaska (Municipality of Anchorage Food Safety & S

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: Municipality of Anchorage, ~289,600 (Census Bureau July 1, 2024 estimate; 291,247 in the 2020 census; ~40% of Alaska) — Anchorage, Eagle River, Chugiak, Girdwood, JBER/Fort Richardson, Indian. 2,086 permitted facilities in the portal.
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores has no Alaska coverage (CLAUDE.md's ~40 metros and four states include nothing in AK); the Alaska DEC statewide program explicitly excludes Anchorage, so this portal is the only source for the municipality.
- **Base URL**: https://anchorageonline.envisionconnect.com (Azure App Service, CNAME azapp3.envisionconnect.com -> 4.236.239.140, IIS 10 / ASP.NET Web API, "Powered by: DECADE an Accela Company"). Food program PressAgentOid = da0e8f78-b3b3-4401-ab82-a84300964cf7 (ui-router state "pa1", activity "Inspection Reports"; "pa2" = 189c82ab-... is Noise Permits). Routes reverse-engineered from /bundles/app (EcPressAgent ...
- **Auth**: None: no login, cookie, CSRF token, API key or reCAPTCHA needed for the /api/pressAgentClient/* calls (the page loads a reCAPTCHA script, but it is not involved; every call worked from a bare curl/urllib with a Chrome User-Agent and an Accept: application/json header). The Accela Citizen Access portal the muni links as "Inspection Results" IS a login page, but it is not needed.
- **Date filter**: None (verified). The search form only knows FacilityName/StreetName/City/Zip; putting Begindate/Enddate (the Noise-permit portal's params) in the body makes the server 500, and a Begindate query param on /inspections is ignored (byte-identical 200 body). 'New since X' can only be computed client-side from each inspection's activity_date ('YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00'), which means every program's inspections call must be repeated each run.
- **Pagination**: None. searchFacilities returns the whole inventory (2,086 rows) in one 369 KB response; the UI pages client-side at 15 rows. programs and inspections return complete arrays per facility/program (inspections: min 1, max 9, mean 3.12 per program in a 60-facility sample).
- **score**: score (0-100 float, official; equals 100 - sum(points) in 174/187 sampled inspections; the 13 exceptions are 2022-2023 rows with score < 100 and an EMPTY violations list, i.e. lines not published). LastScore on search and programs rows equals the newest inspection's score (60/60).
- **grade**: None published: no letter grade or placard. The muni's own pass/fail or reinspection threshold was not found (unverified; FSS FAQ PDF has no scoring text). Inventory LastScore distribution: 1,940 >= 90, 143 in 80-89, 3 in 70-79, 0 below 70.
- **violations**: violations[] per inspection: violation_code ('0036'), violation_description (item title, e.g. 'INSECTS, RODENTS, ANIMALS NOT PRESENT'), v_memo (the AMC/Food Code citation text followed by the inspector's observation after a '***' marker; 184/216 memos carry the marker, 2 are empty), violation_text (always null), points (1-5). Same program + same day with 2 inspections occurs (6 of 187).
- **severity**: violation_degree: 'C' on every 2-5 point line (140 lines: points 2:62, 3:33, 4:14, 5:30) and 'N' on every 1-point line (75) — i.e. critical vs non-critical; one line had both fields null. points is the deduction.
- **coordinates**: None anywhere (search, programs, inspections, facilityDetail). Must geocode Address + CityStateZip (pipeline's geocode_missing_coords: Census batch then Nominatim capped at 150 per run) — 2,086 addresses on first load; addresses carry suite numbers ('8225 OLD SEWARD HWY #E ', '9220 LAKE OTIS PKWY 7 ').
- **address**: Address (street, trailing space, suite appended) + CityStateZip ('ANCHORAGE AK 99515 '; also EAGLE RIVER 133, GIRDWOOD 92, CHUGIAK 26, JBER/FT RICHARDSON 2, INDIAN 1; ~15 garbled rows such as 'ANCHORAGE S 99515', 'ANCHORAGE AK 9501', '(none)', ZIP+4 without hyphen '995034032'). ProgramIdentifier is a chain tag ('COSTCO ', 'SUBWAY', 'TACO BELL '), usually null.
- **inspection_date**: activity_date, 'YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00' (midnight, no time). datescore is a constant '2014-04-01' (meaning unknown, unverified).
- **facility_id**: FacilityId on the search rows ('FA0000271', stable, ids up to FA0020868); FacilityOid (GUID) on inspections / Oid on facilityDetail; the program needs ProgramId ('PR0000271', from /programs, stable but not derivable from the FacilityId).
- **inspection_id**: Oid (GUID) on each inspection (unique across the 187 sampled; every violation's DailyOid equals it); violations carry their own Oid.
- **inspection_type**: service (text) + servicecode: Regular/001 (110 of 187 sampled), Opening/006 (25), Complaint/003 (20), Change of Ownership/008 (9), Other/009 (7), Reinspection/005 (5), FOLLOW-UP/014 (1), For the Record/099 (1), Enforcement/011 (1). The portal shows the score pie only for Category '01' with servicecode 001 or 005.
- **Weekly volume**: ~36 inspections/week across all program types (~34/week food): 54 of 187 sampled inspections (60 random facilities) fell in the 365 days before 2026-11-02 -> 0.9 per facility-year x 2,086 facilities ~= 1,900/year. Inventory: 2,086 facilities, ~95% FOOD (POOL and QUASI-INSTITUTIONAL programs are mixed into the same list and must be filtered). The portal appears to post with a lag of several weeks: newest activity_date seen anywhere (80 facilities incl. the 20 highest FacilityIds) is 2026-10-05, and the sample holds 3 inspections for Sep-Oct 2026 vs 19 for Jul-Aug (unverified whether lag or seas ...
- **Weekly requests**: ~2,100 requests, ~9 minutes sequential at the observed 0.20-0.25 s per call: 1 POST searchFacilities (inventory) + 1 GET /inspections per known food program (~2,000; every call returns the program's whole history, so the window is filtered client-side) + 1 GET /programs per FacilityId not yet in the cached map (~10-20/week; the 20 highest ids all opened in Jul-Oct 2026). Bandwidth ~17 MB (8 KB per inspections response). No date filter, so there is no cheaper correct variant: LastScore on the inventory changes only when the score changes, and 115 of 187 sampled inspections scored 100, so an unc ...
- **90-day backfill**: ~4,200 requests (~17 minutes sequential): 1 inventory POST + 2,086 /programs GETs (to build the FacilityId -> ProgramId map, with POOL filtered out) + ~2,000 /inspections GETs. Because /inspections has no date filter and returns everything, a 90-day backfill costs exactly the same as the full ~5-year history (~6,500 inspections at 3.12 per program) — take the whole history in the first run.
- **History**: ~5 years: oldest activity_date in the 187-inspection sample is 2021-12-01; by year 2021:1, 2022:30, 2023:28, 2024:31, 2025:53, 2026:44 (sample counts). Violation lines are missing on some 2022-2023 rows (score < 100 with an empty violations list, 13 of 187). A third-party page claims the old portal went back to 2000 — not true of this one (unverified claim, contradicted by the sample).
- **Recommended strategy**: Fetch group 'Anchorage' (source 'anchorage'), stdlib urllib, one fetch_anchorage(since_date, limit) modelled on fetch_omaha: (1) POST searchFacilities with body {} once -> the 2,086-row inventory (name, Address, CityStateZip, LastScore). Treat a non-200, a non-list, or a row count below ~80% of the previous run's count as 'error' for the whole group (a truncated inventory must never read as closures). (2) Keep a committed sidecar map FacilityId -> {ProgramId, ProgramCategory} (like data/king_county_address_map.json); call /programs only for FacilityIds missing from it (~10-20/week; ~2,086 on the first run). Drop ProgramCategory 'POOL' (inspection Category '02'); keep 'FOOD' ('01') and 'QUASI-INSTITUTIONAL' ('07', 4 inspections in the sample — institutional kitchens; a policy call). (3) For every mapped food program GET /inspections (sequential or 2-3 workers with ~0.25 s spacing; 30 back-to-back calls drew no throttling, but be polite), stalest-first by the facility's stored latest date so a deadline cut never starves the same facilities twice. Keep inspections whose activity_date >= the window start from _lookback_since (the API-source 400-day cap is harmless: the response is the whole history either way) and that are not in --skip-known-inspections; dedupe on inspection Oid (source_id 'anc_<Oid>'); the pipeline's _same_day_rank handles the same-day pairs. (4) Completeness guarantee: the group is complete only when every program's call returned 200 and parsed as a list. A call that fails after 3 retries (5xx, timeout, reset) must hold with _hold_window_start('anchorage', window_start, key=FacilityId) because the unread facility's inspection dates are unknown; a deadline cut holds with counted=False and _set_group_status('Anchorage', 'skipped', ...); 3 consecutive failures trip a breaker that fails the rest at once and reports 'blocked'. Never ship a facility whose inspections call did not return 200, and never emit an inspection without its violations array (an empty array on a score < 100 is 'lines not published', not 'no violations': ship the score with no lines and let no text rule force anything). (5) Call _note_source_lag('Anchorage', newest activity_date seen) every run so the portal's multi-week posting lag reads as source lag in the freshness report rather than a broken fetch. (6) Records: original_score = score, risk_score = score (already a 100-minus-points scale like Wake/NC; do not re-score from tiers), inspection_type = service, results = '' (no grade published), state 'AK', city/zip parsed from CityStateZip with fallback 'Anchorage'/'AK' for the ~15 garbled rows, latitude/longitude None -> geocode_missing_coords (strip suite tokens like '#E' / trailing unit numbers before geocoding; the first load geocodes ~2,086 addresses, Census batch plus the 150-per-run Nominatim cap, so stragglers fill in over a few weeks and merges must keep coalescing coordinates). Violations: [classify_violation(title) category, severity from violation_degr ...
- **Risks**:
  - No server-side date filter or incremental endpoint: every weekly run must re-read all ~2,000 food programs' full histories (~2,100 requests, ~9 min, ~17 MB); a cheaper LastScore-diff pass is only a prioritisation hint, never a completeness guarantee (115/187 sampled inspections scored 100, so repeats are invisible).
  - Publication lag: newest inspection visible on 2026-11-02 was dated 2026-10-05 and Sep-Oct 2026 held ~1/4 of the summer volume in the sample — the freshness report will flag STALE (>21 days) unless _note_source_lag is used; unverified whether this is posting lag or a seasonal slowdown.
  - Undocumented, reverse-engineered API (Accela/Decade EnvisionConnect 'PressAgent'): the pressAgentOid, routes or the vendor platform can change without notice; the muni.org site already points 'Inspection Results' at an Accela Citizen Access login page, so a platform migration is plausible. The fetcher should fail loudly (group 'error') on a non-list response or a collapsed inventory count.
  - No coordinates: ~2,086 addresses must be geocoded on first load (Census batch + Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run); suite numbers in Address and ~15 garbled CityStateZip rows ('ANCHORAGE S 99515', '9501', '(none)', ZIP+4 without hyphen) need tolerant parsing.
  - POOL (Category '02') and QUASI-INSTITUTIONAL ('07') programs are mixed into the same inventory; ProgramCategory from /programs (or inspection Category) must be used to filter, which is why the per-facility programs call cannot be skipped (ProgramId is also not derivable from FacilityId).
  - Older rows (2022-2023) can carry a score < 100 with an empty violations list (13 of 187): an empty list must not be read as 'no violations'.
  - v_memo prepends long code-citation boilerplate (median 831 chars, max 4,275) that names 'insects, rodents, and other pests' on every pest item; feeding the whole memo to the regex/Jev could force false F's and overflow D1's 20 lines x 400 chars — ship the observation part after the '***' marker.
  - Reachability from GitHub-hosted runners is unverified (only this container was probed; Azure App Service host, no WAF headers seen, 30 undelayed requests all 200). Two 'connection reset by peer' events occurred in ~230 requests and retried fine; the agent proxy attributed them to its own tunnel, but the server side is unverified — the fetcher needs retries with backoff.
  - No terms of use or data licence were found on the portal (footer only says 'Powered by: DECADE an Accela Company'); unverified whether the muni restricts reuse.
  - No grade/placard and no published pass/fail threshold (unverified): grades will come purely from the pipeline's cutoffs on the official 0-100 score, which is fine but differs from what the county tells the public (it tells them nothing beyond the score).
  - Same-day duplicate inspections for one program (6 of 187) and several permits sharing one name+address (COSTCO, FRED MEYER counters) rely on the pipeline's one-per-restaurant-per-day rule.

- **Weekly volume**: ~242 inspections/week across all programs (970 unique in the 28 days 2026-10-05..11-01; 285 in the week of 2025-06-02; 247 in the first week served, 2024-11-04..08). Food program ≈ 84% (188/224 in the 7-day sample; 226/285 in June 2025) → ~200-230 Food inspections/week, Mon-Fri only (Sat 0-1, Sun 0, holiday Mondays 0). Max observed single day: 84 (2026-10-07); typical 35-70.
- **Weekly requests**: ~230-260 HTTP requests: ~25-30 search POSTs (8-9 day lookback via _lookback_since in 2-day windows, ~3 pages per 2 days) + ~200-230 detail GETs (Food rows only — the programs allowlist drops Schools/Body Art/Pools/Animal Venue in _mhd_row_rejection before the detail phase; ~75 KB each ≈ 16 MB). Well inside one ~250-page detail-ban window; the MHD_SEARCHES_PER_CONNECTION=150 rotation is not reached.
- **90-day backfill**: ~3,000-3,200 requests: ~270-300 search POSTs (45 two-day windows × ~6 pages; _mhd_rotate_connection reconnects at 150) + ~2,700-2,900 Food detail GETs (13 weeks × ~215). At the portal's ~250 detail pages per ~15-min ban window that is ~11 cooldowns ≈ 3 hours of detail phase — stage it with --until-date over several runs or let the 90-day lookback cap (FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_PORTAL_DAYS, line 8346) drain it oldest-first. Search-only backfill is NOT safe here (no score → every record would keep the neutral 70). Full history since 2024-11-04 would be ~22,000 detail pages.
- **History**: Inspections from 2024-11-04 onward (~24 months as of 2026-11-02). Verified: 2024-11-01/02/03 → 0 rows, 2024-11-04 → 49 rows (Food 46); 14 quarterly 7-day probes 2021-11-01 → 2024-10-28 and 13 monthly 7-day probes 2023-07-01 → 2024-06-25 all returned 0 rows (HTTP 200 each). Whether CCBH changed systems in Nov 2024 is unverified. Each permit page lists that establishment's inspections within that span.
- **Recommended strategy**: Config only — no new fetcher code. Add to MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS (line 222): 'ccbh': {'display_name': 'Cuyahoga County', 'default_city': 'Parma', 'state': 'OH', 'score_scale': 'none', 'metro': 'Cleveland', 'programs': ['Food'], 'window_days': 2}; add 'cuyahoga': ['ccbh'] to MHD_METRO_SLUG_GROUPS (line 297); add cuyahoga to --cities in .github/workflows/refresh-data.yml (line 98). It then runs inside the 'MHD metros' group (main() lines 9008-9070): weekly window = weekly_since(group, FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_PORTAL_DAYS=90, days=lag_days default 8) i.e. from the day before the group's recorded latest SHIPPED inspection (source label 'Cuyahoga County Health Dept'), capped at 90 days; the default 8-day lag is enough (CCBH publishes within 0-2 days). 'New since X' is found cheaply because filters.date is honoured: ~5 two-day windows × ~3 pages = ~15-30 searches/week; window_days 2 keeps every window under the 225 cap (max day 84, so a 2-day window peaks ~170) — the default 7-day window caps every normal week and would waste 9 pages per window before _mhd_fetch_window bisects. programs ['Food'] drops Schools (school-building inspections), Body Art, Pools and Animal Venue before the detail phase. Because score_scale is 'none', every Food row must get its detail page: _mhd_fetch_details_bulk(trust_official_score=False) recomputes risk from the parsed observations (existing parser branch works unchanged; empty list → 100). Never ships a partial window silently: (1) each window pages to a short page and a capped query is logged and bisected on calendar days — the only possible silent loss is a single DAY over 225, never within reach (84 max); treat that warning as a red flag; (2) an inspection whose detail page could not be read is dropped and held with _hold_freshness(source, date, key=inspectionID), so the recorded latest stops at the day before the earliest drop and the next run's _lookback_since re-reads it; deadline/ban/breaker cuts hold with counted=False and report 'skipped'/'blocked', which fails the run; (3) only shipped records advance the recorded latest. Optional refinements: an item-number → tier hint (01-37 risk factors vs 38-66 GRP) if classify_violation/Jev under-rates Ohio's standard code titles; and a tests/fixtures entry built from sample_search_ccbh_single_day_2026-10-29.json + sample_detail_page_speedway_4043_2026-11-02.html. Backfill: run 90 days locally/staged with --until-date (not search-only).
- **Risks**:
  - No official score or grade (score=0, scoreDisplay='' on every row): grades depend entirely on the detail pass + classify_violation/Jev, like Clark County WA and Yolo; a detail ban or deadline cut drops that week's Cuyahoga records (held and re-read next run) instead of shipping search-only rows.
  - No per-violation severity in the API/HTML: Ohio's C/NC 'Priority Level' exists only in the printable PDF, whose endpoint timed out twice (60 s; 504 after 126 s). Severity must come from text or the item-number convention (01-37 risk factors / 38-66 GRP, per-item C/NC unverified), so forced F's rely on Jev/regex reading of named vermin/sewage.
  - A 7-day window exceeds the 225 cap every normal week (~242-285 rows all programs): the survey's 'stays under the cap' is wrong at weekly granularity; use window_days ≤2 (max day 84) or accept the bisect's wasted pages.
  - No coordinates anywhere: every new facility needs geocoding (Census batch, Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run); a first backfill of ~3-4k distinct permits should run locally or across several runs. Census hit rate for these addresses is unverified.
  - Cleveland proper (372k) is excluded and not on this portal — the new metro has a hole in its centre until a Cleveland source is found.
  - History only from 2024-11-04 (~24 months); a full-history backfill is ~22,000 detail pages, 90 days ≈ 2,800 pages ≈ 11 detail-ban cooldowns ≈ 3 h — stage it.
  - Mixed programs: Schools, Body Art and Animal Venue pass _mhd_is_food_record, so the programs ['Food'] allowlist is mandatory; 'Food' still includes Non-Commercial FSOs (school cafeterias, nursing homes, churches) and Mobile FSOs (address may be the commissary) — filter permitType if those are unwanted on a restaurant map.
  - Production must go through the relay (GitHub runners are 403'd portal-wide); this probe went direct from the container, so relay + ccbh together is unverified (host allowlist already covers the portal).
  - filters.purpose is ignored by this jurisdiction (harmless for the plain date fetch; bucket mode would not work here if ever needed).
  - Same name+address across several permits (school cafeterias) and the 'N Royalton' city-spelling variant → a few split/duplicate restaurant ids; _same_day_rank covers same-day collisions.
  - The search row's `comments` field is an inspector narrative, not violations — must not be parsed as findings.
  - Jev load: ~200-230 inspections × ~3-5 lines/week ≈ 600-1,100 new lines/week; whether that fits the 20-minute Jev budget alongside the other sources is unverified.
  - Printable-PDF endpoint is unreliable (timeout/504), so an Orange-County-style PDF severity read is not an option today.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `POST https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/` (JSON body {"data":{"path":"ccbh","programName":"","filters":{"date":"YYYY-MM-DD to YYYY-MM-DD","purpose":""},"start":0,"count":25,"searchStr":"","lat":0,"lng":0,"sort":{}},"task":"searchInspections"}; ...) Inspection search: one row per inspection (establishmentName, addressLine1/2, city, state, zip, permitID, inspectionID, inspectionDate, purpose, programName, permitType, status, inspectionStatus, score, scoreDisplay, comments). Newest-first, hard cap 225 rows per query. [200 on all 163 search POSTs (6 slug-probe, 83 window/cap probes, 31 history, 43 floor/mid-2025); no 403/429 at 0.8 s spacing]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ccbh/inspection/?inspectionID=<GUID>` (inspectionID = search row's inspectionID (e.g. F78AC466-8D12-4B63-8FBC-B0076BF9415E); Accept text/html, Referer https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ccbh) Inspection detail page (72-76 KB): server-rendered <h3 class="observations-header">Observations &amp; Corrective Actions</h3><p class="observations-text"> with lines 'NN: 3717-1-xx.x(X)[(Repeat)] <code title>. **<observation/corrective action>' separated by double <br />, then 'Additional Comments'  ... No coordinates, no ISO dates. Not needed by the fetcher (the search rows already carry permitID). [200 (12,003 B)]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ccbh` (none) Jurisdiction landing page (9,878 B): confirms the slug/department name/address; the inspection list is loaded by JS from the POST above. No program list or score legend in the HTML; no terms-of-use link. [200]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ccbh/print/?task=getPrintable&path=ccbh&pKey=<inspectionID>,<inspectionID>` (pKey = inspectionID repeated (as linked from the detail page's 'View Original Inspection PDF')) County's official PDF report (Ohio form HEA 5302A/B, which carries a per-violation 'Priority Level C=CRITICAL NC=NON-CRITICAL' column plus COS/Repeat marks). 'New since X' is cheap because the date filter is honoured server-side and volume (~65 food rows/week) is far under the 225 cap, so one window never needs bisecting. Completeness guarantees already in the code: a window with any failed page returns None and stops the jurisdiction (stopped_early, nothing partial ships silently), len(rows) >= MHD_QUERY_CAP flags a capped window, any inspection whose detail page could not be read is dropped and _hold_freshness keeps 'Arapahoe County Health Dept' latest before that day so the next run re-reads it, a deadline cut lands in _MHD_DEADLINE_CUT and makes the group 'skipped' (red run), and _record_group advances the recorded latest only from shipped records. Two small additions worth making in _parse_mhd_record or a per-jurisdiction hook: drop rows whose addressLine1 starts with '^' / permitType starts with 'Mobile Unit' (no address, ungeocodable), and optionally skip InspectionType 'Pre-Operational'/'Opening' (score 0, no observations, would grade A on nothing). First run: a 90-day backfill with --since-date 2026-08-04 --cities arapahoe (stage with --until-date if the detail-page ban budget bites); do NOT do a search-only backfill (CLAUDE.md: the 100-2*demerit mapping paints false F's for CO counties). Optional later upgrade: read the county's P/Pf/C tiers from the printable PDF (needs a new stdlib decoder; 656 KB per inspection, ~40 MB/week) or approximate severity from the form item number (1-29 vs 30-57) when the text classifier is unsure.

  - Duplicate rows across pages (1 of 97 in one window) and a 'Centennial ' city with a trailing space: both already handled by the pipeline's inspectionID dedupe and .strip(), but any new code path must keep doing so.

Printables are not needed. - **Recommended strategy**: Add one MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS entry and reuse _fetch_mhd_jurisdiction_api unchanged: 'dchd': {'display_name': 'Douglas County CO', 'default_city': 'Castle Rock', 'state': 'CO', 'score_scale': 'demerit', 'metro': '', 'programs': ['Retail Food']} plus MHD_METRO_SLUG_GROUPS['douglas'] = ['dchd'] (and the slug in refresh-data.yml's --cities). display_name MUST carry the 'CO': _parse_mhd_record emits source = f'{display_name} Health Dept' (line 6569) and OMAHA_SOURCE = 'Douglas County Health Dept' (line 4711) is already Omaha NE's source string, present in jev_reader.SOURCE_TIER_SOURCES (exact match, line 1723/1736) and used as the key of _FRESHNESS_HOLDS in _hold_freshness (line 8468) — a bare 'Douglas County' would merge the two counties' freshness holds and apply Omaha's source-tier policy to Douglas CO. programs ['Retail Food'] is required: _mhd_is_food_record (line 6474) only drops pool/spa/septic rows, so 'Child Care' (107 of 456 rows in 91 days, preschools and school-age care) and 'Body Art' would otherwise ship as restaurants; 'Recreational Water' is dropped by the regex either way. Weekly incremental: weekly mode already starts the window the day before the recorded latest SHIPPED inspection (_lookback_since) in MHD_WINDOW_DAYS=7 windows; the portal's date filter is server-side and verified exact (0 of 456 rows outside their window; a single-day window returned only that day), publication is same-day (3 rows dated the run day appeared), so no lag_days is needed. Completeness per window is established the way _mhd_search_all_pages already does it: page at offsets 0,25,... until a page shorter than 25 rows; a window reaching MHD_QUERY_CAP (225) is bisected by _mhd_fetch_window — Douglas peaks at 51 rows/week and 16 rows/day, so the cap is never near. Every record then needs its detail page (score_scale 'demerit' → trust_official_score=False → risk recomputed from the observations; CLAUDE.md: the 100−2×demerit interim mapping is 9-27 points harsh for CO counties, so NO search-only backfill). Partial windows never ship silently: details run oldest-first, an unreadable detail page drops the record and _hold_freshness(source, date, key=inspectionID) pins the recorded latest before it, a deadline cut adds the group to _MHD_DEADLINE_CUT ('skipped') and a breaker trip reports 'blocked', all of which turn the run red via _record_group. Backfill: run --since-date 2022-09-01 in --until-date stages of ~90 days (≈23 searches + ≈313 detail pages per stage, 1-2 detail-ban cooldowns of 15 min each) with --skip-known-inspections so resumed stages do not respend detail pages; the 2022-09..~2024-01 rows carry inspectionType 'zDONOTUSE Retail Food - Archive' (programName still 'Retail Food', observations readable) — normalise that label to 'Retail Food' on the way in. Production traffic rides the existing relay: functions/api/relay.js ALLOWED_HOSTS already lists inspections.myhealthdepartment.com.
- **Risks**:
  - Source-name collision: display_name 'Douglas County' would produce source 'Douglas County Health Dept', which is OMAHA_SOURCE (pipeline line 4711) and a member of jev_reader.SOURCE_TIER_SOURCES (line 1677, exact-match) — use 'Douglas County CO' (source 'Douglas County CO Health Dept') or the two counties share freshness holds and Jev tier policy.
  - Program mix: without programs ['Retail Food'], _mhd_is_food_record keeps 'Child Care' (23% of rows; preschools, school-age care, purpose '') and 'Body Art' rows and they would ship as restaurants; 'Recreational Water' is dropped by the pool regex.
  - Demerit score with undisclosed per-item weights: scoreDisplay is '' on every row, score is 0..105 points (0 on 21% of Retail Food rows), printable legend PASS 0-49 / RE-INSPECTION REQUIRED 50-109 / CLOSED 110+; eight printables fit no constant P/Pf/C point formula (lone Pf item 48 = 5, Pf+C = 7, lone Core item 2 = 10, C+P+Pf+Pf = 15), so the official score cannot be trusted as a risk score and the interim 100−2×score mapping paints false F's (30 points → risk 40) until the detail pass recomputes ...
  - No coordinates anywhere (search rows, detail page, permit page, printable): every new facility goes through geocode_missing_coords (Census batch + Nominatim fallback cap 150/run); ZIPs are ZIP+4 ('80104-2803') and some Douglas addresses carry Englewood/Littleton/Aurora mailing cities, so geocoder quality on those should be spot-checked.
  - MHD portal limits apply (not re-measured for dchd): ~250 searches per TCP connection, detail-page bans after a few hundred per ~15 min, 403 to GitHub-hosted runners — production must use the relay; this probe (65 searches, 5 details, 3 permits, 9 printables from one IP) saw no 403/429.
  - Archive rows 2022-09-07..~2024-01 carry inspectionType 'zDONOTUSE Retail Food - Archive' (the landing template rewrites it to 'Retail Food' client-side); a backfill should normalise the stored inspection_type.
  - Severity is not tagged in the detail HTML (only the observation text with its Food Code citation, plus OUT/COS/Repeat chips in the 57-item checklist); P/Pf/C tags exist only in the ~360-575 KB printable PDF, which once answered HTTP 200 with the text 'error retrieving printable - error code g243qrfg34' — do not depend on it; let classify_violation/Jev read the text as for the other CO counties.
  - Volume is modest (24 Retail Food inspections/week average, 11-33 observed) so a quiet week is normal; the freshness report's 21-day STALE flag could trip over a holiday fortnight — unverified.
