Two small code changes: (a) thread `config.get('sort')` from _fetch_mhd_jurisdiction_api (6268) through _mhd_fetch_window (5945) / _mhd_search_all_pages (5873) into _mhd_search_page, which hardcodes `'sort': {}` at line 5816; (b) in _mhd_search_all_pages, when a sort is configured, treat a repeated inspectionID across pages or a GUID that breaks SQL Server uniqueidentifier order as a failed query (return None, as a 403 exhaustion does) so the window is re-read next run instead of shipping short. Weekly: 1-day windows (never multi-day — the portal returns one row per permit per window), 1-3 pages each, from `weekly_since(..., days=lag_days)` (main, ~9040) back to the day before the recorded latest shipped inspection (_lookback_since, 8392, capped at FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_PORTAL_DAYS=90); keep `programs: ['Food']` and drop purposes 'Consultation' and 'Pre-licensing' (no observations; a purpose-drop config key would be new), then the existing detail pass oldest-first with trust_official_score=False (score_scale 'none'), `--skip-known-inspections`, _hold_freshness on unread pages, _MHD_DEADLINE_CUT on a deadline, and _record_group so a blocked/cut run goes red. Completeness signals per window: id-sorted pages with zero repeated inspectionIDs and monotonic GUID order, a short final page, total < 225 (a 1-day window at the cap cannot bisect — log the 5978 warning and hold; ~60/day today), rows all dated inside the window; every searched inspection either read its detail page or was dropped+held. Known residual: two inspections of one permit on the same day collapse to the latest — rare (0 in 8 days); the permit page (/scph/permit/?permitID=) lists all of a permit's inspections if a spot check is ever wanted (e.g. only for Follow-Up rows, ~5/week). Geocoding: no coordinates in the source, so the records ride the existing geocode_missing_coords path (Census batch + Nominatim cap 150/run); expect a first-run coordinate backlog that heals over runs. Alternative needing no code change: bucket mode with search_terms ['a','e','i','o','u'], purposes [''], window_days 1 (a one-letter searchStr matched every permit of a day, 51/51), at ~5× the search cost. Backfill: run the same 1-day windows with --since-date 2024-08-30 in staged --until-date slices, ~250 detail pages per 15-min ban window.
- **Risks**:
  - Multi-day windows silently return one row per permit (its latest inspection in the window): 3 of 130 rows lost over 6 days, including a 10/30 Follow-Up sandwiched between two other inspections. A plain 7-day MHD_WINDOW_DAYS fetch would ship an incomplete week with no error. Mitigation: window_days 1 (same-day pairs on one permit still collapse; none observed).
  - Default page order is unstable: 7-day paging served 162 unique of 185 rows and missed ~10% that day-by-day paging found; a single day's pages overlapped 15/25. Needs sort by inspectionID (a small change at _mhd_search_page line 5816 where 'sort': {} is hardcoded) plus a repeat/monotonic guard, or the one-letter bucket mode.
  - No official score or grade (score 0/null, score block hidden): every grade depends on the scraped violation text and classify_violation/calc_risk_score, as for Clark County WA and Yolo; Ohio's 3717-1 rule-citation phrasing is new to the classifier and its tiering is unverified until a sample is graded.
  - Severity is not marked in the markup; the Ohio form's critical/non-critical item split is unverified (a web search found no authoritative key), so severity is text-derived only.
  - No coordinates anywhere: ~150 new addresses a week go through the Census batch geocoder with a 150/run Nominatim fallback cap; mobile units carry a base address.
  - Pre-licensing (~11/week) and Consultation (~7/week) rows are not inspections and have empty observation blocks; shipped unfiltered they would appear as violation-free A's and overwrite a real inspection's grade on the restaurant.
  - Posting lag unmeasured: rows dated 10/26 were still the newest view on 11/02 and one row was added during the probe; lag_days 14 is a guess — watch the freshness report's newest-inspection age for the first weeks.
  - Production fetches must go through the relay (functions/api/relay.js): this container got 200 everywhere, GitHub runners get 403 on this portal per CLAUDE.md; the host is already allowlisted, so no relay change is expected but it is unverified for this slug.
  - Detail pages ban at ~250 per 15-minute window: a normal week fits in one ban window but a 90-day backfill needs ~8 cooldowns (~2 hours); schools/pools/body-art rows share the feed and must be dropped by programs ['Food'] before the detail pass.
  - The portal's search API accepts ~250 searches per TCP connection (existing _mhd_rotate_connection handles it); 1-day windows raise the search count to ~15-40 per week, still far under that.
  - Record edits after posting: Rizzi's 10/28 record was briefly absent from an id-sorted pass and then present in single-day queries — explained by the per-permit collapse, but inspectors can amend reports; the 1-day overlap in _lookback_since re-reads only the latest day.
  - Terms of use: none seen on the portal pages; the department's site invites public viewing of reports (unverified beyond that).

## Toledo / Lucas County, Ohio — Toledo-Lucas County Health Dep

- **Feasibility**: low; reachable: True; blocked: Not blocked at the network level (about 90 API POSTs, 4 detail GETs, HTTP 200 throughout from this container, no captcha demanded of the API). Blocked on DATA: the jurisdiction publishes no score (score=0 on 227/228 rows, -1 once; scoreDisplay '' on all; detail page prints 'Score & Grade: 0'), no grade, and no violation text: jurisdiction metadata says displaySettings.showObservations=false so the detail page's observations-text block is empty, and the only violation source, the printable PDF (/oh_toledolucas/print/?task=getPrintable&path=oh_toledolucas&pKey=<id>,<id>), failed 4/4 attempts (HT ...
- **Population**: Lucas County, Ohio: 431,279 (2020 census; fetched from Wikipedia's page-summary API, which also calls it the state's sixth-most populous county). Rows name Toledo (54% of rows), Maumee, Sylvania, Holland, Whitehouse, Swanton, Oregon, Curtice, Grand Rapids, Sylvania Township, plus one Lambertville MI row. Programs on the slug: Food, Recreational Water, Body Art, Campground (keep programName 'Food' only).
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. public/data.js has 0 rows with "c":"Toledo"; its 493 Ohio rows are Cincinnati (491). No Toledo/Lucas fetcher in dinescores_pipeline.py. CLAUDE.md's candidate section lists Toledo as an MHD slug that 'honours the date filter and stays under the 225 cap' (line 246): both claims are contradicted by this probe (filter ignored; the cap is hit on every query).
- **Base URL**: https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/
- **Auth**: None. No token, cookie or captcha needed for the JSON API (the landing controller gates only the browser UI behind a sessionStorage captcha flag `captchaInspectionsLandingPageVerified`). Chrome-like UA plus the pipeline's `_mhd_session` headers (Content-Type application/json, Origin/Referer inspections.myhealthdepartment.com) were sent on every request.
- **Date filter**: NOT honoured (verified). filters.date in 'YYYY-MM-DD to YYYY-MM-DD' form (the controller's own format): '2026-10-26 to 2026-11-02', '2026-06-01 to 2026-06-01' and '2026-10-28 to 2026-10-28' all returned the same newest ~225 rows dated 2026-10-14..2026-11-02; searchStr queries ignore it too ('Burger King' with 2026-10-01..11-02 still returned a 2026-05-29 row). filters.purpose is ignored as well ('Follow-Up' = empty filters). The only server-side levers are sort (asc gives the 225 oldest rows) and searchStr (one latest row per matching permit). This contradicts CLAUDE.md line 246 ('Akron, Toledo — all honour the date filter and stay under the 225 cap').
- **Pagination**: `start` offsets 0,25,...,200 with `count` 25 (server-fixed page size); offset >= 225 answers {"err": true, "msg": "bad request"}: hard cap 225 rows per query. Pagination is UNSTABLE: each 9-page read of the newest 225 yielded 208-218 unique inspectionIDs (rows repeat across pages and others are skipped); two identical desc-sorted reads differed by 28 ids; the union of three unsorted reads was 228 unique. A fetcher must read the 9 pages several times and union by inspectionID.
- **score**: score: 0 on 227/228 rows, -1 on one; no real score. score_scale = 'none' (evidence: every row 0/'' and the detail page's 'Score & Grade: 0' with empty grade; Ohio's Uniform Food Safety Code inspections list critical/non-critical items without a numeric score; the form is 'FoodReportState2024'). Metadata showScore:true merely displays the zero.
- **grade**: scoreDisplay: '' on all 228 rows; detail page 'Grade:' empty. None.
- **violations**: NONE reachable: no field in the search row; detail page observations block empty (displaySettings.showObservations=false); printable PDF 504/timeouts 4/4. Only `comments` (inspector free-text narrative, filled on 118/228 rows: PIC name, follow-up notes, e.g. 'Due to the number of repeat violations and cleaning violations, a reinspection will occur unannounced') — not an itemised list.
- **severity**: None available (would be Ohio Critical / Non-critical markings inside the unreachable PDF; unverified since no PDF was obtained)
- **coordinates**: None (no lat/lng/latitude/longitude keys; pipeline would geocode). Address is geocodable: addressLine1 (+ addressLine2 on 5/228), city, state, zip
- **address**: addressLine1 '6903 Angola Road', addressLine2 (mostly null), city 'Holland'/'Toledo'/'Maumee'/'Sylvania'..., state 'OH' (one 'MI' row, Lambertville), zip '43528'
- **inspection_date**: inspectionDate, ISO midnight UTC '2026-11-02T00:00:00.000Z' (same-day publishing observed); parsed by sane_inspection_date in _parse_mhd_record
- **facility_id**: permitID (GUID, e.g. BE6B042A-8E09-457A-9801-22D7190E0472); establishment name in establishmentName/name
- **inspection_id**: inspectionID (GUID, e.g. B32165EE-7E93-4AE4-872C-FFC956BF63F4)
- **inspection_type**: purpose: 'Standard', 'Standard,CCP' (Critical Control Point), 'Follow-Up', 'Consultation', 'Complaint', 'Pre-licensing', 'Standard,30 Day', 'Standard,Process Review', 'CCP', 'Standard,CCP,Variance Review'. inspectionType key absent. Program: programName/program = 'Food' (213/228), 'Recreational Water' (11), 'Body Art' (3), 'Campground' (1); permitType = Ohio license class e.g. 'Commercial FSO < 25000 sq. ft. - Risk Level 4', 'Commercial RFE < 25000 sq. ft. - Risk Level 3', 'Vending', 'Micro-Mark ...
- **Weekly volume**: Food inspections: 71 (Mon 2026-10-26..Sun 11-01; 77 all programs) and 94 (10-19..10-25; 101 all programs) => ~70-95 food inspections/week, ~75-105 all programs. The newest-225 cap spans ~19 days (2.7 weeks) at this rate, so a weekly read sees the whole week plus ~1.5 weeks of overlap. 228 distinct permits in the 228-row union (no permit inspected twice in 19 days).
- **Weekly requests**: Search phase: 9 pages x 3 passes = 27 POSTs (unstable pagination: one pass captures ~92-96% of the 225; 3 passes unioned reached 228 unique). Detail pages: 0 (they carry nothing the row lacks). Printables: ~75-100 GETs/week IF the endpoint ever works — today each attempt consumed 95-126 s and returned nothing, so a 100-inspection week would cost ~3 hours of wall time at one worker even at the load balancer's edge. MHD_DELAY 0.8 s pacing was used throughout with no 403/429 in ~95 requests.
- **90-day backfill**: A 90-day inspection backfill is NOT possible through the search API: the date filter is ignored and the cap is 225, so only the newest ~19 days (27 POSTs for 3 unioned passes) are reachable. The alternative, one latest inspection per permit via zip-partitioned searchStr (~25-30 Lucas County zips x up to 9 pages = ~150-270 POSTs), is incomplete for any zip with >225 matching permits and still carries no violations. Plus one printable per inspection (~1,100-1,300 for 90 days at ~90/week) if that endpoint recovers.
- **History**: Portal data effectively starts late May 2026 (~5 months): asc-sorted whole-database read gave the 225 oldest rows as 2025-07 (1), 2025-11 (3), 2026-04 (1), 2026-05 (30), 2026-06 (180). But only three slices are reachable through the API: the newest 225 (~19 days: 2026-10-14..11-02), the oldest 225, and each permit's single latest inspection via searchStr. Everything in between is unreachable (date filter ignored, cap 225). Per-permit latest inspections could be enumerated with zip-code searchStr terms ('43615' matched >=25 permits) but a zip with >225 permits would be truncated.
- **Recommended strategy**: Do not add as an MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS entry: the date-window machinery (_mhd_fetch_window / bisection) assumes a working date filter and would re-read the same newest 225 for every window. If the printable PDF recovers (re-probe with ONE GET of /oh_toledolucas/print/?task=getPrintable&path=oh_toledolucas&pKey=<id>,<id> before any build), model it on fetch_orange_county (dinescores_pipeline.py ~6580-7230): (1) weekly, inside the Sunday job, read the empty-filter query (filters {date:'', purpose:''}, searchStr '', sort {}, lat/lng 0) for start 0..200, THREE passes, union by inspectionID, keep programName=='Food' (the daily poll OC needs is unnecessary here: 225 rows cover ~2.7 weeks at ~85/week, so a weekly read overlaps the previous one by ~1.5 weeks and a single missed week self-heals); (2) completeness test = the union's OLDEST inspectionDate must be strictly older than the previous run's recorded latest shipped date AND the three passes must agree to within a few ids — if the oldest row is newer than the recorded latest, a gap exists and the group must report partial via _set_group_status (never silently), and if the cap window ever shrinks below ~10 days (volume spike) switch to twice-weekly; (3) merge into a sidecar JSONL like OC_SIDECAR_PATH, ship only inspections whose printable parsed (Ohio critical/non-critical markings -> severity; score_scale 'none', risk from violations as for Clark WA/Yolo), hold unread ones with _hold_window_start(..., advance=True) keyed by inspectionID, give up per OC_PDF_GIVE_UP_TRIES/DAYS, and treat >50% unreadable printables as an error (OC_PDF_MASS_FAILURE_MIN); (4) ids: permitID is stable, but make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) stays the join key; coordinates come from geocoding (none in the feed); (5) put the row's `comments` narrative nowhere near the grade — it is not a violation list. Until the printable works, feasibility stays low: there is nothing to grade.
- **Risks**:
  - No violation text and no score reachable today: the printable PDF endpoint 504s/timeouts 4/4 (Toledo-specific; Orange County's works in 2.5 s), and detail-page observations are disabled by the jurisdiction (showObservations:false). Shipping would put every restaurant at the neutral placeholder grade.
  - Date filter ignored + hard 225 cap: no incremental 'since X' query exists, no history beyond the newest ~19 days, and no 90-day backfill; a week the job misses twice in a row is lost for good (unlike OC's ~1.7 days, a single missed week is still covered).
  - Unstable pagination: a single 9-page pass drops 3-8% of the 225 rows (208-218 unique; 28-id difference between identical reads), so a fetcher must union several passes and still cannot prove completeness except by overlap with the previous run's coverage.
  - Printable generation is a HealthSpace backend (services-print-api.hscloudsuite.com) timing out behind the portal's AWS load balancer (~120 s), so routing through functions/api/relay.js would not help and would tie up relay invocations for minutes each (unverified through the relay).
  - The portal's ~250-searches-per-connection quota and detail/printable bans (CLAUDE.md) apply; this probe stayed under them (~95 requests, all 200), so sustained rates are unverified.
  - CLAUDE.md line 246 misdescribes Toledo (date filter honoured, under the cap); other Ohio slugs in the same list (Akron 'scph', Cuyahoga 'ccbh') were not re-probed here and may share the ignored-filter/no-observations configuration — verify each before building.
  - Ohio form 'FoodReportState2024' severity markings (Critical / Non-critical) were never observed because no PDF was obtained; the parser design is unverified.
  - No coordinates in the feed: every new permit needs geocoding; the one Michigan row (Lambertville) and non-food programs (Recreational Water, Body Art, Campground) must be filtered out (programName 'Food' allowlist).

## Amarillo, TX — City of Amarillo Environmental Health Departm

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: ~265,000: the department states it serves all of Potter and Randall counties (Amarillo, Canyon, Timbercreek, Lake Tanglewood, Palisades, Bishop Hills). Potter County 114,649 (Census July 2024 estimate), Randall County ~152,400 (USAFacts 2025), Amarillo city 203,729 (Census 2024 estimate). Portal rows: ~95% city AMARILLO, ~5% CANYON.
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores has no Texas Panhandle coverage; the nearest covered metros are DFW (~350 mi) and Colorado Springs (~300 mi). Different health department from every existing Texas source.
- **Base URL**: https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/aaphd (search API: POST https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ with task 'searchInspections' and data.path 'aaphd'; amarillo.gov/environmental-health/inspection-results/ 302-redirects to https://myhealthdepartment.com/aaphd)
- **Auth**: None. Anonymous POST/GET; the server sets a connect.sid cookie but nothing required it (fresh sessions with no cookie got full results). No API key, no captcha, no login.
- **Date filter**: data.filters.date = "YYYY-MM-DD to YYYY-MM-DD" on inspectionDate, inclusive at both ends; a single day is "D to D". VERIFIED: single-day counts 2026-10-26..11-02 = 16, 19, 9, 17, 11, 0 (Sat), 0 (Sun), 11 = 83, equal to the stably-sorted 8-day window (83) and equal per day to the window's date distribution; a future window (2026-11-10 to 2026-11-17) returns []. filters.purpose is ignored ("Routine" returned the same 83 rows). Never send a non-empty programName (CLAUDE.md: the server hangs) — not re-tested here.
- **Pagination**: Offset paging: data.start = 0, 25, 50, ...; data.count is capped at 25 (count=100 still returned 25 rows); the last page is short, or an empty page follows an exact-25 page (2025-10-24: 25 then 0). No total count is returned. VERIFIED PROBLEM: with the pipeline's default "sort": {} the ordering is unstable across page requests (ties on inspectionDate) — the 8-day window returned 83 raw rows but 78-79 unique: 4-5 rows duplicated at the start=75 boundary and 4-5 rows of 2026-10-26 never served; week 2025-10-20..26 gave 110 unique vs 121 true (9% lost); a single busy day (33 rows) lost 4 once and ...
- **score**: score: integer. For GENERAL FOOD INSPECTION it is the official 0-100 score, higher better (63 Tier-3 food scores in one week: 71-100, clustered 94-100; PDF 'Score A/100 ... Total 0' = 100 minus demerits) -> pipeline score_scale '100'. OTHER FORMS USE OTHER SCALES: VENDED WATER/ICE INSPECTION 27-30 with 2 violations, FARMERS MARKET INSPECTION REPORT 17, RECREATIONAL WATER 261-316, GROUP CARE 38 — these must be excluded by inspectionType/programName or they paint false F's.
- **grade**: Not in the API (no scoreDisplay field) and the detail HTML's grade <strong> is empty; the letter exists only in the printable PDF ('A/100', 'Previous A/99'). Local scheme per the department's published roundups (secondhand, Amarillo Globe-News): 100 = zero demerits, A 90-100, B 80-89, C 70-79, F <=69, <=70 triggers re-inspection within 24 h — identical cutoffs to calculate_vetted_grade's A/B/C/F.
- **violations**: Not in the search API (only a free-text 'comments' field, non-empty on 58/78 rows). Detail page <p class="observations-text">: one line per violation separated by <br /><br />, shape '<item no>: <TFER/Food Code citation> <observation>' e.g. '38: 6-501.111(C) Several live flies observed in kitchen. Remove live flies. Correct by 11/06/2027', '13: 3-101.11 Molded food was observed in the walk-in cooler. Discarded.' Parsed unchanged by _mhd_parse_detail_html (22 and 23 lines on the two low-score sam ...
- **severity**: No explicit Priority/Priority Foundation/Core or points markup in the HTML or API; only the form item number (1-47 seen) and the code citation prefix each line. Severity would come from Jev's reading / the regex as for the other MHD sources (the official score is trusted since score_scale is '100': trust_official_score=True in _fetch_mhd_jurisdiction_api line 6440). An item-number-to-tier map for Amarillo's form was not found (unverified).
- **coordinates**: None anywhere (API rows have no lat/lng; detail and permit pages have no map). Every record needs geocoding from addressLine1 + addressLine2 + city + state + zip; addresses are clean street addresses (suite numbers split across addressLine1 'STE' / addressLine2 '100', joined by _parse_mhd_record to '3130 S Soncy Rd Spc 100').
- **address**: addressLine1 (street), addressLine2 (suite/space number or ''), city ('AMARILLO' ~95%, 'CANYON' ~5%, occasionally mixed case), state 'TX', zip (5-digit, 79015 for Canyon, 7910x/7911x/79124 for Amarillo). One name arrives with a leading space (' Paint Rock Coffee Co.'); the parser strips it.
- **inspection_date**: inspectionDate: 'YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00.000Z' (date only; sane_inspection_date handles it). 'time' is a time-only value on a 1899-12-30 epoch ('1899-12-30T14:30:00.000Z').
- **facility_id**: permitID (GUID, e.g. B9FE47C9-8106-42F0-BCC2-91573253E707); the PDF also prints a numeric 'Permit Number' (58298) that the API does not carry. Each permit = one location (the 4 'Teanergy Express' vended-water rows are 4 permits at 4 addresses).
- **inspection_id**: inspectionID (GUID, e.g. 15134A99-CBAB-4291-AA81-55B11350D676); used by the detail URL, the printable pKey and the permit page links. _parse_mhd_record stores it as source_id.
- **inspection_type**: inspectionType (GENERAL FOOD INSPECTION | VENDED WATER/ICE INSPECTION | FARMERS MARKET INSPECTION REPORT | RECREATIONAL WATER INSPECTION | PIWF_RECREATIONAL WATER INSPECTION | GROUP CARE INSPECTION (INSTITUTIONAL) | GROUP CARE INSPECTION 2026 (FOSTER/ADOPTION)); programName (Food Hygiene | Recreational Water | Group Care); permitType (food: 'F - Tier 0', 'F - Tier 0-NC1', 'F - Tier 1', 'F - Tier 2', 'F - Tier 3'; pools 'R - Annual/Seasonal Permit Fee'; day care 'G - Day Care Center'). purpose is ...
- **Weekly volume**: All programs: 41-121 inspections per week in six sampled weeks (2026-10-26..11-02: 83; 2026-10-19..25: >=97; 2026-09-14..20: 49; 2026-06-01..07: 79; 2025-10-20..26: 121; 2025-01-13..19: 41). GENERAL FOOD INSPECTION (the restaurant form): 36-98 per week, typically 65-85; roughly 3,500-4,500 per year. Weekdays only (0 on Sat/Sun); busiest single day seen 33. Published same day (11 rows dated the probe day).
- **Weekly requests**: About 75-100 requests: 7 single-day searches at 1-2 pages each (7-10 POSTs; or one 7-day window with a stable sort = 3-5 POSTs) plus one detail GET per kept GENERAL FOOD INSPECTION (65-85). No printable PDFs needed. Well inside the ~250-detail-pages-per-15-minute portal ban window.
- **90-day backfill**: Searches: ~65 weekdays x 1-2 pages = 70-100 POSTs with single-day windows (or ~13 seven-day windows x ~5 pages = ~65 with the stable sort). Detail pages: ~13 weeks x 70-85 = ~900-1,100 GETs. Total ~1,000-1,200 requests; at the portal's ~250-detail-pages-per-~15-min ban (shared with every other MHD jurisdiction behind the relay) that is 4-5 MHD_BAN_COOLDOWN waits, roughly 1-1.5 hours. A search-only backfill (~70-100 requests) is scale-safe here because the score is a real official 0-100 score (same class as Dallas/Plano in CLAUDE.md) but ships no violation text. Full history (2024-03-07 onward) ...
- **History**: First record 2024-03-07; 5 rows in March 2024, 17 in April 2024, full volume from May 2024. Zero rows for 2015, 2018, March 2020, June 2022, both halves of 2023 and Jan-Feb 2024 — about 2.7 years of history, nothing earlier on this portal.
- **Recommended strategy**: Add 'aaphd' to MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS as {'display_name': 'Amarillo', 'default_city': 'Amarillo', 'state': 'TX', 'score_scale': '100', 'metro': '', 'programs': ['Food Hygiene']} with a CLI slug group (e.g. 'amarillo') so it rides the existing MHD-metros group, _record_group, the relay (_PortalSession) and the detail pass unchanged. Two small machinery additions are required, both verified against live data: (1) a per-config 'sort' (recommend {'field': 'inspectionID', 'direction': 'asc'}) threaded _mhd_fetch_window -> _mhd_search_all_pages -> _mhd_search_page in place of the hard-coded 'sort': {} — the default order is unstable and silently drops 5-9% of rows in multi-page windows, whereas a unique-key sort gave complete, deterministic pages (83/83, identical across runs); (2) an 'inspection_types' allowlist (['GENERAL FOOD INSPECTION']) enforced in _mhd_row_rejection, because VENDED WATER/ICE (scores 27-30) and FARMERS MARKET (17) rows share programName 'Food Hygiene' and would grade F, and because _mhd_is_food_record keeps Recreational Water and Group Care rows (the programs allowlist handles those). Weekly incremental: the normal _lookback_since window (day before the latest shipped inspection, cap 90 days for scraped portals) in 7-day windows is fine (<=121 rows/week, far under the 225 cap; the bisect still guards growth), or window_days 1 so most days are a single page. Completeness guarantee: with the stable sort, page until a short page, and treat ANY duplicate inspectionID across a window's pages as 'ordering shifted' -> return None for that window (stopped_early + hold via the existing paths) instead of shipping; optionally cross-check the window's unique count against the sum of its single-day first-page counts (7 cheap POSTs) and hold the window on a mismatch. Keep the detail pass oldest-first with _hold_freshness on unreadable pages and trust_official_score (score_scale '100'); do not fetch the 441 KB printable PDFs. Backfill: search-only to 2024-03-07 is scale-safe (official 0-100 score), then details in --until-date stages across runs with --skip-known-inspections, as for Dallas.
- **Risks**:
  - Default-sort pagination silently loses rows: measured 83 raw/78-79 unique on an 8-day window (4-5 rows of 2026-10-26 never served) and 110 vs 121 true on a 2025 week; the pipeline's _mhd_search_page hard-codes sort {} so a fetcher that reuses it as-is ships an incomplete window every week with no error. The same mechanism may be affecting the existing MHD jurisdictions and is the likely cause of Tennessee's documented 'offsets 50/75 re-serve earlier pages' (unverified for them).
  - Non-100-point rows inside programName 'Food Hygiene' (VENDED WATER/ICE INSPECTION 27-30, FARMERS MARKET INSPECTION REPORT 17) would be graded F; pools (Recreational Water, scores 261-316) and day cares (Group Care, 38) pass _mhd_is_food_record — an inspectionType allowlist plus programs ['Food Hygiene'] are both mandatory.
  - No coordinates from the source: every one of ~70-85 weekly records and ~9-10k backfill records needs geocoding (addresses are clean, but the never-lose-coordinates merge rule and geocoder availability apply).
  - Portal 403s GitHub-hosted runners per CLAUDE.md; this probe only proves reachability from this container (every request 200). Production must go through functions/api/relay.js, which also means sharing the portal-wide ~250-detail-pages-per-15-min ban budget and the 240-minute fetch deadline with DFW, Portland, CO, Gwinnett, Tennessee and Orange County.
  - robots.txt ends with 'User-agent: *' / 'Disallow: /' and names AI crawlers (anthropic-ai, ClaudeBot, GPTBot, CCBot ...) explicitly; no terms-of-use page exists. It is the same portal every existing MHD source is already fetched from, so not a new condition, but it is the operator's stated preference.
  - No grade, severity tier or points in the API or HTML (grade only in the PDF); severity/chips/F-forcing depend on Jev/regex reading of the observation text, like the other MHD sources. Per-item demerit weights of Amarillo's form unverified.
  - API purpose fields are blank or inconsistent (filters.purpose ignored; detail page says Routine where the API says ''), so Routine vs Compliance re-inspection cannot be told apart reliably from the search rows; a Compliance re-inspection the same week carries its own score and _same_day_rank/latest-wins rules decide what shows.
  - History starts 2024-03-07 only (~2.7 years); the 225-row cap and the 'first page longer than 25' quirk were never exercised for this slug (unverified), and the detail-page ban threshold was not probed (only 4 detail GETs made).
  - Name title-casing quirk of _parse_mhd_record ('9Th Inning Brews & Bites') and mixed-case city values ('AMARILLO'/'Amarillo') are normalised by existing code; a leading space in one establishmentName is stripped — but make_restaurant_id depends on these normalisations staying stable.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `POST https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/` (JSON body exactly as _mhd_search_page (dinescores_pipeline.py:5784) sends it: {"data":{"path":"aaphd","programName":"","filters":{"date":"2026-10-26 to 2026-11-02","purpose":""},"start":0,"count":25," ...) Inspection search: one row per inspection with establishment name/address/city/state/zip, inspectionDate, score, inspectionType, permitType, programName, permitID, inspectionID, comments. Bare JSON list, [] when empty. [200 on every search (~110 POSTs, 7-day/8-day/single-day/monthly/yearly windows, 5 sort variants, count=100, purpose=Routine); no 403/429]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/aaphd/inspection/?inspectionID=<GUID>` (inspectionID = search row's inspectionID (GUID). HTML Accept header + Chrome UA.) Inspection detail page: 'Score & Grade: <strong>N</strong> <strong></strong>' (grade slot empty), <h3>Observations &amp; Corrective Actions</h3><p class="observations-text"> lines separated by <br /><br />, 'Additional Comments', link to the permit page and to the printable PDF. The repo's _mhd_pars ... [200 x4 (9-14 KB each, 0.3-4.5 s)]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/aaphd/permit/?permitID=<GUID>` (permitID = search row's permitID) Establishment page: name, address, most recent inspection, list of that permit's inspections (type | purpose | date | score, links to detail pages). No coordinates, no map. Useful only for per-permit history; not needed by the fetcher. [200 (9,326 B)]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/aaphd/print/?task=getPrintable&path=aaphd&pKey=<inspectionID>,<inspectionID>` (pKey = inspectionID twice, comma-separated (as the detail page links it)) Original inspection report PDF (4 pages, 441 KB): shows 'Score A/100', 'Previous A/99' + previous date/type, 'Total 0' (demerit total), permit number, risk category, temperature table. The only place the letter grade appears. Too heavy for routine fetching; the HTML detail page has the observations. [200, application/pdf, 441,267 B, 4.6 s]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/aaphd` (none) Landing page (program dropdown: Food Hygiene / Group Care / Recreational Water; department address 808 S Buchanan St; link to amarillo.gov/environmental-health). js/page-controllers/jurisdiction-landing/controller.js shows the payload shape and that sort.field/sort.direction come from a <select clas ... [200 (landing 9,111 B; controller.js 17,449 B; robots.txt 3,544 B)]

## Phoenix / Maricopa County, Arizona — Maricopa County Environ

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True
- **Population**: All of Maricopa County, AZ (Phoenix, Mesa, Chandler, Scottsdale, Glendale, Gilbert, Tempe, Peoria, Surprise, Goodyear, Avondale, Buckeye, Queen Creek, ...): ~4.69 million (Census July 2025 estimate 4,689,558 as cited by Wikipedia — unverified against census.gov directly). MCESD permits every food establishment in the county including the incorporated cities (permit addresses: Phoenix 9,299, Mesa 2,826, Scottsdale 1,956, Glendale 1,672, Tempe 1,437, Chandler 1,413, Gilbert 1,161, Peoria 862, ...) ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores has no Arizona source (CLAUDE.md's metro list and the 2026-10-10 survey contain nothing in AZ); this would be the largest metro added.
- **Base URL**: https://envapp.maricopa.gov (inspections, HTML + one JSON search endpoint); https://services.arcgis.com/ykpntM6e3tHvzKRJ/arcgis/rest/services/Restaurant_(External)/FeatureServer/0 (facility coordinates)
- **Auth**: None for /Search/Results, permit pages, inspection detail pages and the ArcGIS layer (no token, cookie, API key or special User-Agent needed; curl's default UA also got 200). The weekly report form alone requires an anti-forgery token plus a Google reCAPTCHA v2 answer (enforcement unverified; not submitted). A second ArcGIS service, Restaurant/FeatureServer/0, answers 499 'Token Required' and is n ...
- **Date filter**: No server-side date filter on any captcha-free endpoint: the fetcher filters client-side on the permit page's 'Inspection Date' column (M/D/YYYY, e.g. 10/19/2026) and dedupes on the inspection id. The captcha-gated weekly report filters by endDate=yyyy-mm-dd and returns the 7 days ending on it (date picker min = today minus 3 years, max = today) — unverified by submission. ArcGIS has no date fields at all. Note the inspection id's year suffix is NOT the inspection year (INSP-134230-2025 is dated 2/10/2026), so never derive dates from ids.
- **Pagination**: None on envapp: /Search/Results returns every permit in the zip in one JSON body (582 rows for 85004; DataTables serverSide:false) and a permit page lists every inspection in its ~3-year window (up to 11 rows seen). ArcGIS: standard resultOffset/resultRecordCount paging, 2000 per page (exceededTransferLimit flag), verified over zip 85004 (2,359 rows in 2 pages).
- **score**: None exists. The county publishes only a letter grade and violation counts; risk_score must be computed from the violation tiers (Priority / Priority Foundation / Core, source-stated in each violation's Comments text). The grade matrix on every page: A = P0/Pf0; B = P0/Pf1-4 or P1/Pf0-2; C = P0/Pf5, P1/Pf3-4, P2/Pf0-2; D = more than that or any legal action/suspension.
- **grade**: Permit page 'Grade' column and detail page 'Grade' block: A, B, C, D (D defined, not seen in 578 sampled rows), 'Cutting Edge A+', 'Not Participating' (voluntary grade card declined; counts still published, so the grade is computable from P/Pf counts), 'R' (re-inspection), blank (advisory). 578 rows sampled: A 226, Not Participating 194, blank 67, R 36, A+ 22, B 30, C 3.
- **violations**: Detail page div#Violations, repeated 'Violation #: N' (county item number), 'Description:' (item title, e.g. 'Proper Cooling Time and Temperatures'), 'Comments:' (code citation, tier, observation after 'Addl Notes:' (2024+) or '>>>' (legacy), 'Corrective Action:'/'Correct By:'). Zero violations = empty div#Violations on a 200 page that still shows the 'Inspection Status' block.
- **severity**: Embedded in each violation's Comments text: 2024+ wording ' - Priority:', ' - Priority Foundation:', ' - Core:' (Priority lines also prefixed 'PRIORITY VIOLATION-'); legacy wording 'PRIORITY VIOLATION-<code>, P:', 'Priority Foundation-<code>, Pf:', 'Core-<code>, C:'. The permit page and detail header also give the Priority count as a number.
- **coordinates**: Not on envapp. ArcGIS Restaurant_(External)/0 point geometry (outSR=4326 -> lon/lat), joined on LicenseNumber == permitId; 99.3% match for zip 85004 (4 of 582 unmatched: one FD- and three MF- mobile units). 1,734 ArcGIS rows have a blank Zip. Fallback: geocode businessAddressConcat.
- **address**: /Search/Results businessAddressConcat = '<street> <CITY> AZ <zip>' (one string; city is upper-case, occasionally mixed case 'Phoenix'); detail page 'Business Address' is the same string; ArcGIS has BusinessAddress, City, Zip as separate fields.
- **facility_id**: permitId / LicenseNumber: 'FD-00408' (16,572 of 25,828), 'FD2403569' (5,118), 'FD-24-01234' (1,588), 'MF2403509' (1,401), 'MF-10-0582' (1,149). Stable across the search, permit page, detail page and ArcGIS.
- **inspection_id**: 'INSP-NNNNNN-YYYY' for 2024+ records (379 of 462 sampled), legacy 'NNNNNNN-MCESD' before (83 of 462); from the permit page link and the detail page title. Suggested source_id: maricopa_{permitId}_{inspectionId}.
- **inspection_type**: Permit page 'Purpose' / detail 'Inspection Purpose': 'Food Routine Inspection' (74%), 'CI Food Routine Inspection' (5%), 'Food Advisory Inspection' (12%, no grade), 'Re-Inspection' (7%, grade 'R'), 'Food Review Inspection' (1%). Status: 'Completed', 'Re-inspection required', 'Re-Inspection Completed'. permitType from the search: 'Food Establishment - Eating & Drinking' (16,039), 'Retail Food' (2,791), 'Micro Market', 'Meat Market', 'Food Catering', 'Bakery', mobile 'MF Type 1/2/3', daycare, food ...
- **Weekly volume**: ~1,100 inspections/week across the 21,723 restaurant-like permits (all purposes; ~900/week routine). Basis: 60 sampled Eating/Retail/Bakery permits averaged 2.68 inspections in the last 365 days (0.92 in 90 d, 0.25 in 30 d), x 21,723 = ~58,000/year; press-reported county total 56,211 food inspections in 2023 (Phoenix New Times citing the MCESD annual report — unverified against the county document, whose URL returned 404). All 25,828 permits would be ~1,330/week.
- **Weekly requests**: ~23,000-27,000 HTTP requests: 158 zip searches (inventory, 11 s) + one permit page per restaurant-like permit (21,723; measured 29.8 req/s at 4 workers = ~12 min, or ~45-70 min if throttled to a polite 5-8 req/s) + one detail page per new inspection (~1,100-1,300 at ~3.2 s each = ~30 min with 2 workers) + one ArcGIS query per 2,000 facilities (~53 pages, or none if the coordinate cache is kept). Fits well inside the weekly job's 240-minute fetch deadline. Scanning all 25,828 permits adds ~4,100 permit pages.
- **90-day backfill**: ~42,000 requests: the same 158 searches + 21,723 permit pages (each page already lists the whole 3-year history, so a 90-day or even 3-year backfill needs no extra listing requests) + ~20,000 detail pages (0.92 inspections per permit in 90 days x 21,723). At the measured ~3.2 s per detail page that is ~9 h with 2 workers or ~4.5 h with 4 (4-worker detail throughput unverified), i.e. more than one weekly run's deadline: run details oldest-first with the freshness hold so it drains over 2-3 runs, or do it as a one-off local run. A full 3-year history would be ~58,000 detail pages.
- **History**: Rolling ~3 years on the permit pages (oldest row seen 11/2/2023 = 1,096 days before the probe; the weekly report's date picker also bottoms out at today minus 3 years). Pre-2024 inspection ids are the legacy 'NNNNNNN-MCESD' form with the older violation wording; a 2017-era id answers 'Inspection Not Available'.
- **Recommended strategy**: Three-phase scrape, captcha-free, with structural completeness checks. (1) INVENTORY: POST /Search/Results once per 5-digit zip (ArcGIS zip list, 158 today, unioned with a static Maricopa zip range 85001-85399 so an unlisted zip cannot hide permits); every response must be HTTP 200 with a JSON 'data' list, else the group is 'error' before anything ships (a missed zip would silently skip its permits); compare the permit count with the previous run's (store it in the freshness file) and go 'error' on a >5% drop; keep only restaurant-like permitTypes. (2) LISTING: GET /Permit/PermitResults/{permitId} for every inventory permit (throttle to ~5-8 req/s with 2-4 workers; measured 29.8 req/s without errors), parse the table rows, keep rows with Inspection Date >= window start where window start comes from _lookback_since with the 90-day scraped-portal cap; a page counts as read only if it is 200 AND contains the 'N Inspections Retrieved' marker (a 200 without it is a layout change or error page); retry a failure once, then record it: if any permit page stays unread call _hold_window_start(source, window_start, key=permitId) (counted=True; counted=False if the deadline cut the phase) and _set_group_status(group, 'partial', ...) so the run goes red and next week re-reads the same window. (3) DETAILS: for every listed inspection not already known (--skip-known-inspections / D1), GET /Inspection/{permitId}/{inspectionId} oldest-first; a read is valid only when the page carries the 'Inspection Status' block and div#Violations (empty div = genuinely zero violations, verified); any other outcome drops the record and calls _hold_freshness(source, date, key=inspectionId) so freshness can never advance past it (never record a blocked detail as clean). Map Priority->priority, Priority Foundation->priority_foundation, Core->core from the Comments text (both wording generations), carry the county letter grade (compute it from the P/Pf counts via the published matrix when 'Not Participating'), compute risk_score from the tiers like other no-score sources, and treat Maricopa as a source-stated-tier source (its text names the tier). Coordinates: one ArcGIS pass (dedupe on LicenseNumber, outSR=4326) cached across runs, geocode the <1% unmatched; coalesce lat/lng on merge as the invariants require. Register the group with _record_group so only shipped records advance the latest date. Optional cheap path: if a human verifies in a browser that /Report/WeeklyReport works without the captcha being enforced server-side (do not automate the captcha), one POST per week replaces phase 2 entirely; also worth a one-time public-records request for a bulk export, which the report page itself invites.
- **Risks**:
  - The only date-filtered listing (POST /Report/WeeklyReport, 7 days per request) sits behind Google reCAPTCHA v2 (sitekey 6LeouXEpAAAAAOaqx12JaCEa9H72oO3gUDzg_lLL); server-side enforcement is unverified because the form POST was not attempted, and it must not be automated around. The captcha-free path therefore re-reads every permit page weekly (~22k GETs).
  - Detail pages are slow (2.9-4.0 s each, measured on 11 pages) so a 90-day backfill (~20,000 pages) needs ~9 h at 2 workers — longer than one weekly run; drain it oldest-first over several runs with the freshness hold, or run it locally once.
  - Sustained-load behaviour beyond 200 permit pages at ~30 req/s and beyond 2 parallel detail pages is unverified; a 22k-page weekly scan could trigger a rate limit or a WAF rule that no probe saw. Throttle, and treat a non-200 or a page without the 'Inspections Retrieved' marker as unread.
  - GitHub-runner reachability is unverified (all probes ran from this container); envapp.maricopa.gov is not in the relay's ALLOWED_HOSTS (functions/api/relay.js), so a runner block would need a relay allowlist change.
  - HTML scraping of an ASP.NET app with no versioned API: a template change silently breaks parsing; the fetcher must fail closed on missing markers (header labels, div#Violations, 'N Inspections Retrieved') rather than ship zero-violation A's.
  - No numeric score: risk_score must be computed from violation tiers; the county letter grade is voluntary ('Not Participating' on ~34% of sampled rows) and must be reconstructed from P/Pf counts via the published matrix, so DineScores' grade will not always match a posted card.
  - Two generations of violation wording (2024+ ' - Priority:' / 'Addl Notes:' / 'Corrective Action:' vs legacy ', P:' / '>>>' / 'Correct By:') and the permit-id format changed in 2024 (FD2403569); a parser must handle both or lose the older third of the history.
  - Coordinates come from a separate ArcGIS layer whose rows are duplicated ~4x and whose refresh cadence is unverified (lastEditDate 2026-10-31); about 0.7% of permits (mostly MF- mobile units) have no point and need geocoding or dropping. Mobile units have no fixed location at all.
  - Inventory enumeration by zip relies on the ArcGIS zip list (158 zips, 1,734 ArcGIS rows have a blank zip); a permit whose envapp zip is outside the enumerated set is never discovered — union a static county zip range and watch the inventory count.
  - Several permits share a name and address (hotel outlets FD-00570/FD-00571 'Renaissance Phoenix Downtown', school cafeterias), so the existing one-inspection-per-restaurant-per-day worst-pick rule applies; mobile 'MF' permits and non-restaurant types (daycare, food bank, warehouse) should be filtered by permitType.
  - Press-reported volume (56,211 inspections in 2023) and population (4.69M) were not verified against primary documents; the weekly estimate rests on a 60-permit sample.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `POST https://envapp.maricopa.gov/Search/Results` (form-urlencoded DataTables request: licenseSearchParams[Zip]=85004 (5-digit zip alone is accepted); other keys the page sends: licenseSearchParams[BusinessName|AddressNum|StreetName|PreDirection|Stree ...) Permit inventory: returns EVERY permit in a zip in one JSON response (no paging). Fields: permitId, permitType, businessName, businessAddressConcat ('12223 N CAVE CREEK RD PHOENIX AZ 85022'), isCuttingEdgeParticipant, cuttingEdgeIcon; issuedDate/GUIDs/notes null. 158 zips -> 25,828 distinct permits  ... ## Roseville, California (City of Roseville Environmental Healt

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True; blocked: Not blocked: ~230 requests (searches, detail pages, permit page, config) all returned HTTP 200 from this container with no 403/429/captcha. Production still needs the relay because MHD 403s GitHub-hosted runners (CLAUDE.md), which this probe did not exercise.
- **Population**: All of Placer County, CA (Placer County Environmental Health is the county-wide food program; the City of Roseville runs none of its own): Census July 1, 2025 estimate 442,081 (figure from the web-search summary of census.gov/FRED — unverified directly). Cities seen in the rows: Roseville 158,494 (40% of rows), Rocklin 73,172 (16%), Lincoln 54,520 (14%), Auburn 13,286 (8%) — CA Dept. of Finance Jan 1, 2025 figures as quoted in a county transit document via web search, unverified directly — plus  ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. Placer County is not in CLAUDE.md's coverage list; it borders Sacramento County (DineScores has Sacramento) and sits near Yolo County (covered), so it extends the Sacramento metro rather than duplicating anything. 4 of 252 rows carry non-Placer mailing cities (Sacramento, Fair Oaks, Irving TX, Arlington VA) — probably mobile-food/caterer permit addresses (unverified); they would not collide with Sacramento County records because the source and permit authority differ, but they geocode outs ...
- **Base URL**: https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/
- **Auth**: None. No login, token, cookie or captcha; the search API is the same anonymous JSON POST the portal's own JS makes (jurisdiction config: enableCaptchaInspectionsLandingPage=false). Browser-like headers as in _mhd_session (dinescores_pipeline.py ~line 5725): CHROME_UA, Content-Type application/json, Accept application/json, text/plain, */*, Origin/Referer https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/ ...
- **Date filter**: Server-side, verified. Field `filters.date` as "YYYY-MM-DD to YYYY-MM-DD" (the frontend turns a single date into "D to D", landing controller lines 266-267); inclusive on both ends; matched against `inspectionDate` (ISO midnight Z). A single-day query returned only that date (2026-10-28: 17/17) and every window read stayed inside its window (all_dates_in_window true on every test). Unlike the 'orange-county' slug, pchd honours it. Rows are published the same day (2026-11-02 rows visible on 2026-11-02); weekends return []. `filters.purpose` is also server-side and case-insensitive.
- **Pagination**: Server pages are fixed at 25 rows regardless of `count` (count=100 -> 25, count=10 -> 25); `start` is a plain row offset (start=10 returned the remaining 18 of 28); the 225-row query cap (MHD_QUERY_CAP) is never approached (max 107 rows per week, 28 per day). Two independent defects, both verified: (1) UNSORTED multi-day reads page over an unstable tie order on inspectionDate — the 7-day read returned 93-96 unique of 107 with 10 rows re-served across pages, and two identical reads differed on pages 3-4; `sort:{"field":"inspectionID","direction":"asc"}` makes pages pairwise disjoint (0 cross-pa ...
- **score**: `score` = 0 on 248 of 252 rows and null on 4; no `scoreDisplay` key; detail page prints "Score & Grade: 0"; config displaySettings.showScore=false and scoreDisplaySettings={}. score_scale = 'none' (no numeric score exists).
- **grade**: `InspectionOutcome`: "Green Placard" (215/252), "Yellow Placard" (2), "" (35 — all Pool/Body Art rows plus ~18 Retail Food rows, e.g. a no-prep gas-station market; why those lack a placard is unverified). "Red Placard" not observed in 252 rows (the county's placard ordinance implies it exists — unverified).
- **violations**: Not in the search API. Only on the detail-page HTML: <p class="observations-text"> items separated by <br /><br />, each "NN: <CalCode section list> <observation + corrective action>", e.g. "8: 114000 Four Hour Rule: Observed (phf) stored at room temperature ..."; _mhd_parse_detail_html already extracts them (4/2/0 on the samples; a clean page yields [] not None). The search row's `comments` is the "Additional Comments" paragraph (inspector summary, 205/252 non-empty), not violations.
- **severity**: None published: no Major/Minor/critical marker in the API rows or the detail HTML (the only 'min' hits were CSS). Severity would come from classify_violation / Jev as for Yolo, Clark WA and Orange County; the CalCode item number (1-56) at the start of each line could serve as a hint (which items are 'major risk factor' items is unverified). The printable PDF may carry MAJ/MIN tags like Orange County's — unverified.
- **coordinates**: None in any response (has_lat_lng = 0 of 252); geocode from address via geocode_missing_coords (line 7723, Census batch + Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run), which main() already applies to MHD records (line 9055).
- **address**: `addressLine1` ("30 N Main St"), `addressLine2` ("" or null), `city` ("Colfax"; mixed case, occasionally upper-case), `state` ("CA" on 250/252), `zip` ("95713").
- **inspection_date**: `inspectionDate` ISO string "2026-11-02T00:00:00.000Z" (handled by sane_inspection_date); `time` carries the clock time as "1970-01-01T11:15:00.000Z".
- **facility_id**: `permitID` (GUID, stable across inspections; the permit page lists its history). `permitName` also embeds the county permit number, e.g. "THE WRECKING CREW BAKERY AND CAFE - RESTAURANT: 0 - 49 SEATS - PR0013294". DineScores id would be make_restaurant_id(establishmentName, addressLine1+addressLine2, city) as _parse_mhd_record (line 6484) already does.
- **inspection_id**: `inspectionID` (GUID), e.g. 23FEE9F7-94D0-4E5F-8E56-E345749364B7; also the detail-page key.
- **inspection_type**: `inspectionType`: "Retail Food Facility" (231/252), "Mobile Food Facilities" (4), "Recreational Water" (11), "Body Art Inspection" (6); `purpose`: Routine (232) / Follow-up (18) / Complaint (2); `permitType`: facility class ("Restaurant: 0 - 49 Seats", "Market - With Food Prep < 5000 Sq Ft", "School Cafeteria - Satellite", "Mobile Food Facility - Full Prep", ...); `programName`: "Retail Food" (235) / "Pool" (11) / "Body Art " (6, trailing space).
- **Weekly volume**: 77-107 inspections per week across all programs (union of per-day reads: 2026-10-12..18 = 77, 10-19..25 = 100, 10-26..11-02 = 107, 2026-01-05..11 = 104); Retail Food (the food program) 67-103 per week, i.e. ~85-95 typical, 9-28 per weekday (max seen 28 on 2026-01-06), zero on weekends. Follow-ups ~7% and Complaints ~1% of rows. Only 2 Yellow placards in 252 rows.
- **Weekly requests**: With the recommended recipe (one-day windows x {unfiltered, Routine, Follow-up, Complaint}, sorted by inspectionID): ~8 days (lookback day + 7) x 4 = ~32 searches, plus a second page only when a day's Routine query fills 25 rows (0-3 per week) -> ~32-40 searches; plus one detail page per Retail Food inspection: ~85-105 -> ~120-150 HTTP requests per weekly run, all via the relay. (A naive 7-day window would cost ~5 searches but silently lose 2-8% of rows — see risks.)
- **90-day backfill**: ~90 one-day windows x 4 queries = ~360 searches (weekends answer [] instantly) + ~13 weeks x ~90 Retail Food detail pages = ~1,150-1,250 detail pages -> ~1,500-1,650 requests. Detail pages are the binding constraint: at ~250 per 15-minute ban window (MHD_BAN_COOLDOWN=900 s) that is ~5 cooldowns, ~75 minutes of waiting — fits one run's 240-minute fetch deadline only if the other MHD jurisdictions leave room, so stage it with --since-date/--until-date over 2-3 runs. Full history (2025-11-24 to date, ~11 months) would be ~1,000 searches + ~4,300 detail pages.
- **History**: Starts late November 2025: 0 rows for 2024-06-03..09 and 2025-06-02..08; first rows dated 2025-11-24/25 (4 rows in 2025-11-17..30); 72 rows in 2025-12-01..07; 82-107 per week since. Matches the county's note that reports dated 2025-12-01 and later are on this portal (earlier reports sit in the county's previous database, not probed). So ~11 months of history, far more than a 90-day backfill needs; the permit page shows each facility's inspections within that span.
- **Recommended strategy**: Add `pchd` to MHD_METRO_JURISDICTIONS (dinescores_pipeline.py:235) and a slug group (MHD_METRO_SLUG_GROUPS, line 297, e.g. 'placer': ['pchd']), then add the slug to the `--cities` list in .github/workflows/refresh-data.yml:98 so it rides the existing 'MHD metros' group (main() loop at lines 9008-9073: fetch_dfw -> _fetch_mhd_jurisdiction_api, geocode_missing_coords at 9055, _record_group at 9070, relay transport, ban cooldowns, deadline). Suggested entry: {'display_name': 'Placer County', 'default_city': 'Roseville', 'state': 'CA', 'score_scale': 'none', 'metro': 'Sacramento' (product decision; '' otherwise), 'programs': ['Retail Food'], 'window_days': 1, 'purposes': ['', 'Routine', 'Follow-up', 'Complaint'], 'sort': {'field': 'inspectionID', 'direction': 'asc'}} — source label "Placer County Health Dept". Two small code changes are REQUIRED before the generic path is safe: (1) _mhd_search_page hard-codes `'sort': {}` — thread a per-config sort dict through _mhd_search_all_pages/_mhd_fetch_window so pages are disjoint; (2) `purposes` is only honoured in bucket mode (`bucket_mode = bool(search_terms)` in _fetch_mhd_jurisdiction_api) — add a non-bucket purpose split: for each ONE-DAY window run the unfiltered query plus one query per purpose and union rows by inspectionID (the unfiltered read catches any purpose outside the list, the purpose reads un-collapse Routine+Follow-up pairs on one permit). Completeness invariant per day, so a partial day never ships silently: with the unique-key sort, pages must be pairwise disjoint by inspectionID and the last page shorter than 25; a cross-page duplicate or an `{"err":true}` answer marks the day incomplete -> _hold_window_start(source, day, counted=False) (line 8486) + _set_group_status(group, 'error', ...) (line 8501) so the run goes red and the next lookback re-reads it; also log any row whose purpose is outside the configured list. Weekly window: the existing _lookback_since (line 8392; cap FRESHNESS_LOOKBACK_PORTAL_DAYS=90, overlap 1 day) from the day before the latest SHIPPED inspection; lag_days default 8 is fine (same-day publishing). Details: _mhd_fetch_details_bulk oldest-first with trust_official_score=False (score_scale 'none' => risk recomputed from observations; a clean page is [] => risk 100); unreadable pages are dropped and hold freshness as today. Record enrichment: store InspectionOutcome (Green/Yellow/Red placard) in `results` and consider a Yellow/Red placard as a grade cap (policy); strip the leading "NN: 113947, 113947.1, ..." CalCode citation prefix from each observation before classify_violation/Jev (keep the item number as a hint) so the 400/500-character caps hold real text; keep state=='CA' rows only. Backfill: --since-date 2025-11-24 staged with --until-date over 2-3 runs (~4 searches/day + ~90 detail pages/week), using --skip-known-inspections on resumption.
- **Risks**:
  - ONE ROW PER PERMIT PER QUERY (verified): any window read returns only a permit's newest inspection in that window, so a plain 7-day _mhd_fetch_window drops exactly the Routine inspections that earned a Follow-up — 8 of 104 in 2026-01-05..11, 2 of 107 in 2026-10-26..11-02 — i.e. the violation-bearing, Yellow-placard inspections. Must split every one-day window by filters.purpose (Routine / Follow-up / Complaint) and union. Same-day Routine+Follow-up pairs collapse even inside a single day (seen t ...
  - The same collapse is plausibly present on every other MHD slug the pipeline reads with plain 7-day windows (DFW, Portland, CO Front Range, Utah County, Yolo, Gwinnett) — UNVERIFIED here; a one-week day-union-vs-week-read audit per slug would settle it.
  - Unsorted pagination is unstable (10-13% loss on multi-day windows, rows re-served across pages); needs sort {field: inspectionID, direction: asc} — a new payload field _mhd_search_page does not send today — and one-day windows.
  - A wrong or renamed slug answers HTTP 200 {"err":true,"msg":"bad request"}, which _mhd_search_page turns into [] (log.debug, 'non-list'), so a slug change would read as an EMPTY week and only surface as STALE after 21 days; treat the error dict as an error.
  - No numeric score (score 0/null everywhere; showScore=false): grades depend entirely on scraped observation text, so every inspection costs a detail page (~90/week) and shares the portal's detail-ban budget (~250 pages per 15-min window) and the 240-minute deadline with 20+ existing MHD jurisdictions.
  - No Major/Minor severity in the API or HTML; severity comes from the regex/Jev reading only (the printable PDF might carry tags — unverified).
  - Observation lines open with long CalCode section lists (item 35: ~230 characters of section numbers) that eat into the 500-character parser cap and D1's 400-character cap and may mislead classification; strip the prefix before classify_violation/Jev.
  - No coordinates: ~90 inspections/week must geocode through geocode_missing_coords (Census batch, Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run); failures leave records without lat/lng until a later run.
  - programName 'Body Art ' (trailing space) passes _mhd_is_food_record (no pool/spa/septic word) — a programs: ['Retail Food'] allowlist is required; Pool rows (11/252) are already dropped.
  - 14% of rows (35/252) have an empty InspectionOutcome, including ~18 Retail Food rows (no-prep markets?) — the placard semantics for those are unverified; do not map '' to a grade.
  - 4 of 252 rows carry out-of-county/out-of-state mailing cities (Sacramento, Fair Oaks, Irving TX, Arlington VA) — likely mobile/caterer permits (unverified); filter state=='CA' and expect a few far-away geocodes.
  - History begins 2025-11-24; nothing older is on this portal (earlier reports live in the county's retired system), so facilities inspected only before December 2025 will appear with no history.
  - The portal's own directory page mislabels Placer under slug /ffhd (an Ohio jurisdiction); never derive slugs from that directory.
  - GitHub-hosted runners are 403'd by MHD (CLAUDE.md); this probe ran from the cloud container, not a runner — production must use the relay, so relay health is a dependency for this source too.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `POST https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/` (JSON body exactly as _mhd_search_page (dinescores_pipeline.py:5784) sends it: {"data":{"path":"pchd","programName":"","filters":{"date":"2026-10-26 to 2026-11-02","purpose":""},"start":0,"count":25,"s ...) Inspection search. Returns a JSON list (<=25 rows) of inspections in the date window — but ONE ROW PER PERMIT (its newest inspection in the window), so Routine inspections followed by a Follow-up in the same window are hidden unless the query is split by purpose. A wrong slug answers HTTP 200 {"err" ... [200 on every one of ~200 searches (lists of 0-25 rows); 200 + {"err":true,"msg":"bad request"} for the 21 wrong slugs and the nonsense slug]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/pchd/inspection/?inspectionID=<GUID>` (inspectionID = the search row's inspectionID; Referer https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/pchd; same URL pattern _mhd_fetch_inspection_detail (line 6025) builds) Inspection detail page; the only place with violation text. Server-rendered <h3 class="observations-header">Observations &amp; Corrective Actions</h3><p class="observations-text"> with items separated by <br /><br />, each "NN: <CalCode section list> <observation>"; the pipeline's _mhd_parse_detail_ ... [200 (11,111 / 9,483 / 9,077 bytes)]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/pchd/permit/?permitID=<GUID>` (permitID from a search row) Per-permit inspection history (date, purpose, placard, link to each inspection) — the path the search results' establishment links use; useful to confirm history depth per facility (sample permit: 2026-01-12 Green, 2026-05-26 Green, 2026-11-02 Yellow). [200 (10,030 bytes)]
  - `POST https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/` ({"task":"readOne","dataObjectName":"jurisdictions","data":{"path":"pchd"}} (the bundle's app.exo.readOne)) Jurisdiction config: titles.full "Placer County Environmental Health ", displaySettings.showScore=false, displaySettings.showObservations=false (list view only), scoreDisplaySettings={}, inspectionsByProgram=false, searchTemplate="", office 11434 B Ave Suite 400 Auburn CA 95603, links.default https: ... [200 (2,348 bytes)]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/pchd` (none) Jurisdiction landing page; <title>Home | Placer County Environmental Health | My Health Department</title> confirms the slug (an unknown slug returns the generic 337,031-byte directory page with HTTP 200 instead). Filter controls: #filterdate, #filterpurpose with options "", Routine, Follow-Up, Comp ... [200]
  - `GET https://inspections.myhealthdepartment.com/pchd/print/?task=getPrintable&path=pchd&pKey=<inspectionID>,<inspectionID>` (link as printed in the detail page) Printable inspection PDF (Orange County's violations live only in this; for pchd the HTML already carries the observations). Might carry MAJOR/MINOR flags the HTML lacks — unverified, not fetched. [not fetched (unverified)]

A token needs Google's script run in a browser; a stdlib Python job or the Cloudflare relay cannot mint one, and the probe that would have tested whether the server enforces the token (POST with ...

- **Base URL**: https://portal.kda.ks.gov/Food/FoodInspectionSearch
- **Auth**: No login. Current portal: ASP.NET anti-forgery pair (cookie __RequestVerificationToken_KdaPortal + hidden field __RequestVerificationToken, both issued by GET /Food/FoodInspectionSearch) on every POST, PLUS a Google reCAPTCHA v3 token (form field recaptchaToken, site key 6LeZTUUrAAAAAJdsOpK9HEH5NZOCKrjLV9Ia8yZW) on _Search, _MapLocations and Export. GET _Counties and GET Results need nothing; _Vio ...
- **Date filter**: Current portal: form fields InspectionDateFrom / InspectionDateTo, Kendo datepicker format MM/dd/yyyy; server-side behaviour UNVERIFIED (gated). Legacy portal: ctl00$MainContent$dteInspectionBeginDate$txtDate / ctl00$MainContent$dteInspectionEndDate$txtDate (MM/DD/YYYY) VERIFIED server-side: 07/01/2026-07/31/2026 statewide returned 43 establishments whose top-row inspection dates were all 07/01-07/02/2026; 06/22/2026-06/28/2026 returned 342 with top rows inside the window; 10/20-11/02/2026 returned none. Semantics: one row per ESTABLISHMENT whose inspection falls in the range, the matching inspection on the top row and every other inspection in the nested "Past Inspection(s)" grid (so a week count under-counts re-inspections of the same place).
- **Pagination**: Current portal _Search: hidden input name="page" (id SearchPage) re-posted with the whole form; the returned HTML carries pager links .fis-page[data-page]; page size and total-count markup UNVERIFIED (gated). _MapLocations JSON is capped at 250 pins (page text: "showing the 250 most recently inspected"). - **score**: NONE. KDA publishes no numeric score. The report has a 'SCORE SUMMARY' block that is a Risk Factor / Good Retail Practice item-count matrix (its content for the sample was not inspected — unverified), not a 0-100 score. Risk must be computed from the P/Pf/core violations.
- **grade**: NONE. Only a compliance flag: report 'Follow-Up Required Yes/No'; JSON FollowUpRequired (true = 'Out of Compliance' badge); legacy 'In'/'Out' column (IsOverallComplianceIn).
- **violations**: Report page (GET Results?inspectionGuid=): <table class="data-table"> under <div class="section-bar"> 'Risk Factors' and 'Good Retail Practices'; columns Item (td.col-item, 1-55 incl. 45A/45B), Code Section (td.col-code, e.g. 3-501.16(B)), Priority (td.col-priority > span.priority-tag), Comment = Food Code text followed by <div class="violation-comment">Inspector Comment: <strong>observation, often ending 'COS - ...'</strong></div>. Also a 55-item checklist (td.item-num / td.item-desc / td.item- ...
- **severity**: span.priority-tag text: 'P' = Priority, 'Pf' = Priority foundation (both seen); core items presumably blank/'Core' (unverified — the sample had only P/Pf rows). Legend on the report: 'P=Priority Violation, Pf=Priority foundation violation'. Maps 1:1 to DineScores priority / priority_foundation / core.
- **coordinates**: Current portal: ONLY in the captcha-gated _MapLocations JSON (pin.Latitude / pin.Longitude, 250-pin cap); the report page has none. - **address**: Report: 'Physical Address 11005 JOHNSON DRIVE SHAWNEE, KS 66203' as one string (needs splitting), plus Telephone, Permit Holder, License Type, Risk Category ('Category I - High Risk Less Than 5,000 sq. ft.'). JSON: Address1, Address2, City, StateProvinceAbbreviation, PostalCode, Phone, DisplayName, LicenseTypeAbbreviation. Legacy: NonFormattedNameAddress="NAME ADDRESS CITY, KS ZIP PHONE" row attribute.
- **inspection_date**: Report page 'Date 08/13/2026' with 'Time In'/'Time Out' (MM/DD/YYYY); JSON InspectionCompletedOn; legacy row <td align="center" style="width:10%;">MM/DD/YYYY</td>.
- **facility_id**: Current portal: 'License Number' on the report page (e.g. 4419) and LicenseNumber in _MapLocations JSON; a foodLicenseGuid rides data-license-guid on each result card (used by _PastInspections) — card markup unverified. Legacy: attribute Key="222900" on the lnkPastInspections anchor (facility key); map arrays keyed by FacilityKey.
- **inspection_id**: Current portal: inspectionGuid (e.g. 7fd77506-6188-4181-88b6-b1a24a80d182) plus the report's 'Inspection No.' INSP00302859. Legacy: InspectionSequenceKey="24191" attribute on the row.

Values seen: Routine, Complaint, Modified Complaint, Follow-up, Verification, Licensing-Preoperational, Expired License, Standardization, Follow-up to Administrative Order, Remote - Follow-up, Remote - Modified Complaint.
- **Weekly volume**: ~340 establishments with at least one inspection per week statewide (legacy count for 2026-06-22..28 = "342 record"; Sedgwick/Wichita June 2026 = 182; the legacy week count under-counts same-place re-inspections), i.e. roughly 350-450 inspections/week. Consistent with the ~20,561 (2017) / 21,102 (2016) inspections per year quoted in a web-search snippet of a KDA presentation that this container could not download (403) — unverified.
- **Weekly requests**: As things stand: 0 useful requests — nothing new can be discovered without a reCAPTCHA token. If KDA removed or exempted the gate: 1 GET (page + anti-forgery token) + ceil(~400 / page size) _Search POSTs (page size unverified; 20-40 pages) + ~400 GET Results?inspectionGuid= (one per inspection, ~1.1 s each) = roughly 430-450 requests, or 1 Export POST (CSV, columns unverified) + ~400 report GETs for violation text; coordinates would need _MapLocations per county (250-pin cap) or geocoding.
- **90-day backfill**: Current portal (gated): ~5,200 inspections over 90 days -> ~5,200 report GETs (~1.6 h at 1.1 s) + 130-520 _Search pages (page size unverified) = ~5,400-5,800 requests. Legacy portal (un-gated but only history up to 2026-07-02): 13 weekly statewide windows (each under the 500 cap) x ~23 postbacks per window (342 rows / 15 per page) = ~300 page postbacks that already carry rows + coordinates (chkDisplayMap=on), plus one lnkViolations postback per inspection with violations (~3,000-4,400) = ~3,500-4,700 requests; per-inspection PDFs (btnInspectionReport) would double that.
- **History**: Legacy portal: 2015-08-04 onward (Riley County FAQ: results after August 4, 2015 are in the KDA search; oldest row seen 08/24/2015) through 2026-07-02, where it stops (0 rows for 08/2026-10/2026). Current portal: newest data (public report dated 08/13/2026, page says 'refreshed nightly'); how far its _PastInspections history reaches is unverified (gated search needed).
- **Recommended strategy**: Do not build a scraper against portal.kda.ks.gov now: its discovery endpoints sit behind reCAPTCHA v3 and bypassing that is off the table. (1) Ask KDA Food Safety & Lodging (kda.fsl@ks.gov, 785-564-6767) for a nightly extract or a Kansas Open Records Act request: the Export CSV and _MapLocations JSON prove the AIMS system already produces a flat inspection list with license number, address, compliance flag, violation count and lat/long; a static file or an un-gated endpoint filtered by inspection date would make this a ~5-request weekly fetcher (file GET + Results GETs only for violation text, or none if the file carries violations). (2) If KDA opens such a path, the incremental is: window = _lookback_since (day before the latest SHIPPED Kansas inspection, 400-day API cap) .. today; search by InspectionDateFrom/To, page until a page has fewer cards than the page size AND no next .fis-page link, collect every inspectionGuid + license id; GET Results?inspectionGuid= per inspection (1.1 s each, run it like the MHD detail phase oldest-first inside --deadline-minutes); parse td.col-item/td.col-code/span.priority-tag ('P' -> priority, 'Pf' -> priority_foundation, else core) and the Inspector Comment text; compliance = 'Follow-Up Required'; one record per license per day (worst). Completeness guards: assert cards parsed == the rendered total (if the result header prints one — unverified) and that page N+1 is empty; any non-200 report, a report missing 'Inspection No.' or Date, or a deadline cut = drop + _hold_freshness('KDA Food Safety', date, key=inspectionGuid, counted=False for deadline/5xx) and _set_group_status('skipped'/'blocked'), so the run goes red instead of shipping a partial week; never record a failed report read as zero violations. Coordinates: _MapLocations per county GUID per window (stay under the 250 cap) or geocode the split 'Physical Address'; coalesce lat/lng on merge. Register the group with _record_group and note 'source refreshed nightly' with _note_source_lag when stale. (3) Optional one-time history seed from the frozen legacy portal (no captcha, verified reachable): week-by-week statewide windows (<500 rows each), 15-row page postbacks, chkDisplayMap=on for coordinates, lnkViolations postback per inspection; only worth doing once a current-data path exists.
- **Risks**:
  - reCAPTCHA v3 on _Search/_MapLocations/Export is the hard blocker: no token can be minted from a stdlib job or the Cloudflare relay, and working around an anti-bot gate is out of policy (the enforcement probe itself was denied). Enforcement is unverified; assume enforced.
  - Discovery depends entirely on GUIDs: Results/_Violations/_PastInspections are un-gated but useless without the gated search (random GUIDs, zero GUID -> 404).
  - Legacy portal is frozen at 2026-07-02 and its Export returns HTTP 500; it is history-only (2015-08 .. 2026-07) and capped at 500 rows per search (per-week slicing needed).
  - No official score or grade: risk must be computed from P / Pf / core items and inspector comments; compliance is only a Follow-Up Required flag, so grades would be fully computed and should NOT be a source-stated tier.
  - Coordinates exist only in gated _MapLocations (250-pin cap) or the frozen legacy map arrays; a current-data fetcher would need per-county map calls or geocoding of 'Physical Address' strings that mix street, city, state, zip in one field.
  - Volume figures (20-21k inspections/yr) and statewide authority could not be verified from KDA's own site (agriculture.ks.gov 403 Akamai from this container).
  - Export endpoint has a 429 rate-limit path in the page JS; other rate limits and terms of use unknown (no robots.txt on the portal, no terms page found).
  - Portal is a custom KDA 'AIMS' app (Production 20261027.1, deployed days ago): markup/class names (fis-*, data-table, priority-tag) may change without notice; the report HTML is a template with many hidden enforcement pages.
  - Legacy date-window searches are establishment-centric (one row per facility, matching inspection on top), so counts and incremental logic must look inside the nested past-inspections grid to avoid missing same-week re-inspections.

- **Pagination**: ArcGIS layer 3: resultOffset/resultRecordCount (max 2000), orderByFields=OBJECTID; verified offset 5000 → 178 rows, no exceededTransferLimit. ACA searches: 10 rows per page, count display capped at '100+', next/prev and page numbers are __doPostBack controls (ctl00$PlaceHolderMain$dgvPermitList$gdvPermitList$ctl13$ctl0N) requiring the page's __VIEWSTATE; attachment lists paginate the same way (< Prev 1 2 Next >). HealthSpace Domino views: &Count=N (&Start=N standard Domino, unverified). PressAgent: unverified.

- **grade**: None from either agency. CPH publishes sign status only: STATUS_DESCRIP 'Standards Met' / 'Under Enforcement' / 'Closed for Violation' / 'No Status' (INSPECTION_STATUS 1/2/3/null) = Green/Yellow/Red sign. FCPH: 'no grade assigned' (department statement). score_scale = 'none'.

OBSERVED CUT ONION CONTAINER STACKED DIRECTLY ON TOP OF ANOTHER. … (CCP) | þ | ¨'; narrative-only reports for complaint inspections. HealthSpace legacy: 'Critical' / 'Non-critical' + 'Repeat' / 'Corrected During Inspection'. CPH: Dispatch says its public database records critical violations only (unverified).
