- **address**: CPH layer: SITE_ADDRESS, CITY, STATE, ZIP (verified). FCPH: ACA search 'address' column and record 'Work Location' (e.g. '9825 Johnstown RD New Albany OH 43054'); PDF header Address / City/State/Zip Code (verified).
- **inspection_date**: CPH layer: absent (PressAgent unverified). FCPH PDF: 'Date' MM/DD/YYYY in the header (verified) and in the filename (_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS); attachment row 'Latest Update'. HealthSpace legacy: 'DD-Mon-YYYY'.
- **facility_id**: CPH: FACILITY_ID 'FA#######' (4,835 distinct; not unique per row), RECORD_ID 'PR#######' and PERMIT_ID 'PT#######' (unique per row). FCPH: ACA record number e.g. 2500-PFFL-23-000197 / 2500-PFSOL-26-006001 (= License Number on the PDF) plus internal capID1/capID2/capID3 (e.g. 23HIS/00000/00FV4). HealthSpace legacy: 32-hex RestrictToCategory ids.
- **inspection_id**: CPH: unverified (PressAgent record_id PR####### is per program record, not per inspection). FCPH: no id — use record number + PDF filename (Food_Inspection_HEA5302A_280_20260819_111428.pdf) as the stable key. HealthSpace: 32-hex inspection id.
- **inspection_type**: FCPH PDF 'Type of inspection (check all that apply)': Standard, Critical Control Point (FSO), Process Review (RFE), Variance Review, Follow up, Foodborne, 30 Day, Complaint, Pre-Licensing, Consultation (þ = checked; page 2 header also prints e.g. 'Standard/Critical Control Point'). HealthSpace legacy: 'Standard/Critical Control Point Inspection'. CPH: unverified.
- **Weekly volume**: Unverified estimates (no inspection feed could be counted): CPH ~150–250 inspections/week (5,178 licensed records; Ohio requires 1–4 standard/CCP inspections per year by risk level, plus follow-ups/complaints); FCPH ~100–200/week (license records number ~5–6k by record-number sequence). New CPH facilities: 430 since 2026-01-01 (~10/week, verified); new/changed FCPH license records: 5 in the last 7 days, 21 since 2026-10-01 (verified).
- **Weekly requests**: CPH: 3 ArcGIS page requests + 1 count request for the roster; inspection detail unverified — if PressAgent has no date search, ~5,200 record-page GETs (one per RECORD_ID/WEB_LINK) plus per-inspection pages. FCPH: ~5–6k attachment-list GETs (one per roster record; the roster/capIDs cached), 1–2 general-search postbacks for new license records, plus ~100–300 PDF POSTs (~235 KB each, ~25–70 MB) — roughly 6–7k requests/week; an Accela Construct API app id (if granted) would cut this to tens of calls (unverified).
- **90-day backfill**: FCPH: roster build ≈ 500–600 search postbacks (10 rows each) or one exact-record redirect per known record number (~6k GETs) + ~6k attachment lists + ~1,500–3,000 PDF downloads (~0.4–0.7 GB) ≈ 10–15k requests; PDFs older than ~2026-05 are sparse. CPH: 3 ArcGIS pages + ~5,200 PressAgent record pages + unverified per-inspection pages (portal structure unknown today). HealthSpace legacy backfill (≤2025-05-08): ~1 JSON view page per 25–999 facilities + 1 history page per facility + 1 detail page per inspection (several thousand).
- **History**: CPH ArcGIS layer: current state only (no history). CPH PressAgent: unverified (down). FCPH ACA: PDFs attached to active license records; stated to start 2026-05-19 but the Chipotle record already held a 2026-04-28 report (observed); records themselves date from 2013 (2500-PFFL-13-…). FCPH HealthSpace legacy: multi-year history (sample facility: May-2022 … Jan-2025) frozen at 2025-05-08. Gap 2025-05-09..2026-05-18: records request only.
- **Recommended strategy**: Treat as two fetch groups registered separately with _record_group. (A) CPH facility roster: page the public ArcGIS layer https://maps2.columbus.gov/arcgis/rest/services/Schemas/Health/MapServer/3/query (where=1=1, outFields=*, outSR=4326, resultOffset/resultRecordCount=2000, orderByFields=OBJECTID; 3 pages for 5,178 rows, verified) for name, address, city, zip, coordinates, FACILITY_ID/RECORD_ID/PERMIT_ID, BUS_TYPE_DESCR, RISK_LEVEL, STATUS_DESCRIP (sign colour) and WEB_LINK. Completeness check: the first page's count vs returnCountOnly=true, and exceededTransferLimit must be absent on the last page. Inspection dates/violations for CPH live only on the vendor portal (pressagent.envisionconnect.com, agency=COL; WEB_LINK = decadeonline.com/insp.phtml?agency=COL&record_id=PR…), which answered HTTP 522 (Cloudflare 'origin unreachable') for the entire 30-minute probe, so its page shapes are UNVERIFIED — re-probe before writing a fetcher; expect one GET per record_id (≈5.2k/week) unless the portal exposes a date search, and per the Dispatch its public database holds critical violations only. Hold freshness (_hold_freshness) for any record page that fails; never ship a facility-layer row as an inspection. (B) FCPH: inspections are PDF attachments ('Final Inspection Report', filename Food_Inspection_HEA5302A_280_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.pdf, timestamp = inspection date on both samples) on Accela Citizen Access license records (agency FRANKLINCPH, module EnvHealth, record type EnvHealth/Food/NA/License, plus Mobile Food). Build a roster once by paging the general search (10 rows/postback, '100+' display cap) or by exact-record redirects, caching each record's capID1/2/3 from the CapDetail redirect; weekly: (1) run the general search with txtGSStartDate/txtGSEndDate = lookback window to pick up new/changed license records (verified: 5 records for 10/26–11/02/2026); (2) GET every roster record's AttachmentsList.aspx iframe (one request each, ~5–6k/week, anonymous) and diff attachment names against the stored set; (3) for each new 'Final Inspection Report' POST the iframe's hidden fields with __EVENTTARGET=attachmentList$gdvAttachmentList$ctlNN$lnkFileName (verified: application/octet-stream PDF, 235–240 KB); (4) parse the HEA 5302A/B PDF with the pipeline's stdlib PDF text path: Date, Type of inspection checkboxes, and the 'Observations and Corrective Actions' table (Item No | Code Section | Priority Level C/NC | Comment | COS | R) → critical=priority, non-critical=core, risk from counts (score_scale 'none'). 'Read everything' guarantee: an inspection ships only when its PDF parsed with a Date and a facility name; a record whose attachments page or PDF failed is held (_hold_freshness key=record number) and the group reports 'skipped'/'blocked' via _set_group_status when any roster page or more than a threshold of attachment lists failed, so a partial week never passes silently. Geocode FCPH addresses (no coordinates anywhere in ACA). Backfill: HealthSpace Domino vi ...
- **Risks**:
  - PressAgent/DecadeOnline (CPH inspection detail) was HTTP 522 (server: cloudflare, cf-ray ATL) on every attempt 21:58–22:24 UTC, http and https, root and page; decadeonline.com 503; another EnvisionConnect host also dropping — vendor outage, so CPH detail page structure, inspection ids, dates and violation format are entirely UNVERIFIED today
  - Dispatch (2025-05-23) states 'Columbus Public Health only records critical violations on its public database' — CPH violation lists may be critical-only, understating risk vs other sources
  - CPH ArcGIS layer carries NO inspection date, score or violations: facility inventory + sign status only (5,170 of 5,178 'Standards Met'); must not be shipped as inspections
  - FCPH has no 'inspections since date X' query: ACA record-type date filter applies to license record dates (verified), so weekly discovery needs ~1 anonymous GET per license record (~5–6k) to diff attachment lists — rate limits of aca-prod.accela.com unknown/unverified; Denver's Accela was previously judged not machine-readable
  - FCPH PDFs are 230–240 KB each (~100–300/week estimated, unverified) and must be parsed (HEA 5302A/B layout); a layout change must read as unread, not zero violations
  - FCPH coverage gap: reports 2025-05-09..2026-05-18 exist only via records request; HealthSpace legacy frozen at 2025-05-08; one record already showed a 2026-04-28 PDF despite the stated 2026-05-19 cutover, so the real start date is unverified
  - No score or grade from either agency (Columbus: sign colours Green/Yellow/Red/White; Ohio form: critical/non-critical items) — risk must be computed from violations; score_scale 'none'
  - ACA 'Download results' export returned HTML in two postback attempts (may need login or AJAX framing) — no CSV shortcut verified
  - FCPH ACA gives no coordinates (addresses only) — every record needs geocoding; CPH layer has points for all 5,178 rows
  - columbus.gov 403s curl from this container and myfcph.org resets/empties the connection (HTTP/2 PROTOCOL_ERROR / empty reply) — official pages readable only via WebFetch; archive.org/archive.ph unreachable from here
  - CPH FACILITY_ID is not unique per row (4,835 facilities vs 5,178 records: hospitals/clubs with several program records); RECORD_ID/PERMIT_ID are unique — join key choice matters for make_restaurant_id
  - Accela Construct API requires a registered app id; anonymous agency access for FRANKLINCPH unverified

## Reno / Washoe County, Nevada — Northern Nevada Public Health

- **Feasibility**: low; reachable: True
- **Population**: Washoe County, NV (Reno, Sparks, Incline Village; ~0.5 M residents — 2020 census 486,492), ~3,972 permitted food establishments (NNPH 2024 figure).
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores' only Nevada coverage is Las Vegas / Clark County (SNHD); Washoe County is a different health authority (NNPH) and system (Accela ONE regional instance).
- **Base URL**: https://aca-prod.accela.com/ONE/ (Accela Citizen Access, agency WASHOE, module Health; reached via https://onenv.us → 200). The official consumer source, the "Washoe Eats" app (www.washoeeats.com → 302 → https://www.nnph.org/resources/food-inspections.php), has no web client and its backend was not discoverable.
- **Auth**: None observed for ACA: anonymous record search, record detail, attachment list and PDF download all worked without login (cookie session + ASP.NET ViewState round-tripping required). nnph.org needs no auth but its TLS chain is incomplete (ship the Sectigo 'Public Server Authentication CA OV R36' intermediate, AIA http://crt.sectigo.com/SectigoPublicServerAuthenticationCAOVR36.crt, in the CA bundle ...
- **Date filter**: ACA general search has txtGSStartDate/txtGSEndDate (mm/dd/yyyy), verified working (10/01/2026–11/02/2026 and 01/01/2024–03/31/2024 returned rows in range), but it filters the RECORD (permit/application) date, not inspection dates; there is no inspection-date search anywhere on the portal. The only inspection date signal is the attachment grid's 'Latest Update' column (mm/dd/yyyy) and the 'Date:' field inside the PDF (verified equal for the sample: 10/02/2025).
- **Pagination**: ACA search grid: 10 rows per page, count text capped at 'Showing 1-10 of 100+'; page postbacks exist in the UI but the pager control name was not captured (unverified) — avoid by searching narrow record-date windows or by permit number. Attachments list: 5 rows per page with '< Prev 1 2 Next >' postbacks on the same AttachmentsList.aspx URL. Every page change is a POST carrying the current __VIEWSTATE.
- **score**: None published. PDF gives 'No. of Critical Risk Factor/Intervention violations' (count) and per-item IN/OUT/N-A/N-O (+COS, R) for items 1–51; a risk_score must be computed from OUT items (or mapped from status like Orange County: Pass/Conditional Pass/Fail/Closed)
- **grade**: PDF 'Facility Status': Pass / Conditional Pass / Fail / Closed (NNPH: Pass <3 critical; Conditional 3–5 critical or same critical 3 routine inspections running; Closed = uncorrectable critical). Sample: 'Pass'
- **violations**: PDF: item table (number, status, title) plus a narrative paragraph per OUT item under 'Item # Violation #' (title line then 'NN <observation text>'), 'Comments', 'Temperatures Observed', Re-Inspection Date; pdftotext -layout extracts all of it (SSRS-rendered typed PDF, 4 pages, 15.7 KB of text)
- **severity**: Critical items are marked only by BOLD in the PDF (lost in text extraction); derive from a static item-number map (items 1–30 are the FDA risk-factor/intervention block; 31–51 good retail practices) and cross-check against the report's critical count; COS and R flags are in the status column
- **coordinates**: None anywhere (ACA has no geometry; no county GIS layer) — geocode the address
- **address**: Search grid 'Short Notes' = '1955 ODDIE BLVD SPARKS NV 89431' style; PDF 'Address: 1255 STARDUST ST, RENO, NV 89503'; business name = grid 'Project Name' / PDF 'DBA/Name'
- **inspection_date**: Attachment 'Latest Update' (mm/dd/yyyy) and PDF header 'Date: 10/2/2025' (verified identical on the sample)
- **facility_id**: ACA record number (e.g. H24-0154FOOD, also printed as 'Permit #' in the PDF) plus the capID1/capID2/capID3/agencyCode triple in the CapDetail link; several permits can share a name/address (make_restaurant_id still keys on name+address+city)
- **inspection_id**: None native. Derive from record number + attachment 'Latest Update' date (+ file name, e.g. TasteofIndiaRoutineHealthInspection1022025.pdf) — the attachment row is the inspection
- **inspection_type**: PDF header 'Insepction Type: Routine Inspection' (sic — the form misspells it); attachment Type distinguishes 'Inspection Report' vs 'Inspection Photos'; construction/pre-opening inspections appear as separate 'Inspection Report' PDFs (e.g. 'ehs amo construction inspection…pdf')
- **Weekly volume**: ~100–150 inspections/week (estimate, unverified): NNPH's 2024 Risk Factor Study counts ~3,972 food establishments, each routinely inspected at least once a year plus follow-ups/re-inspections/complaints (~5,000–8,000 inspections/yr). Not measurable from ACA without the full enumeration.
- **Weekly requests**: ~4,500–8,500 HTTP requests: ACA has no inspection-date search, so a weekly run must open every active food/mobile-food permit's attachments list (~4,000 permits × 1–2 GETs for the 5-row pages; one shared session, ViewState round-trips) to spot 'Inspection Report' rows with Latest Update ≥ window start, then ~100–150 PDF download POSTs plus a detail-page GET per changed record (the iframe src must come from the detail page). Record-level enumeration (search postbacks, 10 rows/page) is a one-time ~400–800 requests, refreshed monthly for new permits.
- **90-day backfill**: Same enumeration pass (~4,000–8,000 attachment-list GETs + ~400–800 search postbacks) plus ~1,300–2,000 PDF downloads (13 weeks × 100–150, each 70–600 KB) → roughly 6,000–11,000 requests and ~0.5–1 GB of PDFs. Deeper history is available in the same pass (reports back to each permit's creation; NNPH says pre-2017 reports may be missing) at ~1 extra PDF per inspection.
- **History**: Per permit, every inspection report ever attached to the record (sample permit opened 03/2024 shows construction inspections 05/2024, routine 10/02/2025, photos 10/02/2026); NNPH states 'Inspection reports prior to 2017 may not be available'. The Washoe Eats app shows 'most recent' results only (description), data 'updated daily'.
- **Recommended strategy**: Two tracks. (1) Preferred, not yet possible: obtain the structured feed behind Washoe Eats — ask NNPH Environmental Health (healthehs@nnph.org, 775-328-2434) / CityGovApp (info@citygovapp.com) for the daily export or an endpoint, or request the Accela 'inspections + checklist results' export under NRS 239; the Accela Construct API (apis.accela.com /v4/records?…&updateDateFrom=, /v4/records/{id}/inspections, /v4/records/{id}/documents) is the documented route if NNPH authorizes an app (unverified). (2) What works today — an ACA scrape, built like fetch_omaha (layer rows → PDF per inspection) with MHD-style statefulness: (a) one-time permit inventory: POST CapHome.aspx searches per food record type ('Health/Food Permit/NA/NA', 'Health/Food/Food Permit/Permit', 'Health/Food/Mobile Permit/Permit', …) in narrow record-date windows (10 rows/page, count capped at 100+), store record number, capID triple, name, address, status; refresh monthly for new permits (records opened since the last inventory, via the record-date filter). (b) Weekly: for every active permit, oldest-last-read first, GET CapDetail (for the iframe src/session) then AttachmentsList.aspx (+Next pages) and take rows with Type == 'Inspection Report' and Latest Update ≥ _lookback_since(group, …) (scrape cap 90 days); download each new PDF via the __EVENTTARGET=attachmentList$gdvAttachmentList$ctlNN$lnkFileName postback, parse with a stdlib text extractor (the SSRS PDF is typed; reuse the Orange County PDF parser pattern), take Date, Facility Status, critical count, OUT items with narrative; ship with inspection_type from the header, status → grade/score mapping (Pass/Conditional Pass/Fail/Closed, like Orange County), source_id = record number + date. (c) Never ship a partial window silently: a record whose list or PDF fetch fails gets _hold_freshness(source, attachment_date or window start, key=record number) and is retried next run (give-up after FRESHNESS_HOLD_GIVE_UP_RUNS); a run cut by --deadline-minutes or an ACA error page/ban marks the group 'skipped'/'blocked' via _set_group_status, and only permits whose attachment list was fully read (all pages) count toward 'read everything'; a PDF that parses without a 'Facility Status' line counts as unread, not as zero violations (OC rule). Register the group with _record_group and expect ~1–2 h of polite sequential requests (MHD_DELAY-style pacing; no throttling was observed in ~15 requests, so limits are unknown). Store no owner contact data from the detail page.
- **Risks**:
  - The structured source (Washoe Eats app backend, CityGovApp) is undiscovered: no web client, no docs, APK mirrors blocked (403/502), and path enumeration on apps.citygovapp.com was denied by the session policy — only the heavy ACA route is verified
  - ACA has no inspection-date search: the weekly incremental must touch every (~4,000) active permit's attachment list, ~4,500–8,500 stateful ASP.NET requests per run; ACA rate limits/bans were not measured (15 requests, all 200) and Accela's shared regional instance can throttle or block a runner (relay via functions/api/relay.js may be needed, like MHD/SNHD)
  - ASP.NET WebForms fragility: every step is a POST with __VIEWSTATE/__EVENTVALIDATION round-trips, autopostbacks and iframe sessions; a markup or control-name change breaks the scraper; the result count is capped ('100+') and the pager control was not captured (pagination unverified)
  - PDF parsing risk: critical severity is encoded only as bold text (lost in extraction) — needs a static item→critical map; form typos ('Insepction Type'); construction/pre-opening inspection PDFs must be told apart from routine ones; photos and permits sit in the same attachment list
  - nnph.org serves an incomplete TLS chain (missing Sectigo OV R36 intermediate): any fetch of nnph.org fails verification with stock CA bundles unless the intermediate is shipped — never disable verification
  - No coordinates anywhere: every establishment needs geocoding (address quality is good: street, city, state, zip)
  - Record detail pages expose owner names, phones and e-mails — the fetcher must not persist them
  - No official score: a Pass/Conditional Pass/Fail/Closed status plus a critical count must be mapped to risk_score (source-stated tier, like Orange County); inconsistent status vocabulary between NNPH pages (one lists no 'Fail')
  - Platform churn on the shared 'ONE' Accela instance: Douglas County left for Oracle OPAL on 2026-03-23 (ACA banner); Washoe reportedly renewed Accela for FY2026–2030 (web search, unverified) — a migration would kill the scraper
  - CivicData's three Washoe datasets are ghosts (index only, all 404): do not build on them
  - Terms of use for automated access to ACA/OneNV.us were not checked (unverified); volume and population figures are estimates, not measured
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://www.washoeeats.com/` (none) Official 'inspection results' site: bare 302 to the NNPH page below; no search form, no API, no Wayback history [302 (Location: https://www.nnph.org/resources/food-inspections.php)]
  - `GET https://www.nnph.org/resources/food-inspections.php` (none (needs the Sectigo OV R36 intermediate added to the CA bundle: the server omits it; curl/requests fail 'unable to get local issuer certificate' with the stock bundle)) NNPH landing page: points to the Washoe Eats app and documents the OneNV.us/ACA path (Record Info → Attachments → 'Inspection Report'); states reports before 2017 may be unavailable; grading model Pass / Conditional Pass / Fail / Closed [200 (28,559 B) with the completed chain; TLS failure (curl 60) with the default bundle; WebFetch tool got 503]
  - `GET https://nnph.org/programs-and-services/environmental-health/food-protection-services/food-inspections.php` (none) Second NNPH results page: only links to Play Store com.citygovapp.washoeeats and App Store id1459538876; no inline API calls or iframes [200 (35,144 B)]
  - `GET then POST (ASP.NET postbacks; cookie session; resend every hidden input incl. __VIEWSTATE, __VIEWSTATEGENERATOR, __VIEWSTATEENCRYPTED, ACA_CS_FIELD) https://aca-prod.accela.com/ONE/Cap/CapHome.aspx?module=Health&TabName=Health` (P=ctl00$PlaceHolderMain$generalSearchForm$ ; step 1 __EVENTTARGET=P+ddlGSSubAgency with P+ddlGSSubAgency=WASHOE (autopostback loads 126 WASHOE record types); step 2 __EVENTTARGET=ctl00$PlaceHolderMain ...) Enumerate food-permit RECORDS (one row per permit: Date, Record Number e.g. H24-0154FOOD, Record Type, Description, Project Name = business name, Expiration, Status, Short Notes = street address with city/state/zip) and get each record's CapDetail link (capID1/capID2/capID3/agencyCode) [GET 200 (238,859 B); agency postback 200 (263 KB); search postbacks 200 (537 KB) showing 'Showing 1-10 of 100+' (count capped; pager postback not located — pagination unverified); a single-hit permit-number search answers with the detail page itself]
  - `GET https://aca-prod.accela.com/ONE/Cap/CapDetail.aspx?Module=Health&TabName=Health&capID1=24CAP&capID2=00000&capID3=00584&agencyCode=WASHOE` (capID1, capID2, capID3, agencyCode from the search grid link) Record detail: Record Info menu = Record Details, Attachments, Conditions — there is NO Inspections tab; page carries the attachments iframe src. Prints owner phone/e-mail (do not store). /ONE/Cap/InspectionList.aspx, CapAttachmentList.aspx, CapDocumentList.aspx do not exist on this instance [200 (237 KB); the three guessed list pages → 200 error page "'…' does not exist"]
  - `GET (same cookie session as the detail page); then POST with the page's hidden fields and __EVENTTARGET=attachmentList$gdvAttachmentList$ctlNN$lnkFileName to download row NN https://aca-prod.accela.com/ONE/FileUpload/AttachmentsList.aspx?iframeid=ctl00_PlaceHolderMain_attachmentEdit&module=Health&isInConfirm=False&isdetail=True&isaccountmanager=False&isAdmin=False&isPeopleDocument=&agencyCode=WASHOE&…` (iframe src copied verbatim from CapDetail; 5 rows per page with Prev/Next postbacks) Per-record attachment grid: Name | Record ID | Record Type | Entity Type | Type ('Inspection Report', 'Inspection Photos', 'Permit') | Size | Latest Update (date). The 'Inspection Report' rows ARE the inspections; the download postback returns the PDF anonymously (Content-Disposition attachment; fil ... [GET 200 (55,734 B); download POST 200 application/octet-stream]
  - `GET https://civicdata.com/api/3/action/package_search?q=washoe&rows=50` (q, rows (CKAN)) Accela's open-data site: index still lists washoe_health_food_permit_inspections_17249 / _violations_17248 / food_permit_businesses_17243 (org 'Washoe County, NV', modified 2026-07-01) but package_show, resource_show, datastore_search (resource_ids 0b9e52bd-71d2-46dd-899f-e4966b6ebf6c, ca8e8e76-5e9c ... [package_search 200; everything else 404; organization_list?q=washoe 200 []]
  - `GET https://apps.citygovapp.com/washoe/` (none) Vendor (CityGovApp, Accela partner; app bundle com.citygovapp.WashoeEats; Play developer contact info@citygovapp.com) host found via DNS/CT logs; /washoe/ exists but is forbidden; sibling guesses 404; root is an Apache test page. Further enumeration was denied by this session's policy classifier — t ... [/ 403 (4,681 B), /washoe/ 403 (199 B), /washoeeats/ /WashoeEats/ /api/ /api/washoeeats /eats/ 404; staging.citygovapp.com/washoe/ 403; qa.citygovapp.com/washoe/ 404]
  - `GET https://wcgisweb.washoecounty.us/arcgis/rest/services?f=json and https://gis.washoecounty.us/arcgis/rest/services?f=json` (f=json) County ArcGIS catalogs (folders incl. NNPH, OpenData, Accela): no food/inspection/health-permit service or layer (OpenData FeatureServer 54 layers, none relevant); ArcGIS Hub search likewise [200 / 200]

## Tulsa, Oklahoma — Tulsa Health Department (THD) public inspe

- **Feasibility**: medium; reachable: True; blocked: Not blocked: every request from this container (through the proxy) returned HTTP 200; no captcha, login, rate limit or terms-of-use gate was encountered.
- **Population**: Tulsa County, OK: 693,514 (Census Bureau V2024 estimate per QuickFacts, as reported by web search — not fetched directly, so unverified by me; 2020 census 669,279). THD's own site says it serves "more than 600,000 Tulsa County residents, 13 municipalities and four unincorporated areas" and inspects ~3,500 food establishments ~11,000 times a year. The inventory confirms Tulsa, Broken Arrow, Owasso, Sand Springs, Jenks, Bixby, Glenpool, Collinsville, Skiatook, Sperry, with a few Catoosa/Sapulpa/Mo ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None. DineScores has no Oklahoma source; CLAUDE.md's survey lists Oklahoma City (Inspect2Go, no date filter) as surveyed-but-not-added and does not mention Tulsa. Tulsa County is ~90 miles from OKC; no geographic overlap with any covered metro.
- **Base URL**: https://ok.healthinspections.us/tulsa/ (ColdFusion/IIS; embedded as an iframe on https://www.tulsa-health.org/food-safety/restaurant-inspections/results-inspections/; the host root redirects to tylertech.com Enterprise Environmental Health). Full inspection forms live one level up at https://ok.healthinspections.us/_templates/125/Food%20Inspection/_report_full.cfm
- **Auth**: None. No login, no API key, no captcha. CFID/CFTOKEN/JSESSIONID cookies are set by index.cfm but are NOT required: facility pages, inspection pages and full forms were fetched cookie-less with identical content (verified). Search needs a POST (a GET with the same query string returns the blank form, verified).
- **Date filter**: NONE that works. The search form's `startDate`/`endDate` (MM/DD/YYYY, hidden inputs built from month/day/year selects; also quick-form defaults 11/02/2025-11/02/2026) are inert: the count stayed exactly 5652 for 11/01-11/02/2026, 10/26-11/02/2026, 01/01-01/07/2026, 2024-2026, 2015-2026, a future range 01/01-01/02/2027 and ISO dates 2026-10-26/2026-11-02 (7 POSTs, all 200). Search results carry no dates (`<span class="resultDate">` is empty). Dates appear only on the facility page (`Inspection Date: MM/DD/YYYY`, newest first), the inspection page (`Inspection Date: Month D, YYYY`) and the full form (`MM/DD/YYYY` + time). The only cheap "new since" signal is the inspection id sequence (see strategy); verified that ids are dense and roughly date-ordered from ~11537980 (Aug 2025) to the curren ...
- **Pagination**: Search (POST index.cfm): `startrow` (1-based) + `maxrows`. The UI offers 10/20/50/100/200 but the server honours any value: maxrows=1000 returned 1,000 rows (367 KB) and maxrows=10000 returned all 5,652 establishments in one 1.74 MB page in 10.4 s (verified). startrow=201 returned rows 201-400 with zero overlap with page 1 (verified). Header text: "Results <a> - <b> of <N> Inspections matching your search criteria" (N counts establishments despite the label). Facility and inspection pages are single pages; inspection ids are enumerable one per request (facility.cfm?action=doViewInspection&insp ...
- **score**: NONE. No numeric score anywhere (inspection page, full form, explainer). Omaha-style computation from violation counts is required (fetch_omaha, dinescores_pipeline.py:5048 uses 100-5P-2PF-C).
- **grade**: NONE. `Inspection Result:` was 'Compliant' in 100% of ~370 published inspections examined (30 sampled details + 117 + 90 + ~300 in id windows), including two with 'Action : Voluntary Closure' and 11 with 'Follow-Up Notice Issued : Yes'. The results explainer has a `fail` anchor and defines failure (e.g. 3+ CDC risk-factor violations, 7+ of items 1-21), but no non-compliant record was found — whether failed inspections are published at all is UNVERIFIED. Form also prints `Inspection Results:` and ...
- **violations**: Inspection page: three `<div id="violationTypes">` blocks (CDC Risk Factor Violations / Other Violations Shown To Cause Foodborne Illness / General Sanitation and Physical Facility Violations), each an `<ol>` of `<li value="<form item #>"><a href="violation_code_text.cfm?violCode=..">item title</a> ... <strong>Inspector Comments</strong><br />free text` ('No violations exist for this category.' when empty). Full form: 58-item OSDH OAC 310:257 report with per-item marks (an `X` in the OT column,  ...
- **severity**: Two signals: (1) the form's item number — items 1-35 are 'PRIORITY & PRIORITY FOUNDATION VIOLATIONS' (correct within 10 days), 36-58 'CORE VIOLATIONS' (90 days); the form does NOT distinguish P from Pf per item; (2) the portal's three public tiers. They DISAGREE: on the two saved inspection pages items 25, 26, 27, 31, 33, 34 (all within 1-35) were listed under 'General Sanitation', while item 7 was 'CDC Risk Factor' and item 21 'Other Foodborne'. Which to score from is a policy choice; the form  ...
- **coordinates**: NONE. No lat/lng on any page; the 'Map with Google Maps' link is an address query. The TCCHD ArcGIS org (services3.arcgis.com/yHSoWow4TMdKqS8y, 72 public services; arcgis.com search of the org, 536 items) has no food-establishment or inspection layer (verified 2026-11-02). Addresses must be geocoded via the pipeline's `geocode_missing_coords` (dinescores_pipeline.py:7723; Census batch, Nominatim fallback), as fetchers returning 'latitude': None already do.
- **address**: Search inventory: '<street>\n<CITY>, OK <zip[-4]>' (e.g. '7130 S MEMORIAL DR / TULSA, OK 74133-2962'); inspection page 'Location: 3701 N ASPEN AVE N BROKEN ARROW, OK 74012' (note the trailing direction suffix 'N'/'S' repeated after the street, absent on the form's '3701 N ASPEN AVE BROKEN ARROW OK 74012'). Pick ONE canonical source for make_restaurant_id (dinescores_pipeline.py:925). 163 zip options in the form; cities: Tulsa 3,505, Broken Arrow 656, Owasso 319, Sand Springs 170, Jenks 154, Bixb ...
- **inspection_date**: Inspection page: `Inspection Date:` cell, long form 'August 21, 2025'. Facility page: 'Inspection Date: 08/21/2025' inside the link text. Full form: '08/21/2025' + 'Inspection Time 1:05 PM'. Portal shows nothing older than May 05, 2025 (earliest observed in ~1,000 direct id probes).
- **facility_id**: `facilityID` (integer, in the base64 query of facility.cfm links, e.g. action=doViewAll&facilityID=1161802; plain unencoded query also works). The full form adds `THD Estab #` (e.g. 34942132) and `State License # & Expiration` (e.g. 110226 / 02/21/2026). Name + street + city/state/zip + `Establishment Type` come from the search inventory and from the inspection page header (`Location: 3701 N ASPEN AVE N BROKEN ARROW, OK 74012`, `Smoking:`). 5,652 establishments, 5,149 with an address line; 503 m ...
- **inspection_id**: `inspectionID` (integer, e.g. 11538036; dense sequence ~11537980..11548586 as of 2026-11-02 plus import blocks 11486170+, 11514790+, 11517600+, 11521910+, 11522360+, 11522750+, 11531070+, 11531630+ covering May-Aug 2025). Resolves with inspectionID alone; facilityID is ignored (verified with a wrong facilityID). Unpublished/unknown id → 1,282-byte stub with no establishment header.
- **inspection_type**: Inspection page `Inspection Purpose:` (observed: Routine, New Establishment, Complaint, 'Complaint - FBI'; the explainer also lists 'Follow-up (Compliance)' and 'Other' but NO follow-up was observed in ~370 published inspections). `Inspection Priority:` High/Medium/Low is the ESTABLISHMENT's inspection-frequency rating, not severity. Form: `Purpose of Inspection:`, `Class`, `Priority` (H/M/L).
- **Weekly volume**: ~150 newly published inspections/week across all programs: the id sequence advances 24.4 ids/day (10,000 ids from 11538036 on Aug 21, 2025 to 11548036 on Oct 5, 2026; 550 more by Oct 27), and 85-94% of ids in settled ranges resolve to a published inspection (25/30, 91/100, 41-47/50). Food programs were 52-93% of resolved ids depending on the window (pools are seasonal), so roughly 100-110 food-program inspections/week. The 60-facility sample gives a floor of ~55/week (0.667 inspections per food facility per year × 4,101 food-related establishments / 52). THD's stated ~11,000 inspections/year ( ...
- **Weekly requests**: Id-enumeration strategy: ~1,600 detail-page probes (re-scan the trailing ~1,100 ids ≈ 6 weeks, plus ~175 new ids, plus a 300-blank frontier tolerance; the probe IS the detail page, 0.15 s each) + ~110 full-form fetches for newly published food inspections + optionally 1 inventory POST (1.7 MB) ≈ 1,700-1,800 requests, 5-10 minutes at 3-4 req/s (observed safe). Facility-sweep alternative/backstop: ~4,100 facility pages + ~110 details + ~110 forms ≈ 4,300 requests (~20 min at 0.25 s).
- **90-day backfill**: 90 days ≈ 2,200 ids (24.4/day) → 2,200 detail probes + ~1,400 full forms (resolving food-program ids) + 1 inventory POST ≈ 3,600 requests, ~20-30 minutes at 0.3 s. Full published history (May 2025 →): the dense range 11537980-11548586 (~10,600 ids) plus the May-Aug 2025 import blocks, which are only discoverable through facility pages → ~4,100 facility pages + ~10,600 probes + ~8,000 forms ≈ 23,000 requests, ~2-3 hours; roughly 8,000-9,000 inspections in all (unverified total).
- **History**: About 18 months: the earliest inspection date found in ~1,000 direct id probes is May 05, 2025 (id block 11514790+); the facility sample's earliest is 2025-07-25; nothing older appears on facility pages (the form's year selects offer only 2024-2026). Per-facility history is thin: 0-3 inspections (24 of 60 sampled food facilities had 1, 6 had 2, 2 had 3, 28 had none). Ids below ~11537980 exist only in a handful of import-like blocks (May-Aug 2025); from 11537980 (Aug 12-20, 2025) the sequence is continuous to 11548586 (Oct 26-28, 2026).
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly incremental by inspection-id enumeration with a trailing re-scan, a facility-sweep backstop, and explicit completeness accounting:
1) State: keep `max_inspection_id_read` (and the first scan start of any unfinished backlog) in the freshness file's source entry (plain dict, like SNHD's `backlog_from`; unverified that nothing strips extra keys) — the recorded `latest` date alone cannot drive this source because ids lag inspection dates by up to 2+ weeks and the search has no working date filter.
2) Scan: GET https://ok.healthinspections.us/tulsa/facility.cfm?action=doViewInspection&inspectionID=N for N from (max_read − 1,100) upward, 0.2-0.25 s apart (3-4 req/s was fine for ~2,000 requests; no limit is documented), stopping after 300 consecutive stubs past the last hit (settled ranges show 6-15% stubs, never long runs — longest observed run 2; the frontier region is 48-74% stubs for ~2 weeks because records are published late, so the trailing re-scan is what picks them up). Classify each answer: (a) parseable detail page → candidate; (b) the 1,282-byte stub (no establishment header, 'No inspector comments were entered during this inspection activity') → unpublished, not a failure; (c) anything else (non-200, timeout, unparseable 200) → failure: `_hold_freshness(source, date, counted=True, key=N)` (dinescores_pipeline.py:8468) using the nearest resolved neighbour's date when the page gave none.
3) Detail: for candidates whose Establishment Type is a food program (skip Swimming Pool/Spa/Wading Pool/Therapy Pool/Hotel-Motel/Water Vending/Retail MM/Processor MM/CBD/Bottled Water/Privately Owned Prisons) and whose `tul_<inspectionID>` is not already in D1, GET the full form (`_report_full.cfm?ispub=1&inspectionID=N&parentTableName=tblInspection&dsn=DHD_125&domainid=125`) for item numbers (1-35 vs 36-58), R/W/T flags, THD Estab # and State License #; a form failure drops the inspection AND holds (never ship a half-read inspection; never record a blocked fetch as clean). Build the record like fetch_omaha (dinescores_pipeline.py:5048): original_score None; risk_score = 100 − 5·(CDC-tier items) − 2·(Other-Foodborne-tier items) − 1·(General items) or from the form split (1-35 → 2-5 pts, 36-58 → 1) — a calibration decision to make before shipping, since the two disagree (items 25/26/27/31/33/34 are 1-35 but tiered General); grade via calculate_vetted_grade (line 576) over title + inspector comments; violations as [classify_violation(title)[0], severity, 'title — comments']; inspection_type = Inspection Purpose; results = 'Compliant' plus Actions Taken text (surface 'Voluntary Closure'/'Follow-Up Notice Issued'); inspection_date from the detail page's 'Month D, YYYY' cross-checked with the form's MM/DD/YYYY; name/address/city/state/zip from ONE canonical source (the search inventory or the form; the detail/list 'Location:' repeats the street direction, e.g. '3701 N ASPEN AVE N', which would change make_restaurant_id); latitude/longitude None → geocode_m ...
- **Risks**:
  - No numeric score or grade: `Inspection Result` was 'Compliant' in 100% of ~370 published inspections, even with 'Action : Voluntary Closure' or 'Follow-Up Notice Issued : Yes'; failed inspections and Follow-up (Compliance) inspections were never observed, so the public feed may omit them (unverified) — grades must be computed from violations and may run lenient.
  - Severity must be inferred: the form does not distinguish Priority from Priority Foundation (items 1-35 are one block), and the portal's three public tiers disagree with the form's 1-35/36-58 split on 6 of the 1-35 items seen (25, 26, 27, 31, 33, 34 shown under General Sanitation). Scoring needs calibration before any backfill paints grades (cf. CLAUDE.md's MHD calibration invariant).
  - No server-side 'new since' listing: the search date filter is inert (7 ranges, identical counts) and results carry no dates. Incremental fetching relies on inspection-id enumeration (or a 4,100-page facility sweep).
  - Publication lag: within ~2 weeks of the id frontier only 26-52% of allocated ids resolve vs 85-94% in settled ranges, so a run must re-scan a trailing window; the maximum lag is unverified (≥2 weeks observed). The newest published inspection was 5 days old on 2026-11-02.
  - Id allocation below Aug 2025 is block-based (import blocks at 11486170+, 11514790+, 11517600+, 11521910+, 11522360+, 11522750+, 11531070+, 11531630+); enumeration cannot discover unknown blocks, and a future re-import/migration could repeat this — the facility sweep is the completeness backstop.
  - Only ~18 months of history (earliest May 05, 2025) and 0-3 inspections per facility; 28 of 60 sampled food facilities have no published inspection, so the map would show many venues with no grade.
  - No coordinates anywhere (portal or TCCHD ArcGIS): every record depends on geocode_missing_coords (Census batch + Nominatim); ~500 mobile units have no address at all. Address text differs between the list/detail pages ('3701 N ASPEN AVE N') and the form ('3701 N ASPEN AVE'): choosing inconsistently would change make_restaurant_id.
  - Scraping a ColdFusion vendor portal: markup can change on a Tyler release (template id 125 and the 'Food Inspection' template path are hard-coded in links); no robots.txt (404) and no terms of use were found on the portal, only the department's 'snapshot in time' disclaimer and a copyright notice on tulsa-health.org; no rate limit is documented (none hit at 3-5 req/s over ~2,000 requests, one 6.8 s latency spike).
  - The 1,282-byte stub for an unpublished id is indistinguishable from a deleted or never-existing id; a transient portal fault that returned stubs would look like 'nothing new' unless the fetcher checks that the trailing window still resolves where it did last week.
  - Jurisdiction edge: Broken Arrow straddles Tulsa and Wagoner counties; THD's authority is Tulsa County, so part of Broken Arrow (and all of Wagoner/Rogers/Creek County suburbs such as most of Catoosa, Claremore, Sapulpa) would be missing — unverified which addresses.
  - Volume uncertainty: id-rate (~150/week all programs, ~100-110 food) vs facility-sample floor (~55/week) vs THD's stated ~210/week; the sample's id/date pairing had a parsing bug (noted in sample_summary.json), so per-facility counts, not id pairings, were used for the floor.

## Alabama statewide — ADPH "Food Establishment Scores" search 

- **Feasibility**: medium; reachable: True; blocked: Not blocked from this container: 200s throughout, no captcha, login, 403 or rate limit. One county (Tallapoosa) is broken on the server side (persistent 500), and two counties (Jefferson/Birmingham, Mobile) are hand-offs to other agencies' portals.
- **Population**: 65 of Alabama's 67 counties, ~4.1 million people (Alabama ~5.2M minus Jefferson ~0.67M and Mobile ~0.41M — census figures from memory, unverified); 64 counties reachable today (Tallapoosa ~41k people currently 500s). Includes Huntsville metro (Madison+Limestone+Morgan ~530k), Montgomery metro (Montgomery+Autauga+Elmore ~390k), Tuscaloosa, Auburn-Opelika, Dothan, Decatur, Florence/Muscle Shoals, Gadsden, Baldwin County (Gulf Shores/Orange Beach/Daphne/Fairhope), Shelby and St. Clair (Birmingham s ...
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: None — CLAUDE.md lists no Alabama coverage; nearest covered places are statewide Florida, Nashville/Memphis/Chattanooga TN and Gwinnett County GA. Alabama is also absent from the 2026-10-10 candidate survey.
- **Base URL**: https://foodscores.state.al.us/  (GET / answers 302 to a cookieless-session path /(S(<24 chars>))/default.aspx; every search is a WebForms POST to that session path)
- **Auth**: None. Public ASP.NET WebForms app; cookieless session id lives in the URL path; the hidden __VIEWSTATE/__EVENTVALIDATION from one GET of the landing page were accepted for ~95 POSTs across several sessions. robots.txt -> 404; no terms of use published (ADPH policies page has only a privacy statement).
- **Date filter**: NONE server-side (verified: the form has only name, type, city, county; no hidden date parameter in the HTML). Each establishment shows only its LATEST inspection date (M/D/YYYY), so 'new since X' is a client-side diff of (restaurant id, date) against what is already stored. City and name filters are fuzzy prefix matches ('Montgomery' also returns Montevallo and Monroeville; 'Camp Hill' returns Camden) — scope by county dropdown only.
- **Pagination**: None in practice: a county search returns the whole county in one response (Madison: 2,460 records, 6.1 MB, 'Total Number:  2460' == parsed count; label == parsed count in all 64 counties; lblPageNumber empty everywhere). ImgPrevious/ImgNext buttons and A-Z letter postbacks exist in the markup but never engaged — a fetcher must still assert lblPageNumber is empty and parsed count == 'Total Number' before shipping.
- **score**: span id ctl00_ContentPlaceHolder1_DtList_ctlNN_LblScore — official 0-100 integer (18,794/18,794 numeric; min 60, median 97; 156 < 85, 4 < 70, none < 60). Use as original_score and risk_score like fetch_wake (dinescores_pipeline.py:2998); calculate_vetted_grade (line 576) gives A>=90/B/C/F<70.
- **grade**: None published by ADPH (thresholds only: 85-100 satisfactory, 70-84 follow-up within 60 days, 60-69 reinspection within 48 h, <60 closed).
- **violations**: NOT AVAILABLE. No detail page exists on the state site (record markup has only sort links and a Google Maps link). Violation text exists only on the separate JCDH (Birmingham) portal.
- **severity**: Not available (ADPH says priority / priority-foundation items 'have a higher point value', values unpublished).
- **coordinates**: None. Address only (the maps link is built from address+city+zip). All ~18.8k establishments need geocode_missing_coords (line 7723: Census batch 10k/request, then Census2020 benchmark, Nominatim fallback capped at 150/run).
- **address**: a id ..._DtList_ctlNN_LnkAdd text (street line; 77 of 18,794 have no leading house number, 19 are PO boxes); city span ..._Label1; state implicit AL; ZIP span ..._Label2 — 14,658 five-digit, 1,686 ZIP+4, 2,450 truncated like '36117-' (strip to 5 digits).
- **facility_id**: None exposed (no permit number). Use make_restaurant_id(name, address, city) (line 925); 39 name+address duplicate pairs (multiple permits) collapse to one id, handled by the one-inspection-per-day rule.
- **inspection_id**: None. Synthesize source_id like f'alabama_{county_id}_{restaurant_id}_{inspection_date}' (pattern of fetch_wake's 'wake_{hsis}_{insp_date}').
- **inspection_type**: Not available per record. Establishment TYPE is available only by filtering DrpEstdType (Madison: Food Service Establishment 1,149; Limited Food 635; Retail Food Store 73 of 2,460 'All'); Smoke Free Yes/No/blank is the only other attribute.
- **Weekly volume**: Roughly 650-1,050 inspections per week statewide (64 reachable counties): latest-inspection counts per ISO week Aug-Oct 2026 were 818, 955, 998, 1043, 870, 660, 937, 824, 647, 737 (a floor, since superseded inspections vanish); 1,180 dated in the last 30 days and 8,798 in the last 90 of the 2026-11-02 snapshot. Because the site is batch-refreshed, most weekly runs will see nothing new and then one run will see 2-3 weeks at once. ~18.8k establishments total (Madison 2,460; Baldwin 1,410; Montgomery 1,255; Tuscaloosa 998; Shelby 909; Lee 837).
- **Weekly requests**: 66 requests: 1 GET landing + 65 county POSTs (64 that work + the Tallapoosa retry), ~47 MB uncompressed / ~3-4 MB gzipped on the wire, ~17 s of server time measured. Optional +~5 type-filter POSTs per county (~325) if establishment type tags are wanted. Plus Census geocoder batches for new establishments only.
- **90-day backfill**: Same 66 requests, but a real 90-day backfill is impossible: the source holds one (latest) inspection per establishment, no history. One sweep yields the current snapshot — 18,794 inspections of which 8,798 fall in the last 90 days — and history accrues only from weekly diffs after that.
- **History**: None beyond the latest inspection per establishment (verified: 18,794 rows, 39 name+address repeats that are multiple permits, not history). Stale permits keep old dates (oldest 2017-02-02, Marengo); 18,456 of 18,794 rows are within the last 365 days.
- **Recommended strategy**: Build fetch_alabama() as a 66-request county sweep, not a date-window fetch: (1) GET https://foodscores.state.al.us/ once, follow the 302 to the /(S(...))/default.aspx session path, parse __VIEWSTATE/__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR/__EVENTVALIDATION and the DrpCnty option list (fail the group if the 67 options or the field names change). (2) For each county id except 37 and 49, POST the landing hidden fields + DrpEstdType=All + DrpCnty=<id> with gzip, allow_redirects=False, 1-2 s apart. (3) Per county, parse the DtList spans; ship the county ONLY if status 200, lblPageNumber is empty, and the parsed record count equals the 'Total Number:  N' label; otherwise (500 like Tallapoosa, 302, timeout, mismatch) drop the whole county and call _set_group_status('Alabama', 'error', '<county> <status>') so the run goes red rather than shipping a partial state silently — never emit a county from a partial parse. (4) Normalize: strip ZIP to 5 digits ('36117-' -> '36117'), state='AL', original_score=risk_score=int(LblScore), violations=[], inspection_type='', source_id=f'alabama_{county_id}_{make_restaurant_id(...)}_{date}', city from Label1, county from the query; parse M/D/YYYY through sane_inspection_date (line 457). (5) 'New since X' = client-side diff: keep only (restaurant id, inspection_date) pairs not already in data.js/D1 (the existing --skip-known-inspections machinery; the D1 upsert keyed restaurant+date is idempotent anyway); drop rows whose latest date is older than the stored one for that id. (6) Call _record_group('Alabama', records) and _note_source_lag('Alabama', max_date) (line 8426) every run: the site is batch-refreshed ~2-3 weeks behind, so a run with zero new rows is normal and must read as source lag, not failure; the self-healing lookback is irrelevant because every run re-reads the full snapshot. (7) Geocode new establishments through geocode_missing_coords (Census batch first); ~18.8k addresses on the first run (two 10k batches + Census2020 pass), a few hundred a week after; expect PO boxes and 'shopping center' addresses to stay unmapped. (8) Optionally one extra POST per county with DrpEstdType=Food Service Establishment (and Limited Food / Retail Food Store / Mobile Food Service) to tag establishment type and let the map exclude school lunchrooms, daycares and nursing homes; and a weekly GET of the closings page to force F for sewage/vermin closures the score site never lists. Measure the refresh cadence for a few weeks (diff two snapshots) before relying on weekly freshness.
- **Risks**:
  - Score-only source: no violation text, severity, or inspection type exists on the state site, so Jev has nothing to read, chips/summaries will be empty, no text-forced F is possible, and 99% of grades will be A (median score 97; only 156 of 18,794 below 85).
  - Latest-inspection snapshot only: no history, no backfill beyond the current snapshot; two inspections of one establishment inside one refresh interval lose the earlier one; a follow-up that restores a score erases the low score from the public record.
  - Batch publication lag: newest date 2026-10-16 on 2026-11-02 and 26 of 64 counties cut off on exactly that day; refresh cadence unverified — weekly runs will often ship nothing and must report it via _note_source_lag, not as an error.
  - Tallapoosa County (id 62) returns HTTP 500 persistently (20/20 attempts incl. every type filter and city Dadeville/Daviston); a fetcher must mark it error/skipped rather than treat it as empty, and the county is unshippable until ADPH fixes the row.
  - No stable facility or inspection ids and no coordinates: ids hash name+address+city, so a renamed or re-addressed permit becomes a new restaurant; 39 duplicate name+address permits collapse under one id; every establishment needs geocoding (~18.8k initially; 77 addresses lack a house number, 19 are PO boxes, 2,450 ZIPs are truncated '36117-').
  - Birmingham (Jefferson County, ~0.67M) and Mobile County (~0.41M) are excluded and redirect to separate portals (jcdh.org has violation-level detail pages; mobilecountyhealth.org 503s/resets TLS from this container) — each needs its own fetcher.
  - ASP.NET WebForms scraping fragility: cookieless session path, __VIEWSTATE/__EVENTVALIDATION echo, control ids (DtList_ctlNN_LblScore etc.) and the 'Total Number' label are all unversioned markup; a redesign silently breaks the parser (mitigated by the count==label and lblPageNumber-empty checks).
  - Each weekly sweep downloads ~47 MB uncompressed (~3-4 MB gzipped) from a small state server; no robots.txt or terms exist, and no rate limiting was observed in ~95 requests, but the site is clearly not built for bulk access — keep 1-2 s spacing and one pass per week.
  - City/name search filters are fuzzy prefix matches (Montgomery -> Montevallo/Monroeville); any scoping other than the county dropdown over- or under-fetches.
  - Date typos appear in the feed (Chambers County shows a Sunday 2026-10-18 inspection); sane_inspection_date catches only impossible dates.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://foodscores.state.al.us/` (none; follow the 302 to /(S(<session>))/default.aspx and read the hidden fields __VIEWSTATE (2,836 chars), __VIEWSTATEGENERATOR (CA0B0334), __EVENTVALIDATION (720 chars)) Landing/search form; supplies the session path and the hidden fields that every search POST must echo. Also lists the 67 county option values (1..67, alphabetical, ST.CLAIR=58; MADISON=45, JEFFERSON=37, MOBILE=49, MONTGOMERY=51, TUSCALOOSA=63) and 13 establishment types. [302 -> 200 (every GET; ~95 requests total this probe, never throttled)]
  - `POST https://foodscores.state.al.us/(S(<session>))/default.aspx` (__LASTFOCUS=, __EVENTTARGET=, __EVENTARGUMENT=, __VIEWSTATE, __VIEWSTATEGENERATOR, __EVENTVALIDATION (copied from the landing page — reusing the LANDING page's values for every POST works, even agains ...) County search = the fetch unit. Returns EVERY establishment in the county in one HTML response (no paging engaged): per record name (span ..._DtList_ctlNN_LblEst), address (a ..._LnkAdd), city (..._Label1), ZIP (..._Label2), official score (..._LblScore), Smoke Free (..._LblSmoke), latest inspection ... [200 for 64 of 67 counties (18,794 records, 47.4 MB uncompressed, 0.21-0.66 s each); 500 for TALLAPOOSA (id 62) persistently; 302 for JEFFERSON (37) and MOBILE (49)]
  - `POST https://foodscores.state.al.us/(S(<session>))/default.aspx with DrpCnty=62 (TALLAPOOSA)` (same as above, DrpCnty=62; also 500 with every one of the 13 DrpEstdType values and with txtCity=Dadeville or Daviston; txtCity=Alexander City -> 200) Documents the one broken county: ASP.NET 'Runtime Error' page, reproduced 20 times over 15 minutes — a bad row crashes the render, not a transient fault. [500 (all attempts)]
  - `POST https://foodscores.state.al.us/(S(<session>))/default.aspx with DrpCnty=37 (JEFFERSON) / DrpCnty=49 (MOBILE)` (same as above) Hand-off to the two independent county health departments: 302 Location https://webapps.jcdh.org/scores/ehfs/foodservicescores.aspx (which itself 302s to a broken Air Pollution page on jcdh.org) and 302 Location http://www.mobilecountyhealth.org/Default.asp?p='Restaurant' (503 / TLS reset from this  ... [302 (state site), then jcdh.org 200 on a wrong page; mobilecountyhealth.org 503 over http, connection reset over https]
  - `GET https://www.alabamapublichealth.gov/foodsafety/closings.html` (none) Optional supplement: plain-text list of closures in the last 90 days (75 food entries on 2026-10-28: name, street, city/state/zip, date, 'Purpose: ...' e.g. 'Sewage backing up from the dishwashing sink'); the score site never lists scores below 60, so closures only appear here. No CSV/XML. [200 (via WebFetch)]

Start Date' is 10/16/2026, and the facility list's 'Date of Most Recently Completed Inspection' includes inspections not yet public. Facility inspection grids are small (5-10 rows). Legacy: 15 establishments per page via __EVENTARGUMENT=Page$N postbacks (verified Page$2, 0 overlap), hard cap 500 records per search (30-day and no-criteria searches both returned exactly 500); daily statewide counts s ...
- **score**: None (legacy CSV column OverallInspectionScore is always empty). Risk must be computed from violations: PDF 57-item form with OUT marks.
- **grade**: None. Iowa publishes no letter grade.
- **violations**: SAFHER PDF 'OBSERVATIONS AND CORRECTIVE ACTIONS': Item Number, Regulatory Code (e.g. 5-205.11, 4-601.11(A), 3-501.16(A)(2)), Comment 'Observation: ... Corrected by: ...', Repeat, Correct-By; plus 'PUBLISHED COMMENTS' free text. Only inspections completed in SAFHER (since 2026-10-20) have a PDF; migrated history shows '-'. Legacy popup per inspection: code (lblRegulatorCodeType), code title (lblCodeExplanation), Inspector Comments '--Observation: ... Corrected by: ...' (risk-factor items only).
- **severity**: SAFHER PDF: items 1-29 'FOODBORNE ILLNESS RISK FACTORS' print 'OUT *' ((*) = Critical), items 30-57 'GOOD RETAIL PRACTICES' print plain 'OUT'; 'COS' = corrected on site; observations table has 'Repeat Violation' Yes/No and 'Correct-By Date'. Legacy popup: no flag, but it lists ONLY risk-factor items (5/5 sampled popups matched the 'Risk Factor Violations' count; no GRP code ever appeared).
- **coordinates**: SAFHER: none (geocode needed). Legacy portal with chkDisplayMap=on: page-script arrays var points=[new google.maps.LatLng(lat,lng),...] keyed by var keys=[InspectionSequenceKey,...] for every result (269 of 300 geocoded, 4 decimals) — usable to seed coordinates for establishments present in both systems (name+address match).

Legacy: NonFormattedNameAddress attribute 'NAME ADDRESS City, IA ZIP [phone]' plus the address <div>s.

Start Date: 10/16/2026 4:00 PM CDT' (true visit date); grid 'Completed Date' (sign-off datetime, days later); migrated rows show legacy dates as '6:00 PM CST'/'7:00 PM CDT' i.e. UTC midnight of the next day — convert, likely off by one otherwise (unverified). - **facility_id**: SAFHER grid identifiers (facility record id, e.g. 19549) and the PDF's 'Facility ID: 1558' / 'Program: 5562' (two different ids; which one is stable across relicensing is unverified). Legacy: InspectionSequenceKey (e.g. 210847).
- **inspection_id**: SAFHER: facility-view grid identifier = inspection id = PDF 'Insp. ID' (91380); PDF filename Inspection_<id>_<MMDDYYYY>.pdf; document id (278210) in the DocumentDownloadLink. Legacy: Key attribute / hfViolationInspectionKey (e.g. 230695).
- **inspection_type**: SAFHER grid 'Inspection Type' (Retail Food, Mobile Food) + 'Primary Reason' (Routine, Follow Up, Complaint, Pre-Opening); PDF 'Insp. Reason'. Legacy: Routine, Physical Recheck, Pre-Opening, Follow Up-Letter of Correction, Follow Up-Risk Control Plan, Non-Illness Complaint.

Des Moines ~33/week), Linn/Cedar Rapids ~20, Johnson/Iowa City ~15, Scott/Davenport ~7.
- **Weekly requests**: SAFHER weekly incremental: 1 shell GET + 1 page GET + 1 date-filter POST + ~12 paging POSTs + ~300 facility-view POSTs (315-455 KB each) + ~310 PDF GETs (~150 KB each) = roughly 620-650 requests, ~150-200 MB, ~15-20 min at 1-2 s per request (no throttling seen in ~45 probe requests; limits unverified).
- **90-day backfill**: SAFHER holds PDF-bearing inspections only from 2026-10-20: today that is ~600 inspections (~1,300 requests); a full 90 days once accumulated is ~3,900 inspections ≈ 7,500-8,000 requests (one facility POST + one PDF per inspection, plus paging). Legacy history (≤ 2026-10-14, risk-factor violations only, no PDF): ~90 daily statewide searches (map on) + ~270 Page$N postbacks + ~3,900 violations postbacks ≈ 4,300 requests of 200-600 KB each; its nested past-inspection grids reach back to 2021, so a multi-year backfill is the same walk over more popups.
- **History**: SAFHER: facility inspection grids list migrated inspections back to at least 2022 (dates, type, reason only — no report, no violations); violation detail (PDF) only for inspections completed in SAFHER, i.e. since 2026-10-20. Legacy: violation popups available for every listed inspection; oldest past inspection seen 2021-10-14 (~5 years). Inspections between 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-19 may be in neither public view (unverified).
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly incremental from SAFHER with a small stdlib (urllib+json) SAIL client, registered as its own fetch group (e.g. 'Iowa (SAFHER)'): (1) new session: GET the inspections shell (cookies), then GET /_/ui/page/g.public-search.p.inspections with the header set (X-Client-Mode: PORTALS etc.); treat 406/500 or a response without a PagingGridLayout as 'blocked' via _set_group_status, never as 0 records. (2) POST a SaveRequest on the DateRangeWidget with startDate = _lookback_since(...) but at least 21 days before the recorded latest shipped inspection (publication lags days to weeks) and never before 2026-10-20, endDate = today; read totalCount N. (3) Page the grid with pagingInfo startIndex 1, 26, 51, ... collecting each row's facility DynamicLink (_cId, saveInto, label) and identifier; completeness check #1: rows collected == totalCount, else hold the whole window (_hold_window_start) and report 'skipped'. (4) For each facility: POST its DynamicLink (from the filtered-list state; re-POST the filter or re-GET with the list's $sp token to return to the list), read the inspection rows; for every row whose date is in the window: if it has a DocumentDownloadLink, GET /content/<_documentDownloadLink> in the same session and parse the PDF (Insp. ID, Facility ID, Insp. Start Date as the inspection date, Reason, OUT items with (*) critical flag and COS, observations table); if it has no report yet ('-'), or the facility's 'most recently completed' date is in the window but no row shows that date, call _hold_freshness(source, that_date, counted=False, key=inspection_id) so the window re-opens next week instead of shipping clean. (5) Records: name/address/city/zip from the PDF header (county from the grid address), state IA, latitude/longitude None (geocode; seed from the legacy map arrays where name+address match), original_score None, risk_score computed like Omaha's from counts (critical OUT items 1-29 weighted as priority, GRP OUT 30-57 as core; repeat flag available), violations [category, severity, text] from the observations table, inspection_type from Primary Reason, source_id 'ia_<insp_id>', source_url the facility page with its $sp token (token lifetime unverified). Order facilities oldest-first so a deadline cut drops the newest tail, and pass counted=False for deadline/breaker drops. Sanity bound: a weekly totalCount outside ~100-600 or a run with 0 PDFs read reports 'error'. One-time legacy backfill (soon, before DIAL switches the portal off): per day 2021-10-14..2026-10-14 (or whatever history is wanted) a statewide search with chkDisplayMap=on (daily counts ≤ 80, far under the 500 cap; split by jurisdiction only if a day ever hits 500), parse the grid + nested past-inspection grids, then one exact-replay lnkViolations postback per inspection for the risk-factor violation text (mark these records as risk-factor-only), take lat/lng from the map arrays keyed by InspectionSequenceKey, and keep a name+address map from InspectionSequenceKey to SAFHER  ...
- **Risks**:
  - Undocumented, version-coupled protocol: the Appian SAIL wire format and the X-Appian-Features/-Extended header values come from the portal's JS bundle (portals-5f385766c073e0465068.cache.js); an Appian upgrade can change them and the page then answers 406 'Client not supported' or a 500 evaluation error — the fetcher must report blocked, and the probe scripts must be re-run to refresh the header values.
  - Fresh system (launched 2026-10-20): the public-search pages already throw a server-side expression error for any client missing X-Client-Mode: PORTALS; DIAL may change the public pages, rename stubs (g.public-search.p.inspections) or add filters without notice.
  - Publication lag and withholding: inspections appear days to weeks after the visit (10/16 visit signed 10/23 visible 11/02; a 10/28 inspection still absent on 11/02; news reports an 8-day disclosure hold) and the facility list's 'most recently completed' date includes unpublished ones — a window can look complete while PDFs are missing, so holds are mandatory and a 21-day lookback is the minimum.
  - Which date the DateRangeWidget filters on (visit vs completion vs publication) is unverified; mis-assuming it can skip inspections at window edges — use the PDF's Insp. Start Date and a generous lookback.
  - Violation detail only exists as a PDF per inspection: parsing must be stdlib (the pipeline already has stdlib PDF text parsers for Omaha/Orange County; this PDF is text-based and pdftotext extracted it cleanly), but layout changes break it silently — a parsed report with no Facility/Insp. ID or an empty 57-item form must count as unread, not as zero violations.
  - No official score or grade: risk must be computed from OUT counts; Iowa prints 57 items with (*) critical marks, so a new scoring map is needed and the regex/Jev pipeline must handle 'Observation: ... Corrected by: ...' text.
  - Stateful, chatty protocol: every facility view is a 315-455 KB POST and each PDF 150 KB (~150-200 MB a week); the PDF token is bound to the session that rendered it (cookie-less GET returned 500), so sessions must be kept and re-created on expiry; raw-HTTP paging of the 25-row grid was not exercised (shape inferred from the grid's pagingInfo).
  - Cloudflare fronts SAFHER (__cf_bm cookie): it accepted this container's egress; GitHub-hosted runners were not tested and the site's own relay only allows two portal hostnames, so a runner block would need an allowlist change.
  - Legacy portal is a one-time, time-limited opportunity: DIAL says USFS 'will no longer be accessible' for food establishments; its PDF button never returned a PDF to a non-browser client; it publishes risk-factor violations only (no Good Retail Practice items), caps searches at 500 records, and every postback must replay the previous form exactly or it 500s.
  - Inspections completed 2026-10-15..2026-10-19 may be in neither public view (unverified); migrated SAFHER history has no violations at all, so pre-cutover risk scores can only come from the legacy risk-factor lists.
  - Two facility ids (grid record id vs PDF 'Facility ID') and date/timezone quirks (grid 'Completed Date' is the sign-off; migrated rows display as 6:00/7:00 PM local = UTC midnight next day) can fragment restaurant ids or shift inspection dates by a day if not normalized.
  - Rate limits and terms of use unverified (no throttling in ~45 requests).
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://safher-portal.dial.iowa.gov/safher-portal/group/public-search/page/inspections` (none (plain HTML shell; sets Cloudflare __cf_bm cookie; its window.APPIAN.dataUrls["data-url"] names the UI endpoint below)) Start a session and discover the page's SAIL data URL [200]
  - `GET https://safher-portal.dial.iowa.gov/safher-portal/_/ui/page/g.public-search.p.inspections` (headers Accept: application/vnd.appian.tv.ui+json; Content-Type: application/vnd.appian.tv+json; X-Appian-Features: 7ffceebc; X-Appian-Features-Extended: 3fff7797ffdbff7f49dc1fffceebc; X-Appian-Ui-Sta ...) [200 with the headers above (455 KB JSON); 406 {"message":"Client not supported"} without X-Appian-* headers; 500 'Could not find variable env!clientMode' without X-Client-Mode: PORTALS (bodies in safher_page_endpoint_responses.json)]

[200 (1.2-1.4 s, 315-455 KB); 500 {"message":"Expected uiConfig to be of type Record."} for an empty body]

Start/End Date, Reason, 57-item IN/OUT form with (*) critical marks and COS, OBSERVATIONS table with item number, regulatory code, Observation/Corrected-by text, Repeat Violation, Correct-By Date, published comments) [200 application/pdf, Content-Disposition attachment; filename="Inspection_91380_10232026.pdf" (in-session); 500 HTML from a fresh cookie-less session]

## Long Beach and Pasadena, California (the two LA County citie

- **Feasibility**: high; reachable: True; blocked: Long Beach only: the city publishes no inspection results online (in-store 'Inspection Summary Report' placard since 1999; website has only program pages, a 12-month closures HTML table and a CPRA request portal at longbeachca.govqa.us); open-data catalog (16 datasets) has nothing on food/health; the city's ArcGIS org has only sidewalk-vendor enforcement rows; Accela ACA path -> 404; decadeonline.com dead. Pasadena is not blocked.
- **Population**: Pasadena city ~134k-139k (138,699 in the 2020 Census; 133,560 July-2023 estimate — from Census QuickFacts snippets in search results, unverified against census.gov which answered 403/'Missing Key'). Long Beach ~451k-467k (450,901 July-2024 estimate per a search-result list; 2020 Census ~466,742 from memory, unverified) — NOT coverable, no machine-readable source.
- **Overlap with existing coverage**: Essentially none. LA County's hub extracts exclude Pasadena, Long Beach and Vernon (pipeline comment at dinescores_pipeline.py ~line 3112, confirmed by the county's dataset notes). data.js today has 17 'Pasadena, CA', 10 'South Pasadena' and 1 'Long Beach' rows from the LA County feed — unincorporated Pasadena-addressed facilities / South Pasadena is a separate LA-County city — so new Pasadena ids would not collide with them (different addresses/jurisdiction).
- **Base URL**: https://healthinspectionreports.cityofpasadena.net (Pasadena only; Long Beach has no machine-readable source)
- **Auth**: None. Public ASP.NET Core (Kestrel) SPA; backend routes are POST + JSON body with no anti-forgery token, cookie or API key (the bundle's fetch helper sends only Content-Type: application/json with credentials:"include"). /GetMenuStatus reports isAuthenticated:false with optional staff logins that the public search does not need. robots.txt -> 404; SPA shell carries <meta name=robots content=noinde ...
- **Date filter**: None server-side (verified: unlisted routes SearchInspections/GetRecentInspections/GetInspectionsByDate/GetAllInspections -> 404; GET on data routes -> 405). Filter client-side on `InspectionActualStartDateTime` (current system, "2026-09-14T11:00:00", local time) or `InspectionDate` (legacy, "2025-07-21T00:00:00").
- **Pagination**: None effective. SearchPermits/SearchEnvisionPermits accept limit/offset but ignore them (limit=5 -> 976 rows, offset=1600 -> 976 rows); the whole matching list comes back in one response (976 food permits, ~235 KB; 948 legacy permits, 171 KB). GetInspections/GetEnvisionInspections return every inspection of one permit in one response.
- **score**: Official 0-100 (100 minus 1/2/4-point deductions, LA-County-style): current `ViolationScore` (routines 89-100 in sample; 0 on 'No Violations Observed' reinspections; two rows had -5/-1 with empty rating — treat <=0 as unscored); legacy `InspectionScore` (82-99 in sample) with `TotalViolationScore` = points deducted.
- **grade**: Placard, not letters: current `ViolationRating` ('Pass' / 'No Violations Observed' / ''), PDF 'Rating'. City thresholds (placard PDF): PASS 85-100, CONDITIONAL PASS 75-84, CLOSED <75/suspension; follow-up needs >=95. Legacy JSON has no placard field. DineScores grades from risk_score (score) as elsewhere.
- **violations**: Current: ONLY in the PDF 'Violation Details' section — item number, title, optional MAJ/MIN marker, 'Repeat Violation', 'Correct By' date, code text, inspector observation paragraph; parseable with the existing stdlib `_oc_pdf_text_rows`. Legacy: `Violations[]` with `ViolationCode` (K0xx), `ViolationItem` (title), `ViolationText` (code language), `InspectorComments` (observation).
- **severity**: Current: `MAJ` / `MIN` markers on critical-risk items 1-24 in the details and checklist; good-retail-practice items 25-49 are marked `OUT` with no MAJ/MIN -> core. Legacy: per-violation `Score` points (4 = major critical e.g. K007 holding temps, K014 contact surfaces, K023 vermin; 2 = minor critical; 1 = good practice; rare 12/26 values unverified).
- **coordinates**: None in any endpoint (unverified whether EnerGov holds them). Geocode via `geocode_missing_coords` (Census batch; 802 distinct street addresses in 6 zips).
- **address**: `FullAddressLine1` (street, may include suite) + `FullAddressLine2` ('PASADENA CA 91105' -> city, state, zip). PDF prints the same.
- **inspection_date**: Current: `InspectionActualStartDateTime` ISO local ('2026-09-14T11:00:00'); legacy: `InspectionDate` ('2025-07-21T00:00:00'). PDF header prints mm/dd/yyyy.
- **facility_id**: Current: `BlLicenseId` (GUID) plus `LicenseNumber` ('PF-25000721'); legacy: `BlLicenseId`=`LicenseNumber`=`RegistrationId` ('PR0002721'). Join legacy->current by normalized name+street (769/948 exact; 179 closed/renamed). DineScores id stays make_restaurant_id(DBA, FullAddressLine1, 'Pasadena').
- **inspection_id**: Current: `ImInspectionId` (GUID) and human `InspectionNumber` ('ROUT-26011108'); legacy: no id — key on permit+`InspectionDate` (matches D1's restaurant+date key).
- **inspection_type**: Current: `InspectionType` ('Routine Inspection' 97%, 'Reinspection', 'Permit/Owner Investigation') + `InspectionStatus` ('Complete', 'Reinspection Required'); legacy: `InspectionType`/`Service` ('ROUTINE INSPECTION' code 100, 'INSPECTION FOLLOW-UP' 102, 'BILLABLE FOLLOW-UP' 104, 'COMPLAINT INVESTIGATION').
- **Weekly volume**: Pasadena: ~22 inspections/week (976 food permits x ~1.19 routine inspections/permit/year measured over 2025-11-22..2026-10-28 in a 150-permit sample; plus ~134 food trucks/carts if wanted). Long Beach: 0 (no source; its closures table adds ~2 closures/week).
- **Weekly requests**: ~1,000: 1 SearchPermits (whole list) + 976 GetInspections (~4.6 min sequential at 0.28 s, or ~1.2 min with 4 workers) + ~22 DownloadReport PDFs (~13 MB). No date filter exists, so the enumeration is the floor.
- **90-day backfill**: ~1,270: the same 977 enumeration requests + ~290 PDFs (~165 MB) for the ~13 weeks of inspections. Full history is cheaper than that: 1 SearchEnvisionPermits + 948 GetEnvisionInspections (~4 min, no PDFs) yields ~8,400 structured inspections 2014-2025; the current system itself only goes back to 2025-11-22.
- **History**: Current system: 2025-11-22 onward (feed current to 2026-10-28 on probe day 2026-11-02; 29% of sampled permits have no inspection in it yet). Legacy Envision endpoint: 2014-01-28 .. 2025-12-03 in a 70-permit sample (~2-week overlap with the current system). Long Beach closures table: rolling 12 months only.
- **Recommended strategy**: PASADENA (new fetch group `pasadena`, source 'Pasadena Public Health'):
1) Enumerate: POST /Inspection/SearchPermits {"keyword":"%","permitType":"Permanent Food",...}; assert Success and Total == len(Rows) and Total >= ~900 (sanity floor; a response of a few hundred rows means the wildcard broke — fall back to keywords a-z/0-9 deduped on BlLicenseId, or report 'error', never ship a partial list). Optionally a second call with permitType null filtered to 'Food Truck/Cart'.
2) For every permit (all 976, every week, because there is no date filter): POST /Inspection/GetInspections {"entityId": BlLicenseId}. Keep inspections with InspectionActualStartDateTime >= `_lookback_since('pasadena', ...)` (the day before the recorded latest shipped inspection, capped at 400 days like other API sources) AND whose ImInspectionId is not already shipped (--skip-known-inspections style). This is the 'new since X' test: cheap (JSON, ~0.3 s each) and complete, since every permit is visited.
3) For each new inspection with an AttachmentId: POST /Inspection/DownloadReport (form fields ImInspectionId, AttachmentId, FileName, DBA) and parse the PDF with `_oc_pdf_text_rows`: read Score/Rating/Date from the header (must equal the JSON's ViolationScore/date or drop the record), the 49-item checklist statuses, and the 'Violation Details' items (title, MAJ/MIN -> priority/priority_foundation, no marker on items >=25 -> core, observation text -> violation text for Jev). Severity from the checklist status when the details line has no marker. Ship score = ViolationScore (official 0-100, do not re-score; Pasadena is NOT a tier source), results = ViolationRating, inspection_type = InspectionType.
4) Never ship a partial window silently: an inspection ships only once its PDF is read and parsed with a Score and a Facility Name (same rule as Orange County's printable); a PDF that fails (non-200, non-%PDF, parse without Score) is dropped and held with `_hold_freshness('Pasadena Public Health', date, key=ImInspectionId)` so the lookback re-reads it next run; inspections with no AttachmentId (3/165) or ViolationScore <= 0 / empty rating (reinspections, 'No Violations Observed', the two negative rows) are skipped as unscored, not held. If any GetInspections call fails (non-200/exception) after one retry, hold at that run's window start via `_hold_window_start` and `_set_group_status('pasadena','partial',...)` so the run goes red; if SearchPermits fails or returns an implausible Total, `_record_group` with status 'error' and ship nothing. Register with `_record_group('pasadena', records)`; `_note_source_lag` is not needed (feed is current). Process permits oldest-latest-first is unnecessary (all are visited), but process PDFs oldest-first so a deadline cut drops the newest tail.
5) Geocode: records carry no coordinates; `geocode_missing_coords` (Census batch) on Pasadena addresses; coalesce as the merge paths already do.
6) One-time history seed (separate --mode or script, not weekly): P ...
- **Risks**:
  - No server-side date filter: every weekly run must call GetInspections for all ~976 permits (~1,000 requests); a change that enforces `limit` (currently ignored) or breaks the '%' wildcard would silently shrink the enumeration — guard with a Total/row-count floor and fall back to a-z keyword sweeps.
  - Violations exist only in a ~570 KB SSRS-generated PDF per inspection; a layout change (item numbering, 'Violation Details' heading, MAJ/MIN markers) would blank violations — require header Score + Facility Name parsed and fail closed (hold the inspection), as Orange County does.
  - Current-system data only begins 2025-11-22; the legacy Envision endpoint (2014-2025) is a separate, probably frozen system that could be switched off at any time — seed history from it soon.
  - Legacy->current identity: 179 of 948 legacy permits do not match a current DBA/address (closed or renamed), so legacy history for renamed places lands under a separate restaurant id; multi-permit addresses (77 current, 63 legacy, e.g. supermarket deli/bakery counters, hotel outlets) share one DineScores id — `_same_day_rank` picks the worst per day.
  - Reachability from GitHub-hosted runners is unverified (all probes ran from this container's proxy egress); the relay (`functions/api/relay.js`) only allows the two MHD/SNHD hosts, so a runner block would need the allowlist extended.
  - Rate limits/terms unknown: ~270 requests in minutes drew no 403/429 and no terms-of-use text exists on the site, but a weekly ~1,000-request sweep plus ~13 MB of PDFs is far above human usage; keep concurrency low (<=4) and a polite delay.
  - Two of 165 sampled inspections carried negative ViolationScore with an empty rating and 3 had no AttachmentId — meaning unverified; treat as unscored to avoid false F's.
  - No coordinates in any endpoint; Pasadena suites ('300 E COLORADO BLVD 214') may lower Census batch geocoder hit rates — coalesce lat/lng and let the Nominatim fallback catch stragglers.
  - Long Beach (~450k people) remains uncoverable: no results portal, open-data portal has no food datasets and its Opendatasoft host resets most connections from this container, Decade portal dead, records only via CPRA (GovQA).
