- **Risks**:
  - Harris County reachability from GitHub-hosted runners is UNVERIFIED (only this container was tested, 200 everywhere); if the host 403s runners, the relay allowlist ALLOWED_HOSTS (functions/api/relay.js:24-27) must grow to include secureweb.hcphtx.org and a relay redeploy is needed before the weekly job can use it.
  - Harris County's API is an undocumented internal app API with no terms page; no rate limiting was observed in ~40 calls, but a weekly run needs ~600 requests and a 90-day backfill ~7,800 — pace them and watch for the first 403/429, with a breaker like SNHD's.
  - Harris County rows carry owner PII (ownerName, ownerPrimaryEmailAddress, ownerPrimaryPhone, ownerIdentificationNumber, salesTaxId) and inspector names; the fetcher must whitelist fields and never log raw rows; the saved samples were scrubbed.
  - Harris County: no cross-establishment inspection list by date exists (api/inspection/search -> 405); completeness depends on the establishment search's lastCompletedInspectionDate filter, which is the inspection date not the publish date, so late-entered inspections older than the lookback would be missed — use a >= 14-day minimum lookback and monitor dateUtc lag.
  - Harris County: only 'Active' permits are searchable (status group: Active 9138), so an establishment that closes or changes permit may vanish with its history; coordinates exist on only 39/9,138 establishments so geocoding ~300 addresses/week (and ~3,700 for a backfill) is on the critical path — never ship without coalescing coords.
  - Harris County violations are item TITLES only (no observation narrative), so Jev/regex F-forcing on vermin text will rarely fire; severity/demerits are explicit instead. Score == 100 - demerits was verified on 4 inspections only.
  - Harris County: 'Permitting', 'Non-permitted' and 'Consultation' inspection types carry scores (a 'Permitting' inspection scored 59); shipping them as grades is a policy choice to make deliberately (Fort Bend's 'Permit' purpose rows likewise score 84-99).
  - Fort Bend: ~50 inspections/week is small (and its history only starts 2025-03-07); the MHD detail-page ban (~250 pages/15 min) governs any backfill; the fbceh landing page states no score scale, so the '100' classification rests on data evidence (100 <-> 0 observations, 72 <-> 6) rather than a published legend.
  - Fort Bend rows with city 'Sugar Land', 'Missouri City', 'Stafford', 'Houston' are presumably unincorporated mailing addresses (those cities run their own programs) — unverified; a reader may expect those cities to be 'covered' when only their unincorporated fringes are.
  - MHD robots.txt disallows ~40 named AI/SEO crawler agents (ClaudeBot, GPTBot, CCBot...) with no wildcard rule — the same policy the pipeline already operates under for every MHD jurisdiction; the HCPH host publishes no robots.txt or terms (404), so no explicit permission or prohibition exists.
  - Pasadena TX, Baytown, Sugar Land, Missouri City, Stafford and Rosenberg remain uncovered even after adding both sources (own city programs; not probed).

- **Population**: Williamson County, TX: ~727k (Census QuickFacts 7/1/2024 per search summary; ~753k 2025 est.; not verified first-hand). WCCHD's feed conversely holds 337 'Austin' and 61 'P ...
- **Base URL**: https://storage.googleapis.com/ehyelp/WCCHD%20Food%20Inspections.zip (public-read GCS object linked from https://www.wcchd.org/healthy_spaces/inspection_scores.php; the page's own link carries a stray '1' — '.zip1' — which 403s)
- **Auth**: None for the zip: anonymous public-read Google Cloud Storage object (bucket 'ehyelp' is not listable: 403/401). The Quickbase portal path needs a Quickbase user token that the district hard-codes in its public QB.js page (pageID=4) for its own widget; the classic XML API without it answers errcode 24 'Invalid Application Token'. Do not build on that token (classifier denied exercising it; it is a  ...
- **Date filter**: No server-side filter: the zip is a complete snapshot. Filter client-side on inspections.csv `date` (string YYYYMMDD, e.g. '20261028'); verified by parsing all 6,734 rows (0 unparseable; min 2021-12-14, max 2026-10-28). feed_info.xlsx `feed_date` (20261029) gives the snapshot date; the HTTP Last-Modified/ETag give a cheap no-change check (cache-control: public, max-age=3600).
- **Pagination**: None. One 258,656-byte zip (1,049,511 bytes uncompressed) is the whole dataset: 12,145 permits, 6,734 inspections. The Quickbase page would page by options.skip over metadata.totalRecords, but that path is not recommended.
- **score**: inspections.csv `score`: integer Inspection Score on a 100-point scale (= 100 − demerits per the Quickbase page text: 'Number of Demerits > 30 is a failed inspection; Inspection Score ... below 70 is a failure'). Fail <=> score < 70 holds for all 606 Fail rows. 8 rows have negative scores (−10 .. −532, 2022–2023, all Fail) — clamp to 0. Demerits themselves are only in the Quickbase table (fid 11 numberOfDemerits), not in the zip.
- **grade**: None. `result` ∈ {Pass 6,066, Fail 606, IHH 62}; IHH = 'Imminent Health Hazard' closure 'not score-related' (42 of 62 IHH rows have score ≥ 70) — policy question whether to force F like a severe-violation text (recommended: yes, it is a closure).
- **violations**: None in the feed (no violations.csv; LIVES optional). The Quickbase public select list has no violation text either (fids 3,6,7,9,10,11,12,13,15,18,20,21). Unverified whether other fields exist (fields API not exercised). Scores-only source, like Austin.
- **severity**: None.
- **coordinates**: None (no latitude/longitude, no postal_code, no phone columns) — geocode address+city+'TX' with `geocode_census_batch` (dinescores_pipeline.py:7661) / `geocode_missing_coords` (:7723), as Austin does; ~2,200 distinct inspected addresses for a backfill, a handful per week after.
- **address**: businesses.csv `address` (street only; all 2,203 inspected businesses have a street number), `city` (84 raw spellings; case varies 'ROUND ROCK'/'Round Rock'/'leander'; one 'TAYLOR,' — title-case and strip punctuation), `state` always 'TX'. No zip.
- **inspection_date**: inspections.csv `date` YYYYMMDD (weekdays only; 12 weekend rows of 6,734).
- **facility_id**: `business_id` = permit number, e.g. '2026-1214-FFE', 'RR-1410', 'CP-0233', 'GE-761', 'LES-29-ISD'. Stable per permit but re-permitting assigns a new id (old 'RR-####' style declining, 'YYYY-####-FFE' growing in 2026), so use it only in source_id; DineScores' make_restaurant_id(name,address,city) is unaffected. 22 inspected ids (0.3% of rows) have no businesses.csv row — drop them.
- **inspection_id**: None; compose business_id + date. 19 same business_id+date duplicate pairs exist (e.g. RR-1410 2025-06-06: 65 Fail Routine then 84 Pass Followup) — `_same_day_rank` keeps the worst, consistent with the one-per-day invariant.
- **inspection_type**: inspections.csv `type` ∈ {Routine 5,725, Followup 1,009}. Business type (fid 7 typeOfBusiness) only in Quickbase.
- **Weekly volume**: ~12 inspections/week over the 12 full weeks 2026-08-03..2026-10-26 (143 rows); ~25/week over the trailing 365 days (1,289 rows); 30–50/week in Jan–Apr 2026 (130–211/month); 2025 averaged ~147/month (~34/week). The 2026-H2 feed volume (Jun 64, Jul 75, Aug 69, Sep 40, Oct 38) is about a third of 2025's same months — cause unverified (publishing lag of the public flag, system migration to new permit ids, or fewer inspections).
- **Weekly requests**: 1 (conditional GET of the 258 KB zip; HEAD/If-None-Match first costs 1 more) + Census batch geocoder calls for addresses not yet geocoded (1 POST per ≤10,000 addresses; a few new permits per week). No per-record requests at all.
- **90-day backfill**: 1 — the same zip already holds the full history since 2021-12-14 (a 90-day window is ~150–300 rows of it) — plus geocoding of the ~2,200 distinct inspected addresses on first load (1 Census batch POST; `geocode_missing_coords` caps Nominatim fallbacks at 150).
- **History**: 2021-12-14 → 2026-10-28 in the 2026-10-29 snapshot (page says 'data beginning from 1/1/22'): 6,734 inspections across 2,225 permits (mean 3.03 per permit; max 31). Per-year rows roughly: 2022 ~900, 2023 ~1,300, 2024 ~1,700, 2025 ~1,770, 2026 ~1,035 through Oct. Whether the zip will keep growing or roll off old years: unverified (one observation).
- **Recommended strategy**: Add `fetch_williamson()` (Austin-style scores-only API fetcher, stdlib zipfile+csv+openpyxl-free: feed_info.xlsx can be ignored or read via zipfile+xml): (1) HEAD/GET https://storage.googleapis.com/ehyelp/WCCHD%20Food%20Inspections.zip with If-None-Match = last ETag; on 304 report 'no change' with the previously recorded feed_date as the source lag via _note_source_lag (not a failure). (2) Require all three members (feed_info.xlsx, businesses.csv, inspections.csv) and a parseable feed_date; refuse the snapshot if inspections.csv has fewer rows than the last run's count minus a small tolerance, or its max date is older than the last shipped latest (a truncated/rolled-back feed must not ship), or >1% of rows fail to parse. (3) Load businesses.csv into dict by business_id; iterate inspections.csv, keep rows with date >= _lookback_since (self-healing window; the file is a full snapshot so re-reading a wide window is free), drop rows whose business_id has no businesses.csv row or no name, title-case name/city, strip the trailing comma city, clamp score to 0..100, map result: Pass/Fail by score, IHH -> force F (closure) [owner policy], type -> inspection_type, source_id f'wcchd_{business_id}_{date}', state 'TX', zip '' and lat/lng None -> downstream geocode_missing_coords (Census batch). (4) Completeness is structural: one atomic file, no pages, so 'partial window' can only mean a stale or truncated snapshot — record the feed_date and newest inspection in the freshness file, _note_source_lag(feed_date) when it is older than ~10 days, and _set_group_status error when the row-count/max-date guards fail. (5) Register the group with _record_group ('Williamson County'); cost is 1 request/week, so it can run first, outside the MHD/SNHD deadline budget. Do not use the Quickbase user token; instead email eh@wilco.org (feed_info contact) asking for the LIVES optional postal_code/latitude/longitude columns and a violations.csv (standard LIVES files) — that would add violation text and remove the geocoding dependency. Hays County: no fetcher possible now; revisit only if Hays County or San Marcos publish a portal (Hays County site is Cloudflare-WAF-blocked from this container, so verify from a GitHub runner or by hand).
- **Risks**:
  - Scores only: no violation text/severity, no demerit column, no coordinates, no zip in the zip — every record needs geocoding (Census batch, Austin precedent) and gets no infraction chips; grades come from score (A/B/C/F) plus a policy decision on IHH closures.
  - Unexplained volume drop in the public feed since 2026-06 (38–75 rows/month vs 120–210 in the same months of 2025) — could be a publishing lag on the Quickbase public flag (fid 19), the permit re-numbering migration, or real; a fetcher must treat the feed's own newest date as source lag, and a second snapshot should be compared month-by-month before trusting the series.
  - Feed cadence unverified: one observation only (Last-Modified 2026-10-29 15:56 GMT, feed_date 20261029, newest inspection 2026-10-28, metageneration 2). It is a Yelp LIVES-style file on a GCS bucket with no SLA; the official page even links the wrong filename (.zip1 -> 403), so the object could be renamed or removed without notice — make the fetcher fail loudly on 403/404.
  - Permit ids change on re-permitting (old 'RR-####'/'CP-####'/'GE-###' vs new 'YYYY-####-FFE'), so source_id continuity breaks; DineScores ids depend on name+address+city text, whose casing varies run to run ('ROUND ROCK' vs 'Round Rock') — normalize before make_restaurant_id or restaurants will split.
  - Data quality edges: 8 negative scores (demerits >100 artifacts), 22 inspected business_ids with no businesses.csv row, 19 same-day duplicate rows (a Followup Pass hours after a Fail will be hidden by the worst-of-day rule), 127 unnamed permits (none inspected).
  - Form change on 2026-11-01 (47-item -> 56-item DSHS report): WCCHD says demerits still max 100 with 31+ failing, so the score scale should not shift, but the first post-Nov-1 rows are unverified.
  - Quickbase path is the only place demerits/typeOfBusiness live, but it rides a user token hard-coded in the district's public widget — using it from a scraper was denied by the permission classifier and is a credential/terms risk; the classic API refuses anonymous calls (errcode 24).
  - Hays County half of the candidate is blocked: hayscountytx.gov is behind Cloudflare's WAF (403 to this container and to WebFetch), no score portal, PDF or dataset is indexed anywhere, San Marcos' city site has no scores page, and the one aggregator claiming a San Marcos scores search also 403s — zero Hays coverage from this probe.
  - GCS reachability from a GitHub-hosted runner not tested from here (public GCS objects normally are reachable); this container also reaches MHD, which runners do not, so container results do not prove runner results.
- **Endpoints**:
  - `GET https://storage.googleapis.com/ehyelp/WCCHD%20Food%20Inspections.zip` (none (conditional GET with If-None-Match on etag "64002b2b6ed04fbe80149ce346bd88fc" recommended); 258,656 bytes; Last-Modified Thu, 29 Oct 2026 15:56:36 GMT; x-goog-generation 1793289396262238) THE data source: Yelp LIVES-style bulk feed. Zip holds businesses.csv (business_id,name,address,city,state; 12,145 rows), inspections.csv (business_id,date YYYYMMDD,score 0-100,result Pass|Fail|IHH,type Routine|Followup; 6,734 rows, 2021-12-14..2026-10-28) and feed_info.xlsx (feed_date 20261029, fee ... [200]
  - `GET https://storage.googleapis.com/ehyelp/WCCHD%20Food%20Inspections.zip1` (none) The URL exactly as linked on wcchd.org (typo). Also tried bucket listing https://storage.googleapis.com/ehyelp/ (403) and https://storage.googleapis.com/storage/v1/b/ehyelp/o (401): object is public, bucket not listable [403 AccessDenied]
  - `GET https://www.wcchd.org/healthy_spaces/inspection_scores.php` (none) Official 'Inspection Scores & Permits' page: embeds two Quickbase dashboards, links the mobile Quickbase page and the zip; states the demerit scale (47 items, 1-3 demerits; <31 pass, >30 fail; IHH = closure not score-related) [200]
  - `GET https://wcchd.quickbase.com/db/bt8m9jeu9?a=dbpage&pageID=2 (+ pageID=3 search JS, pageID=4 QB.js wrapper)` (none) Public 'Food Service Inspection Records' Quickbase code page. pageID=3 queries alias _dbid_inspections = table bv7giszuf with where {19.EX.'1'} (fid 19 = public flag), select fids 3 recordId, 6 businessId, 7 typeOfBusiness, 9 businessAddress, 10 inspectionType, 11 numberOfDemerits, 12 inspectionResu ... [200 (code pages); records/query with the embedded token NOT exercised — permission classifier denied it as access circumvention]

Rule out an MHD jurisdiction (would have ridden the existing relay machinery) [200 with {'err': True, 'msg': 'bad request'} for all four slugs; dallas control 200 with 25 records. The SPA shell GET /<anyslug> returns 200/337,031 B for every slug incl. '/', so it proves nothing]

## DFW cities without a portal on MyHealthDepartment

Probed 2026-11-03 (live verification from the container, via the agent proxy); previous agent's captures 2026-11-02. Shared vendors: Mesquite's Hedgerow portal (/api/v0/portal/disclosure/... routes, program id from siteInfo menuItems menuType 5) is the same vendor product as the Lubbock and Tucson/Pima portals listed as candidates in CLAUDE.md. VERIFIED 2026-11-03 for Lubbock: https://clhd-portal.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/siteInfo ('City of Lubbock Environmental Health Department') lists disclosure programs 'Food Inspection Results' (d88b111d-edad-49f8-83a8-8c1d7931f99b), 'Body Art' and 'Recreational Water', and POST disclosure/program/facilities returns facilities (25 rows for pageSize 25 - Lubbock honours pageSize where Mesquite ignores it, so the shared fetcher must page until a short page AND tolerate an all-in-one response). The Tucson/Pima host name is unverified (tucson./pima./pimacounty.hedgerowsoftware.com do not resolve). One parametrized fetcher (portal host + program id) can serve Mesquite and Lubbock today. McKinney is a plain ArcGIS MapServer layer query - reuse the existing ArcGIS/FeatureServer request code (LA County inventory) and the DATE 'YYYY-MM-DD' where-clause pattern; Irving's stale data is ArcGIS too (hosted FeatureServer), so a revived Irving source would reuse the same code. De ...

### Irving

- **Feasibility**: blocked; population ~257k (2020 census 256,684; 2024 est. ~259k); vendor: ArcGIS Online hosted tables (org OfsJXUlu8pSkbl7B, both stale); city CMS (CivicPlus) behind a Cloudflare JavaScript challenge
- **Base URL**: https://services3.arcgis.com/OfsJXUlu8pSkbl7B/arcgis/rest/services
- **Date filter**: Food_Establishment_Scores: LATEST_INSPECTION_DATE is an nvarchar 'M/D/YYYY' string, no server-side date filter possible. RestaurantInspections_gdb: inspdate is esriFieldTypeDate (filterable) but the layer is a 2024 snapshot.
- **score**: none numeric. LATEST_SCORE / latestscore are letter grades (A+, A, B+, B, C+, C, D, F; 3 blank). The 'Score' double in Scores_Geocoded is the geocoder match score, not an inspection score.
- **violations**: none
- **severity**: none
- **coordinates**: none in Food_Establishment_Scores (a table). Scores_Geocoded has point geometry (X/Y in a State Plane SR) for 565 of ~1,000 establishments, 2024 snapshot.
- **inspection_date**: LATEST_INSPECTION_DATE, PREVIOUS_INSPECTION_DATE (strings M/D/YYYY, 2020 table); inspdate / USER_LATEST_REVIEW_DATE (epoch ms, 2024 layer)
- **Weekly volume**: unverified (no live source; ~1,000 permitted establishments per the 2020 table; at 2 routine inspections/yr that would be ~40/week)
- **Weekly requests**: n/a (no live source)
- **History**: latest + previous inspection only, frozen at 2020-02-03 (table) / 2024-06-05 (geocoded layer)
- **Recommended strategy**: Do not build now. No current machine-readable Irving source was found: both ArcGIS items are abandoned snapshots and the city's live scores page sits behind a Cloudflare JS challenge (would need a browser or the relay; relay fetch is unverified). Re-check quarterly with the AGOL org search above (orgid:OfsJXUlu8pSkbl7B) and the hub; if a new Feature Service appears with an esriFieldTypeDate inspection date, reuse the ArcGIS-layer fetcher pattern recommended for McKinney. Ask Irving Health Inspections (cityofirving.org) whether an export exists.
- **Risks**:
  - Both discovered datasets are stale (2020-02-03 and 2024-06-05); shipping them would show 2-6 year old grades as current.
  - Letter grades only, no numeric score, no violations.
  - City website requires JavaScript (Cloudflare challenge) from this container; the embedded scores app URL is unverified.

### McKinney (Collin County)

- **Feasibility**: high; population ~195k (2020 census 195,308; 2024 est. ~220k); vendor: City-hosted Esri ArcGIS Server MapServer (maps.mckinneytexas.org) fed from the permitting system (HEALTH####-##### permit numbers, FOOD-######-YYYY inspection numbers); public AGOL Instant App 'Food Permits Inspections'
- **Base URL**: https://maps.mckinneytexas.org/mckinney/rest/services/MapServices/FoodPermitsAndInspections/MapServer
- **Date filter**: VERIFIED server-side: actual_end_date (esriFieldTypeDate, epoch ms) with where=actual_end_date >= DATE '2026-10-27' (29 rows) and TIMESTAMP '2026-10-27 00:00:00' (29 rows); yearly buckets 2019..2026 = 1799/1653/1640/1882/1935/1830/1782/1621.
- **score**: total_score (integer DEMERIT points, lower is better; 0-41 observed, one outlier 101). VERIFIED on 3 rows that total_score == sum of the 47 per-item point columns. Official 100-point score is unverified (the PDF report in table 5 may print one); mapping 100 - total_score is the natural candidate but needs calibration before shipping.
- **violations**: custom_comments free text ('Violations:\n#9. Observed raw chicken over raw eggs...'), plus 47 per-item integer point columns named by item (temp_cold_holding, no_insect_contamination, toxic_substances, approved_sewage, ...). comments field exists (empty in samples).
- **severity**: per-item points: 3 (priority items: temps, hands, toxic, sewage, insects...), 2, 1; critical_violations count exists on layer 0 permits
- **coordinates**: geometry x/y (request outSR=4326) and POINT_X/POINT_Y strings on layer 4. VERIFIED gaps: 6,613 of 17,035 inspections (39%) and 604 of 1,796 in the last 365 days (34%) have no POINT_X. Layer 0 (permits) has coordinates for 709 of 738 (96%); joining link_number recovered coordinates for 16 of 20 sampled null-geometry inspections (the other 4 permit numbers, e.g. HEALTH2017-000634, are not in layer 0 ...
- **inspection_date**: actual_end_date (epoch ms, UTC-flagged local time), also actual_start_date, requested_date, date_entered
- **Weekly volume**: ~30-35 inspections/week (29 in the last 7 days; 1,796 in 365 days; 1,650-1,935 per year 2019-2026)
- **Weekly requests**: 2-3: one returnCountOnly, one data query (<2,000 rows), optionally one layer-0 permits query for coordinates
- **History**: 2017-10-16 .. 2026-10-30 (17,035 inspections), newest row 4 days old at probe time
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly: (1) returnCountOnly with where actual_end_date >= (_lookback_since - 1 day); (2) page the same where with orderByFields=OBJECTID, resultOffset, outSR=4326 until exceededTransferLimit is absent; abort and report the group 'error' (never ship) if rows received != count or any page fails; (3) fetch layer 0 once (738 rows, 1 request) and coalesce coordinates by link_number where geometry is null; (4) parse custom_comments after 'Violations:' into lines (#NN. ...) for Jev, keep per-item point columns as severity (3-pt items -> major); (5) score: calibrate 100 - total_score against the inspection PDFs (table 5) on ~20 inspections before enabling the grade; until then ship as a bounded-tier/score-from-points source. Register with _record_group; latest date = max actual_end_date shipped. No holds needed except on page failure (_hold_window_start).
- **Risks**:
  - 34-39% of inspection rows carry null geometry; the layer-0 join fixes ~80% of those (sample of 20), the rest need geocoding or the previous run's coordinates (coalesce).
  - total_score is demerit points; the official score mapping is unverified - a wrong mapping paints false grades.
  - Rows with null purpose_of_inspection (112) and non-routine purposes must be handled; only 'Approved' status seen - whether failed/closed inspections appear is unverified.
  - No editingInfo/last-edit metadata on the layer; freshness must be inferred from max(actual_end_date).
  - City GIS server (single host, 5 s statistics calls) - no SLA; MapServer is not a hosted Feature Service, so no sync/changes endpoint.

### Lewisville (Denton County)

- **Feasibility**: high; population ~112k (2020 census 111,822; 2024 est. ~135k); vendor: City of Lewisville custom JSON web service (query.cityoflewisville.com/v2 '?webservice=Health/...') behind a Vue app at eservices.cityoflewisville.com
- **Base URL**: https://query.cityoflewisville.com/v2/
- **Date filter**: VERIFIED server-side: DateBegin/DateEnd (ISO 'YYYY-MM-DD' or 'M/D/YYYY'); results are capped to the trailing 365 days (older windows return empty, not an error).
- **score**: Score (string): numeric 70-100 observed; also 'NO SCORE' and 'PASS' (non-numeric, 2 values seen in 365 days)
- **violations**: none
- **severity**: none
- **coordinates**: Lat / Lng (floats; 0 of 1,146 rows missing)
- **inspection_date**: InspectionDate ISO with 'Z' suffix but local wall-clock time (e.g. 2026-11-02T13:44:00.000Z); LastImported shows the feed lag (minutes)
- **Weekly volume**: ~22-27 inspections/week (27 in the last 7 days; 1,146 in 365 days)
- **Weekly requests**: 1 GET (plus 0-1 retry)
- **History**: rolling 365 days only (verified: 2024-Q1 and 2019 return 0 rows)
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly: one GET for DateBegin=(_lookback_since - 1 day, floored at today-364) .. DateEnd=tomorrow. Ship only when HTTP 200, body parses, has a 'projects' list and no 'BusList'[...]['Error_ID'] marker; an empty 'projects' for a window that previously had rows is suspicious -> report 'error' rather than advance. Because there is no count endpoint, require that every InspectionID already shipped for the overlapping days reappears (the window overlaps by design) before advancing the recorded latest; otherwise hold with _hold_window_start. Drop 'NO SCORE'/'PASS' rows (or map PASS like Orange County's Pass, a policy call). No violations -> grade from score only (Jev has nothing to read). Because history is 365 days, a backfill must run now; an outage longer than a year loses data permanently.
- **Risks**:
  - TLS: the API host serves only its leaf certificate (GoDaddy G2 intermediate missing; verified with openssl 2026-11-03). Python ssl and curl fail verification until the public intermediate (certs.godaddy.com/repository/gdig2.crt, sha256 97:3A:41:27:...) is added to the trust store; behaviour on the GitHub runner is unverified. Ship gdig2.pem in data/ and load it with load_verify_locations - never d ...
  - Undocumented v2 web service; could change without notice.
  - Scores only - no violation text, no inspection type; grade derived from score.
  - 365-day retention; InspectionDate 'Z' is really local time.

### Mesquite (Dallas County)

- **Feasibility**: high; population ~150k (2020 census 150,108); portal zips also list Balch Springs 75180 and Sunnyvale 75182 (whether those facilities are city-inspected is unverified); vendor: Hedgerow Software public 'Portal' (Angular, appVersion 5.15.2.6253) over a Web API at /api/v0/portal - same product family as the Hedgerow portals noted for Tucson/Pima and Lubbock in CLAUDE.md (Lubbock's route compatibility verified live 2026-11-03; the Tucson/Pima host is unverified)
- **Base URL**: https://mesquite.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/ (identical answers from https://msqt-app-prod.hedgerowsoftware.com/api/v0/portal/)
- **Date filter**: none. No server-side date or changed-since filter on facilities or inspections (a filters key is ignored); incremental = diff of per-facility inspection lists.
- **score**: hazardScore (numeric 0-100; 94-100 observed) per inspection and latest per facility
- **violations**: inspectionResults[].description (item title) + .cannedComments[].description (TFER code citation + rule wording, e.g. '228.75(c)(2) - Thawing...'); writtenComment/observationComment were null in all 52 sampled violations, so the text is standard wording only (like SF/NYC) - Jev gets no observation detail; 2 of 17 sampled inspections had an empty results list (clean or unpublished)
- **severity**: cannedComments[].severity with TFER tiers - VERIFIED values over 52 violations in 17 inspections: Priority 8, Priority Foundation 12, Core 32; inspectionResults[].isCritical was false on all 52 (not usable); result is 'Out of Compliance' (35) or 'Corrected During Inspection' (17)
- **coordinates**: facility latitude/longitude (268 of 689 = 39% null)
- **inspection_date**: inspectionDetails[].inspectionDate ISO with local offset
- **Weekly volume**: ~50 inspections/week: a random sample of 24 facilities (live_mesquite_volume_sample.json) had 90 inspections in the last 365 days = 3.75 per facility per year x 689 facilities / 52
- **Weekly requests**: ~740: 1 facilities POST + 689 inspectionDetails GETs + ~50 inspectionResults GETs (no changed-since filter)
- **History**: rolling ~12 months: across 24 sampled facilities (90 inspections) the oldest inspectionDate was 2025-11-05 and the newest 2026-11-02 - nothing older than a year is exposed (sample-based; whether this is a portal setting is unverified)
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly: (1) POST facilities; require 200 + JSON with 'result' and len(result) >= previous run's count*0.9, else report 'error' and ship nothing; (2) GET inspectionDetails for every facility (paced ~2/s); any new inspection id (not in D1) with inspectionDate >= _lookback_since -> GET inspectionResults; (3) an inspection whose results call fails is dropped and `_hold_freshness(source, date)` is called (never record it as clean); a facility whose inspectionDetails call fails holds the window start (_hold_window_start) and the group reports partial via _set_group_status. Score: hazardScore is a stated 0-100 score (bounded-tier source: never re-score, only read severity); grade from DineScores cutoffs. Build the fetcher around (portal_host, program_id) so Tucson/Pima and Lubbock can be added by config once their /api/v0/portal/siteInfo is confirmed to expose menuType 5.
- **Risks**:
  - ~740 requests per weekly run with no documented rate limit (24 inspectionDetails calls in 17 s at ~1.4 req/s all returned 200; sustained behaviour over 700 calls is unverified).
  - Only ~12 months of inspections are exposed per facility (sample), so an outage longer than a year loses history; ~180 of 689 facilities are schools, daycares, convenience/grocery/dollar stores by name.
  - 39% of facilities lack coordinates -> geocoding dependency; address is one unsplit string.
  - Grades, inspection type and facility type are redacted by config; the list mixes schools, daycares and stores with restaurants and offers no type field to filter on.
  - Undocumented v0 API of a vendor product; a portal upgrade could rename routes (the Angular bundle hash changes with releases - re-read chunk routes if calls start 404ing).
  - No changed-since filter: a run that stops early silently misses facilities unless the partial status is reported.

- **Base URL**: https://foodindex.garlandtx.gov/

- **violations**: none

Report 'error' when the grid is empty for a window the previous run saw rows in. Contact Envhealth@garlandtx.gov about the July-2026 stop.

Nothing newer than 2026-04-17 on 2026-11-03 -> the portal is ~6.5 months behind the city (or inspections stopped being posted); when it was current (Feb-Mar ...

Geocode every address (no coordinates). Low value: no violations, no ids, no numeric score.

  - Letter grade only; no coordinates; no ids.
  - Portal recency: the GET letter crawl of 36/36 letters (398 facilities) found no 'Last Inspection Date' after 2026-04-17 (as of 2026-11-03), so the portal lags the city's inspections by months or stopped updating - a fetcher would show stale grades as current.

### Denton (city)

- **Feasibility**: medium; population ~140k (2020 census 139,869; 2024 est. ~165k); vendor: CKAN open-data portal (data.cityofdenton.com) with DataStore; resource is an FME export 'fme_latestrestauranthealthscores.xlsx' refreshed daily (~11:50 UTC)
- **Base URL**: https://data.cityofdenton.com/api/3/action/
- **Date filter**: VERIFIED server-side via SQL on the text column HealthScoreDate: 'YYYYMMDD' (2,881 rows, through 2024-12-09) and 'YYYYMMDDHHMMSS' (373,539 rows) formats coexist, 5 nulls; string comparison >= 'YYYYMMDD' works for both. WHERE >= '20261027' -> 33 rows / 9 permits; >= '20260805' -> 9,041 rows / 213 permits.
- **score**: HealthScoreInt (text, 0-100)
- **violations**: none
- **severity**: none
- **coordinates**: Lat / Long text; 137,811 of 376,425 rows (37%) null (e.g. Walmart, Buc-ee's)
- **inspection_date**: HealthScoreDate (text, mixed 8/14 digits)
- **Weekly volume**: ~15-20 inspections/week (213 distinct permits in 90 days; 9 in the last 7 days as of 2026-11-02; 3,135 distinct permit+date pairs over 666 permits since 2010)
- **Weekly requests**: 2 (COUNT check + SELECT DISTINCT for the window)
- **History**: 2010-05-25 .. 2026-10-28; up to 9 inspection dates per permit
- **Recommended strategy**: Weekly: (1) GET datastore_search_sql SELECT COUNT(*) ... WHERE "HealthScoreDate" >= 'YYYYMMDD' (window = _lookback_since - 1 day); (2) SELECT DISTINCT "PERMIT_NO","Name","Address","Lat","Long","RESULT","HealthScoreInt","HealthScoreLetter","HealthScoreDate" ... ORDER BY "HealthScoreDate" LIMIT 5000; treat any non-JSON/403 body as 'blocked' (Cloudflare WAF) and ship nothing; keep SQL to plain comparisons (no SUBSTR/functions); verify package_show metadata_modified advanced in the last 8 days, otherwise flag STALE with _note_source_lag; dedupe on (PERMIT_NO, date); score = HealthScoreInt (stated score; grade recomputed by DineScores); coalesce coordinates, geocode the 37% without. No violations -> nothing for Jev.
- **Risks**:
  - Cloudflare WAF 403 on some SQL shapes (SUBSTR) and on HEAD; keep queries simple and treat HTML bodies as blocked.
  - Derived 'latest scores' export duplicates every row ~100x (376k rows, 3,135 distinct); counts must be on DISTINCT.
  - 37% of rows lack coordinates; grade letters use Denton's cutoffs (94/87/80), not DineScores' (90/80/70) - never copy the letter.
  - Mixed date formats in a text column; a format change would silently break the filter - validate every date.

### Carrollton (Dallas/Denton County)

- **Feasibility**: blocked; population ~133k (2020 census 133,434); vendor: City Environmental Quality division publishes a 'Restaurant Scores' section on its own CMS page (per web-search snippet: risk-based frequency, heavy-food 4x/yr, light-food 2x/yr, no-prep 1x/yr); the CMS (Akamai) returns 403 to this container, to curl and to the WebFetch service alike, so the scores format (inline table / PDF 'showpublisheddocument' / app) is unverified
- **Base URL**: unverified
- **Date filter**: unverified
- **score**: unverified
- **violations**: unverified
- **severity**: unverified
- **coordinates**: unverified
- **inspection_date**: unverified
- **Weekly volume**: unverified
- **Weekly requests**: unverified
- **History**: unverified
- **Recommended strategy**: Do not build from the CMS. The only known publication is the 'Restaurant Scores' section/document on cityofcarrollton.com, which Akamai serves only to real browsers (403 to Python, curl and WebFetch on 2026-11-03). Options, all unverified: fetch through the site's Cloudflare relay (functions/api/relay.js) once its host allowlist is extended, or ask Environmental Services (foodpermit@cityofcarrollton.com, 972-466-3060) for an export. Third-party aggregators (ratradar.com lists 318 Carrollton restaurants) are not primary sources.
- **Risks**:
  - Akamai bot protection on the only publication host; GitHub runners will almost certainly be blocked the same way (unverified).
  - Scores format unknown (document vs. app); no vendor portal found under common vendor hostnames; no AGOL/Hub dataset.

### Allen (Collin County)

- **Feasibility**: blocked; population ~105k (2020 census 104,627); vendor: none published: the city's Food Safety page states 'Current health inspection scores are unavailable at this time due to system updates' (captured 2026-11-02); the ArcGIS Experience linked from that page is the city's development-projects map, not scores
- **Base URL**: unverified
- **Date filter**: n/a
- **score**: none published
- **violations**: none published
- **severity**: none published
- **coordinates**: none published
- **inspection_date**: none published
- **Weekly volume**: unverified (city states routine inspections twice a year for all food establishments; establishment count not published)
- **Weekly requests**: n/a
- **History**: none published
- **Recommended strategy**: Do not build. Re-check the Food Safety page quarterly for the 'system updates' notice to disappear and for a new scores link; when it returns, re-run the AGOL org search (orgid:EzT1Zn8fMm7PXUEL) and the city GIS folder walk to find the service.
- **Risks**:
  - Publicly announced outage of the scores system; no machine-readable source exists today.

### Collin County (county program: unincorporated areas and contracting cities; McKinney/Allen/Plano/Frisco run their own)

- **Feasibility**: low; population county ~1.06M (2020) / ~1.26M (2024 est.); the county program only covers unincorporated areas plus Wylie, Melissa, Lowry Crossing, Weston, Josephine and Farmersville (stated on the county page) - roughly 100-130k people (unverified estimate); vendor: Collin County Development Services (Engineering) - Environmental Health; permits and inspections run in Tyler EnerGov with a public Citizen Self-Service portal (collincountytx-energovweb.tylerhost.net/apps/selfservice, 'Search Public Records'); no scores page
- **Base URL**: https://www.collincountytx.gov/services/engineering/development-services/food-service-and-health
- **Date filter**: unverified
- **score**: unverified
- **violations**: unverified
- **severity**: unverified
- **coordinates**: unverified
- **inspection_date**: unverified
- **Weekly volume**: unverified (small program)
- **Weekly requests**: unverified
- **History**: unverified
- **Recommended strategy**: Not worth building now: no published scores; the only machine path would be Tyler EnerGov CSS record search (permit/inspection results without scores or violation text), covering a small unincorporated population. Revisit only if the county starts posting scores. Note the cities that matter in Collin County (McKinney, Allen, Plano, Frisco) run their own programs.
- **Risks**:
  - No scores published; EnerGov CSS exposes permit/inspection status, not inspection results, in comparable deployments (unverified).

### Denton County (county program: unincorporated areas and small cities; Denton, Lewisville, Carrollton, Frisco run their own)

- **Feasibility**: blocked; population county ~906k (2020) / ~1.03M (2024 est.); population served by the county program unverified (unincorporated areas only); vendor: none published: Denton County Public Health's Environmental Health Division page describes only the on-site sewage facility (septic) program; no food-inspection scores or reports are posted
- **Base URL**: https://www.dentoncounty.gov/657/Environmental-Health-Division
- **Date filter**: n/a
- **score**: none published
- **violations**: none published
- **severity**: none published
- **coordinates**: none published
- **inspection_date**: none published
- **Weekly volume**: unverified
- **Weekly requests**: n/a
- **History**: none published
- **Recommended strategy**: Do not build; nothing is published. The Denton County cities that matter (Denton, Lewisville) have their own sources above.
- **Risks**:
  - No public data; web search (dentoncounty.gov only) also found nothing beyond septic permitting and a food-handler Shigella brochure.

### Ranking

- 1. McKinney (~195-220k) - HIGH feasibility: live ArcGIS MapServer with 17,035 inspections back to 2017, per-item points + violation text + ids + coordinates (join), verified server-side date filter, ~30 inspections/week in 2-3 requests. Needs a score calibration (total_score is demerit points).
- 2. Mesquite (~150k) - HIGH feasibility: Hedgerow portal API verified end to end (689 facilities -> inspections -> violations with TFER citations, isCritical, 0-100 hazardScore, ~50 inspections/week, 12-month rolling history); costs ~740 requests/week because nothing is date-filterable; 39% of facilities lack coordinates; the same fetcher serves Lubbock (routes verified live) and probably Tucson/Pima (host unverified).
- 3. Lewisville (~112-135k) - HIGH feasibility, low richness: one GET per week returns every inspection in the window with Score/Lat/Lng/ids (27 last week, 1,146 last year); no violations; 365-day retention; needs the GoDaddy intermediate in the trust store.
- 4. Denton city (~140-165k) - MEDIUM feasibility: CKAN DataStore SQL with daily refresh and history to 2010, scores/results only, 37% rows without coordinates, Cloudflare WAF blocks some SQL shapes, heavy row duplication (use DISTINCT).
- 5. Grand Prairie (~196k) - LOW: letter grade + last-inspection date only, 398 facilities over 40 HTML pages, 503s/timeouts, no coordinates/ids/history.
- 6. Garland (~246k) - LOW/STALE: the only portal (foodindex.garlandtx.gov) has published nothing since 2026-06-30 although it is still up; latest-only scores with coordinates when it works.
- 7. Irving (~257k) - BLOCKED: both ArcGIS datasets are abandoned snapshots (2020-02, 2024-06); the live scores page on irvingtx.gov/cityofirving.org sits behind a Cloudflare JS challenge.
- 8. Carrollton (~133k) - BLOCKED: scores exist only on the Akamai-protected city CMS (403 to Python, curl and WebFetch).
- 9. Allen (~105k) - BLOCKED: city states scores are 'unavailable at this time due to system updates'; no GIS/AGOL dataset.
- 10. Collin County program - LOW: no scores published; covers only the unincorporated county plus Wylie, Melissa, Lowry Crossing, Weston, Josephine, Farmersville; EnerGov CSS API answers 500 anonymously.
- 11. Denton County program - BLOCKED/none: nothing published (septic-only environmental health page).
- BUILD FIRST (population x feasibility): McKinney, Mesquite, Lewisville, Denton city. Together ~600-680k residents and ~110 inspections/week for roughly 750 requests/week, all with ids and server-side or diffable incremental paths. Grand Prairie is the only other buildable source and is worth it only if letter-grade-only coverage is acceptable.
